Thus you too, my brothers, show obedience to your spiritual fathers and you will receive blessings, and through them you will put on the armor of prayer; like Joshua, son of Nun, who obeyed the blessed Moses, entered, and inherited the promised land, that which makes milk and honey flow for those who inherit it.
ܐܟܙܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܝܫܘܥܒܪܢܘܢ ܘܟ̇ܿܠܒ ܒܪ ܝܘܦܢܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܥܼܠܘ ܠܐܪܥܐ ܕܡܘܠܟܼܢܐ ܡܢ ܫܬܡܐܐ ܐܠܦ̈ܝܢ ܓܒܪ̈ܝܢ ܡܛܠ ܡܫܬܡܥܢܘܬܗܘܢ ܒܗܿܝ ܕܢܛܪܘ ܡܠܬܗ ܕܡܘܫܐ ܘܫ̇ܡܠܝܘ ܒܬܪ ܡܪܝܐ ܘܟܠܗ ܥܡܐ ܗܿܘ ܣܦܼܘ ܒܡܕܒܪܐ ܡܛܠ ܠܐ ܡܫܬܡܥܢܘܬܗܘܢ ܕܠܛܘܒܢܐ ܡܘܫܐ. ܗܟܢܐ ܐܦ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܗܿܘܝܢ ܠܐ ܡ̈ܫܬܡܥܢܐ ܇ ܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܢ ܠܡܘܬܼܒܐ ܫܠܝܼܐ ܕܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܘܢܝ̇ܚܐ ܕܒܗܿ. ܗܿܘ ܕܨܵܐܪ ܛܘܦܣܐ ܕܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܡܘܠܟܢܐ ܘܕܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܢܘܬܐ ܇ ܗܿܝ ܕܒܗܿ ܩܿܛܠ ܗܘܢܐ ܫܒ̈ܥܐ ܚܫ̈ܐ ܇ ܗܢܘܢ ܕܠܒܼܝܟܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܐܪܥܐ ܕܡܘܠܟܢܐ ܀ ܡܢ ܩܕܡ ܕܥܼܒܪ ܗܘܐ ܐܝܣܪܐܝܠ
For just as Joshua, son of Nun, and Caleb, son of Jephunneh, were the only ones, among the six hundred thousand men, to enter the promised land, because of their obedience, from the fact that they had observed the word of Moses and followed the Lord to the end, all the people having perished in the desert because of their disobedience to the blessed Moses; likewise also all those who are disobedient become strangers as regards the silent stay in the cell and the rest that one finds there, this (rest) which represents the figure of the promised land and of the psychic stage, during which the intellect exterminates the seven passions, those which occupied the promised land before Israel crossed the Jordan.
ܝܘܪ̈ܕܢܢ ܀ ܐܠܐ ܐܦ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܐܘ ܐܚ̈ܝ ܚܘܘ ܡܫܬܡܥܢܘܬܐ ܘܝܨܝܦܘܬܐ ܒܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܥܡܼܝܠܐ ܕܒܩܢܘܒܝܢ. ܕܡܐ ܕܡܬܛܲܦܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܒܡܕܒܪܐ ܘܡܚ̈ܘܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܡܫܬܡܥܢܘܬܐ ܫܪܝܪܬܐ. ܘܦܣܿܩܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܟܠ ܨܒܝ̈ܢܝܟܘܢ ܡܿܝܬܝܢ ܡܢ ܠܒܟܘܢ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܚܘܫ̈ܒܐ ܒܝ̈ܫܐ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܨܿܒܝܢ
57 But you too, my brothers, show obedience and diligence in the painful exercise of the monastery, for when you have been hidden in the desert, when you have shown true obedience and have tamed all your wills, from your heart will disappear all the evil thoughts, those which wanted to make you return to the perverse Egypt.
ܕܢܗܿܦܟܘܢܟܘܢ ܠܡܨܪ̈ܝܢ ܕܒܝܼܫܘܬܐ ܀ ܐܢ ܕܝܢ ܡܚܿܘܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܡܫܬܡܥܢܘܬܐ ܫܪܝܪܐ ܒܦܘܠܚܢܐ
58 If indeed you show true obedience in the exercise of the monastery, you too will cross the Jordan, you will inherit the land
ܕܒܩܢܘܒܝܢ ܥܿܒܪܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܐܦ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܝܘܪ̈ܕܢܢ ܘܝ̇ܪܬܝܬܘܢ ܐܪܥܐ ܕܡܘܠܟܢܐ. ܕܐܝܬܝܗ̇ܿ ܕܟܝܘܬܐ. ܘܐܢ ܕܝܢ ܠܐ ܡܚܿܘܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܡܫܬܡܥܢܘܬܐ ܦܫܝܼܛܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܥܘܩܒ ܡܿܝܬܝܢ ܐܝܬܘܢ ܡܢ ܝܕܥܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ. ܐܝܟ ܥܡܐ ܗܽܘ ܡܡܪܡܪܢܐ. ܕܙ̈ܓܝܓ ܗܘܐ ܫܟܝܖ̈ܬܐ ܕܒܡܨܪܝܢ ܇ ܘܠܐ ܐܫܬܡܼܥ ܠܦܘܩܕܢܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܇ ܘܡܝܼܬ ܟܠܗ ܒܡܕܒܪܐ ܘܠܐ ܥܠܼܘ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܡܘܠܟܢܐ ܣܛܪ ܡܢ ܝܫܘܥܒܪܢܘܢ ܘܟ̇ܠܒ ܒܪ
promised which is (the state of) purity. On the other hand, if you do not show a simple obedience, without claim, you will die, far from the knowledge of God; like that complaining people who desired the shames of Egypt and did not obey the commandment of God: it died entirely in the desert, and did not enter into the promised land, with the exception of Joshua, son of Nun, and Caleb, son of Jephunneh, who made the people of the Lord inherit the promised land.


[p. 330] {#po.45.p330}
ܝܘܦܢܐ ܆ ܕܗܼܢܘܢ ܐܘܪ̈ܬܘܗܝ ܠܥܡܗ ܕܡܪܝܐ ܐܪܥܐ ܕܡܘܠܟܢܐ ܀ ܗܟܢܐ ܐܦ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܐܘ̇ ܐܚ̈ܝ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܒܩܢܘܒܝܢ ܦ̇ܠܚܝܢ ܐܡܝܼܬܘ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܟܠܗܘܢ ܚܘܫ̈ܒܐ ܕܡܢ ܡܨܪ̈ܝܢ ܥܠܼܘ ܥܡܟܘܢ ܠܡܕܒܪܐ ܕܦܘܠܚܢܝܐ ܕܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܦܓܪܢܘܬܐ. ܕܡܐ ܕܡܝܬܝܢ ܚܘܫ̈ܒܐ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܡܢ ܥܠܡܐ ܐܬܘܼ ܥܡܟܘܢ ܘܣܿܝܦܝܢ ܘܡܬ̇ܿܛܼܠܩܝܢ ܡܢ ܢܦܫ̈ܬܟܘܢ ܘܡܬܝܿܠܕܝܢ ܚܠܦܝܗܘܢ ܚܘܫ̈ܒܐ ܬܩ̈ܢܐ ܕܡܢ ܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܟܝ̈ܢܝܐ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܕܕܟܝܘܬܐ ܗ̇ܝܕܝܢ ܡܫܲܢܐ ܡܘܫܐ ܗܘܢܐ ܡܢ ܥܠܡܐ ܗܢܐ ܠܘܬ ܡܪܝܐ ܗ̇ܿܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܡܢ ܚܙܬܐ ܡܬܪ̈ܓܫܢܝܬܐ ܇ ܘܡܢ ܗܺܝ ܕܡܬܪ̈ܓܫܢܐܝܬ
59 Thus you too, my brothers, you who exercise yourselves at the monastery, put to death all the thoughts which, from Egypt, have entered with you into the desert of asceticism at the somatic stage, for when these thoughts which have come from the world with you are dead, destroyed, and disappeared from your souls, and there are born in their place valid thoughts, issued from natural seeds, which are the fruits of (the state of) purity, then Moses will make the intellect go away to the Lord, out of this world, that is to say, out of sensible vision and out of that which makes one understand spiritual things through the senses.
ܡܣܬܟܿܠ ܨܒ̈ܘܬܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܬܐ ܀ ܡܛܠ ܕܙܒܢܐ ܗ̇ܘ ܡܟܝܠ ܕܢܥܼܒܪ ܥܡܗ ܕܡܪܝܐ ܝܘܪ̈ܕܢܢ ܘܢܲܩܪܒ ܥܡ ܥܡܡ̈ܐ ܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܒܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܡܘܠܟܢܐ ܥ̇ܡܪܝܢ. ܗ̇ܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܕܫܠܼܡ ܙܒܢܐ ܕܦܼܘܠܚܢܐ ܕܒܩܢܘܒܝܢ ܇ ܕܢܦܘܩ ܘܢܬܒܼ ܝܚܝܕܝܐܝܬ ܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܗܺܝ ܕܨܝܪܐ ܛܘܦܣܐ ܕܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܡܘܠܟܢܐ ܘܢܲܩܪܒ ܥܡ ܫܒ̈ܥܐ ܥܡ̈ܡܝܢ ܕܥܿܡܪܝܢ ܒܗܿ. ܐܝܟ ܕܢܬܡܼܲ̈ܛܐ ܠܫܦܝܘܬܐ ܕܗܼܝ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ܿ ܫܘܡܠܝܐ ܕܡܫܘܚܬܐ
For it is time already that the people of the Lord cross the Jordan, and fight the foreign peoples who inhabit the promised land; that is to say, that they put an end to the time of exercises at the monastery, to go out to live solitarily in a cell - which represents the figure of the promised land, - and that they fight against the seven peoples who inhabit it, in order to arrive at (the state) of limpidity which is the accomplishment of the psychic stage.
ܕܢܦܫܢܘܬܐ ܀ ܒܪܡ ܕܝܢ ܚܫ̈ܐ ܕܡܩ̇ܪܒܝܢ ܥܡ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܒܩܢܘܒܝܢ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܚܪܝܝܘܬܐ. ܡܩܼܡܘܬ ܨܒܝܢܐ. ܠܐ ܡܫܬܡܥܢܘܬܐ. ܚܒܢܢܘܬܐ. ܣܪܝܩܘܬ ܡܡܠܠܐ. ܠܐ ܟܘ̇ܚܕܐ. ܕܓܠܘܬܐ. ܬܪ̈ܝܢܘܬ ܡܠ̈ܐ. ܠܐ ܡܛܟܣܘܬܐ ܕܪܕ̈ܓܫܐ. ܝܨܝܦܘܬܐ ܝܬܝܪܬܐ ܕܥܠ ܚܫܚܬܗ ܕܦܓܪܐ. ܡܒܣܪܢܘܬܐ. ܥܝܢܐ ܚܣܘܡܬܢܝܬܐ. ܣܓܝܐܘܬ ܫܼܢܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܒܛܟܣܐ. ܘܕܒܝܼܫ ܡܢ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܚܫ̈ܐ ܕܬܢܿܝܬ ܆ ܦܘܪ̈ܓܝܐ ܘܓܘܚܟܐ ܠܐ ܒܛܟܣܐ. ܘܪ̈ܒܘܬ ܟܪܣܐ ܘܚܫܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ ܇ ܕܡܚܲܒܠ ܠܙܗܝܘܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܗܘܢ ܕܐܝܠܝܢ
60 In truth, the passions, which fight against those who are at the monastery are the following: complaining, stubbornness of will, disobedience, laziness, vain speech, impudence, lying, duplicity of words, disorder of the senses, excessive concern for the needs of the body, contempt, the jealous eye, the disorderly excess of sleep, and, which is worse than all the passions I have mentioned, voluptuousness, disorderly laughter, gluttony, and the passion of fornication which corrupts the splendor of the soul of those who are enslaved to it.
ܕܡܫܬܡܥܝܢ ܠܗ ܀ ܗܠܝܢ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܚܫ̈ܐ ܐܘ̇ ܐܚܘܢ ܚܒܼܝܒܐ ܇ ܥܡ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܡܬܛܲܪܦܝܢ ܒܡܕܒܪܐ ܕܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܕܒܩܢܘܒܝܢ ܇ ܘܡܬܝ̇ܐܝܒܝܢ ܠܡܩܼܛܦ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܕܕܟܝܘܬܐ ܇ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܫܘܡܠܝܐ ܕܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܦܓܪܢܘܬܐ ܡܲܩܪܒܝܢ. ܕܡܐ ܕܙܿܟܝܢ ܠܗܘܢ ܒܚܝܠ ܥܘܕܪ̈ܢܗ ܕܡܪܢ ܡܩܲܒܠܝܢ ܒܘܪ̈ܟܬܐ ܘܨܠ̈ܘܬܐ ܡܢ ܐܒܗ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܪ̈ܘܚܝܐ ܀
All these passions, O our beloved brother, make war with those who in the desert tire themselves in the ascetic exercises of the monastery, and who desire to gather the fruits of (the state of) purity, which is the accomplishment of the somatic stage; because, when (these) have conquered them with the strength of the help of Our Lord, they will receive blessings and prayers from their spiritual fathers.
ܐܝܟ ܝܫܘܥܒܪܢܘܢ ܕܩܲܒܠ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܒ݁ܣܿܝܡ ܐܝܼܕܗ ܕܛܘܒܢܐ ܡܘܫܐ ܇ ܘܥܒܼܪ ܝܘܪ̈ܕܢܢ ܘܥܼܠ ܘܝܼܪܬܘ ܐܪܥܐ ܕܡܼܘܠܟܢܐ. ܗܟܢܐ. ܐܦ ܬܠ̈ܡܝܕܐ ܫܪܝܪ̈ܐ ܇ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܦ̇ܠܚܝܢ ܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܕܕܚܠܬܿ ܐܠܗܐ ܒܩܢܘܒܝܢ
61 Just as Joshua, son of Nun, received the Spirit by the laying on of hands of the blessed Moses, crossed the Jordan, entered, and they inherited the promised land, likewise also the true disciples who devote themselves to the exercise of the fear of God at the monastery, will have blessings and prayers


[p. 332] {#po.45.p332}
ܡܩܲܒܠܝܢ ܒܘܪ̈ܟܬܐ ܘܨ̈ܠܘܬܐ ܡܢ ܐܒܗ̈ܝܗܘܢ. ܘܥܿ̇ܒܪܝܢ ܝܘܪ̈ܕܢܢ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܬܚܘܡܐ ܕܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܢܘܬܐ. ܘܝ̇ܪܬܝܢ ܐܪܥܐ ܕܡܘܠܟܢܐ ܘܡܣܬܝܒܪܝܢ ܡܢ ܛܒ̈ܬܗܿ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܘܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܕܝܠܗ̇ܿ ܕܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܢܘܬܐ. ܘܡܫܬܘܝܢ ܠܡܘܬܼܒܐ ܥܡܼܝܠܐ ܘܫܲܠܝܐ ܕܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܇ ܕܨܿܐܪ̈ ܛܘܦܣܗ̇ ܕܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܡܘܠܟܢܐ ܇ ܕܒܗܿ ܡܬܝܿܗܒܝܢ ܠܗܘܢܐ ܦܿܠܘܚܐ ܇ ܣܘܟܿܠ ܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܘܝܲܕܥ̈ܬܐ ܕܟܠܗܘܢ ܥ̈ܠܡܐ ܕܗܼܘܘ ܘܕܗ̇ܘܝܢ. ܘܒܗܿ ܡܩܲܒܠ ܐܪ̈ܙܢܐ ܘܓ̈ܠܝܢܐ ܕܥܠ ܗܢܐ ܟܠ ܀
from their fathers and will cross the Jordan, which is the border of the psychic stage; they will inherit the promised land and will feed on its goods, which are the intellections and the mysteries of the psychic stage itself; they will be judged worthy of the laborious stay and of the silence in the cell, which represents the figure of the promised land, where there will be given to the intellect in asceticism the intellection of the mysteries and the knowledge of all the worlds that have been and that will be: it will receive there the mysteries and the revelations about all that. III. Departure to the cell: Psychic stage. First summit.
?& ܗܫܐ ܕܝܢ ܐܬܡܲܛܝܢܢ ܐܘ̇ ܚܒܝܒ ܢܦܫܝ ܠܡܿܥܒܪܬܐ ܕܒܝܘܪܕܢܢ ܇ ܗܿܺܝ ܕܗܼܝ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܡܿܦܩܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܩܢܘܒܝܢ ܘܡܘܬܒܼܐ ܕܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܒܛܟܼܣܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܡܘܫ̈ܚܬܐ. ܡܥܒܪܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܦܓܪܢܘܬܐ ܠܕܢܦܫܢܘܬܐ ܐܦܢ ܚܢܢ ܐܘ̇ ܡܪܝ ܡܢ ܫܘܪܝܐ ܡܬܚܼܢܗܿ ܠܡܠܬܢ ܥܠ ܡܦܩܬܗ ܕܒܪܢܫܐ ܕܡܢ ܥܠܡܐ ܇ ܘܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܡܝܬܪ̈ܐ ܕܒܩܢܘܒܝܢ ܘܦܪܫܢܢ ܒܝܬ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܩ̇ܝܡܝܢ ܒܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܦܓܪܢܘܬܐ ܠܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܥܕܟܝܠ ܠܐ ܫ̇ܪܝܘ ܒܗ̇ ܡܛܠ ܥܠܼܬܐ ܕܐܢܫ̈ܝܢ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܐܡܿܪܝܢ ܒܠܐ ܝܕܥܬܗܘܢ ܕܐܢܫ̈ܐ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܒܢ̈ܝ ܥܠܡܐ
62 And now, O darling of my soul, here we are arrived at the crossing of the Jordan which is the exit from the monastery and the stay in the cell; as for the order of the stages, it is the passage from the somatic stage to the psychic (stage), although, 6 My Lord, at the beginning, we developed our discourse concerning the exit of man out of the world and the exercise of virtue at the monastery, and distinguished those who are at the somatic stage from those who, until then, have not approached it, because of the people who say in their ignorance that all the people of the world are at the somatic stage.
ܒܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܦܓܪܢܘܬܐ ܩ̇ܝܡܝܢ ܀ ܐܠܐ ܐܢܬ ܐܘ̇ ܚܒܝܒ ܟܠܗ̇ ܒܥܼܬܟ ܕܡܢܢ ܗܕܐ ܗܘܬ ܇ ܥܠ ܡܘܬܼܒܐ ܫܲܠܝܐ ܕܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܘܥܠ ܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܕܕܘܒܪ̈ܐ ܥܡܝ̈ܠܐ ܕܒܗ̇ ܘܕܐܝܠܝܢ ܡܝܬܪ̈ܬܐ ܙܪܩ ܠܗܘܢ ܠܡܦܼܠܚ ܠܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܝ̇ܬܒܝܢ ܒܫܠܝܼܐ ܕܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܇ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܐܡܝܢܐܝܬ ܘܠܐ ܡܗܡܝܢܐܝܬ ܢܓܡܪܘܢ ܐܢܝ̈ܢ ܘܕܐܝܠܝܢ ܣܥܘܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܗܘ̈ܝܢ ܠܘܬܗܘܢ ܘܕܐܝܠܝܢ ܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܕܝܕܥܬܐ ܡܬܓܼܠܝܢ ܠܗܘܢ ܘܕܐܝܠܝܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܚܫ̈ܐ ܕܡܲܩܪܒܝܢ ܥܡܗܘܢ ܒܚܘܡܣܢܐ ܕܒܫܼܠܝܐ ܕܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܘܕܐܝܠܝܢ ܩܪ̈ܒܐ ܥܣ̈ܩܐ ܕܡܢ ܫ̈ܐܕܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܢ ܥܡܗܘܢ ܘܕܡܢ
But you, O dearest one, all that you have asked of us is what concerns the silent stay in the cell, the exercise of the laborious disciplines that one (carries out) there, what virtues those who stay in the silence of the cell must exercise, what they must accomplish continually and without negligence, what visitations of grace they are the object of, and what mysteries of knowledge are revealed to them; what are the passions that make war on them constantly in the silence of the cell, what violent attacks come to them from the demons, and, after the victory that frees them from the passions and the combats, at what stage they will arrive.
ܒܬܪ̈ ܙܟܘܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܚ̈ܫܐ ܘܩܪ̈ܒܐ ܠܐܝܿܕܐ ܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܡܬܡܲܛܝܢ ܀ ܘܐܡܼܪܬ ܠܝ ܕܦܲܪܫ ܠܝ ܒܝܬ ܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܠܡܫܘܚܬܐ. ܘܣܝܼܡ ܒܗ ܝܕܝ̈ܥܐ ܇ ܘܐܝܿܬܐ ܠܗ̇ ܬܚ̈ܘܝܬܐ ܡܢ ܟܬܒ̈ܐ ܩܕܝ̈ܫܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܟܠܗܝܢ ܕܒܥܼܝܬ ܡܢܝ ܐܘ̇ ܚܒܝܒ ܪ̈ܚܝܡܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܚܙܝܬܗ̇ܿ ܠܢܦܫܟ ܕܒܬܪ̈ ܫܟܚ̈ܬܐ ܕܚ̈ܕܬܬܐ ܕܪܚܡܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܢܦܝܼܩܐ ܗܐ ܡܫܲܪܝܢܢ ܠܡܫܡܠܝܘ ܒܥܼܬܗ ܕܚܘܒܝ ܥܘܕܪ̈ܢܗ ܕܡܪܢ ܘܙܒܢܐ ܗܘ ܡܟܝܠ ܕܢܥܼܒܪ ܝܘܪܿܕܢܢ ܘܢܥܼܘܠ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ
63 You had said to me, however: Distinguish for me stage and stage: mark milestones there and bring proofs for them from the holy Scriptures; all that, you asked of me, O dear friend. As I saw that your soul had gone out to the discovery of the new things of the love of God, here then is that we are going to begin to put into execution the request of your Charity, with the help of Our Lord. It is time now for us to cross the Jordan, to enter into the promised land, to fight with the seven nations.
ܕܡܘܠܟܢܐ ܘܢܬܟ̇ܬܫ ܥܡ ܫܒܥܐ ܥܡ̈ܡܐ ܕܒܗܿ. ܘܢܐܣܘܪ̈ ܡܠܟܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܒܥܪ̈ܩܐ ܕܦܪܙܠܐ ܘܢܐܪ̈ܬ ܟܪ̈ܟܝܗܘܢ ܘܡܕܝ̈ܢܬܗܘܢ ܘܢܣܬܝܿܒܪ ܡܢ ܛܒ̈ܬܗܿ ܕܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܗܕܐ. ܗܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܚܠܒܐ ܘܕܒܫܐ. ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܚܙܬܐ ܕܬܐܘܪ̈ܝܣ ܘܝܕܥܬܐ ܕܝܠܗܝܢ ܀
that are found therein, and to chain their kings with iron chains; to inherit their strongholds and their cities; and to feed ourselves on the goods of this land, namely milk and honey, that is to say, their contemplative vision and their knowledge.
ܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܓܝܪ ܙܒ̈ܢܝܢ ܥܒܼܪܘ ܒܢ̈ܝ ܐܝܣܪܐܝܠ ܝܘܪ̈ܕܢܢ ܇ ܘܗ̇ܿܝܕܝܢ ܥܠܼܘ ܠܐܪܥܐ ܕܡܘܠܟܢܐ. ܡܥܒܪܬܐ ܓܝܪ ܩܕܡܝܬܐ ܥܒܼܪܘ ܒܝܘܪ̈ܕܢܢ ܐܝܟ ܟܠܢܫ ܒܝܘܪ̈ܕܢܢ. ܟܕ ܠܐ ܐܬܚܙܝܼܬ̇ ܐܬܼܐ ܕܬܕܡܘܪ̈ܬܐ ܩܕܡܝܗܘܢ. ܡܛܠ ܕܛܘܒܢܐ ܡܘܫܐ ܡܕܒܪ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܠܥܡܐ ܗܲܝܕܝܟ. ܒܗܕܐ ܡܥܒܪܬܐ
64 It was on two occasions that the children of Israel crossed the Jordan, and it was only afterwards that they entered the promised land. In the first crossing, indeed, they crossed the Jordan like everyone else, without there having appeared


[p. 334] {#po.45.p334}
ܡܬܟ̇ܬܫ ܗܘܐ ܥܡܠܝܩ ܥܡ ܐܝܣܪܐܝܠ ܘܫܒܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܡܗܦܼܟܘܬܗ ܠܡܕܒܪܐ ܇ ܕܠܐ ܢܥܼܘܠ ܘܢܐܪ̈ܬ ܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܥܡ̈ܠܩܝܐ. ܗܕܐ ܕܝܢ ܡܥܒܪܬܐ ܩܕܡܝܬܐ ܇ ܛܘܦܣܐ ܨ̇ܝܪܐ ܕܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܦܓܪܢܘܬܐ. ܕܠܐ ܡܬܚܿܙܝܐ ܒܗܿ ܐܬܿܐ ܕܬܕܡܘܖ̈ܬܐ ܇ ܘܠܐ ܦܪܝܫܐ ܝܕܥܬܗ ܕܦ̇ܠܘܚܐ ܡܢ ܕܟܠܢܫ ܐܦܢ ܕܘܒܪ̈ܘܗܝ ܦܪܝܫܝܢ ܇ ܐܝܟ ܥܡܐ ܐܝܣܪܠܝܐ ܕܦܪܝܼܫܝܢ ܕܘܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܢܡ̈ܘܣܘܗܝ ܡܢ ܕܟܠܗܘܢ ܥܡ̈ܡܐ. ܒܪܡ ܕܝܢ ܝܕܥܬܗ ܫܲܘܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܥܡ ܕܟܠܢܫ. ܗܟܢܐ ܐܦ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܒܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܦܓܪܢܘܬܐ ܇ ܐܦܢ ܕܘܒܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܦܪܝܫܝܢ ܡܢ ܕܟܠܢܫ ܐܠܐ ܝܕܥܬܗܘܢ ܦܓܪܢܝܬܐ ܗܝ ܐܝܟ ܕܟܠܢܫ ܀ ܗܕܐ ܕܝܢ ܡܥܒܪܬܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܕܒܢ̈ܝܐܝܣܪܐܝܠ ܕܒܝܘܪ̈ܕܢܢ ܕܐܬܚܙܝܬ ܒܗܿ ܬܕܡܘܪ̈ܬܐ ܬܡܝܼܗܬܐ ܇ ܕܒܗ̇ ܥܠܼܘ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܡܘܠܟܢܐ ܛܘܦܣܐ ܨܲܝܪܐ ܕܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܢܘܬܐ. ܗܕܐ ܕܒܗ̇ ܡܬܦ̇ܪܫ ܝܕܥܬܗ ܕܗܘܢܝܐ ܡܢ ܕܟܠܢܫ. ܢܫܼܒܘܩ ܗܟܝܠ ܐܘ̇ ܚܒܝܒ ܢܦܫܝ ܡܠܬܐ ܕܥܠ ܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܢܘܬܐ ܘܢܐܡܪ ܗܫܐ ܥܠ ܡܘܬܒܐ ܕܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܫܘܘܕܝܢ ܕܠܘܬ ܚܘܒܟ ܘܗܿܝܕܝܢ ܢܠܒܼܘܟ ܣܕܪ̈ܐ ܕܡܘ̈ܫܚܬܐ ܘܢܐܡܿܪ ܥܠܝܗܝܢ. ܐܝܟ ܕܬܿܒܥܐ ܘܠ̈ܝܬܐ ܀ ܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܫܿܡܼܠܝ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܦ̇ܠܘܚܐ ܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܥܡܼܝܠܐ ܕܒܩܢܘܒܝܢ ܒܡܟܼܝܟܘܬܐ ܘܒܡܫܬܡܥܢܘܬܐ ܘܒܡܣܝܒܪܢܘܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܟܠ ܥܕܠܝ̈ܝܢ ܕܡܢ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܡܫ̇ܬܡܪܝܢ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܘܫܲܡܠܝ ܩܢܘܢܐ ܕܓܼܘܐ ܐܝܟ ܙܕܩܼܐ ܗܿܝܕܝܢ ܡܬܝܼܗܒ ܠܗ ܡܢ ܐܒܗ̈ܘܗܝ ܕܪ̈ܘܚ ܐܝܟ ܕܢܦܼܘܩ ܠܩܠܝܬܐ ܇ ܕܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܡܘܬܼܒܐ ܥܡܼܝܠܐ ܕܒܫܼܠܝܐ. ܘܦ̇ܪܫ ܝܘܡܐ ܚܕ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܚܓܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܠܟܠܗܘܢ ܐܒܗ̈ܘܗܝ ܘܐܚ̈ܘܗܝ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ. ܐܝܟ ܕܢܠܒܫܘܢܝܗܝ ܙܝܢܐ ܪܘܚܢܐ ܕܨܠܘܬܗܘܢ ܘܢܬܟ̇ܬܫ ܥܡ ܥܡ̈ܡܐ
before them any prodigious sign, because at that time it was the blessed Moses who was leading the people. During this crossing, Amalek fought against Israel and wanted to send it back to the desert, so that it would not enter to inherit the land of the Amalekites: this is the first crossing, a figure representing the somatic stage, during which no prodigious sign appears, and where the knowledge of the one practicing is not different from that of everyone else, even if his conduct is different, like the people of Israel among whom the conduct (required by) its laws is different from those of all peoples; but nevertheless, its knowledge was similar to that of everyone else. Thus, all those who are at the somatic stage, even if their conduct is different from that of all others, their knowledge is nevertheless bodily, like that of everyone. As for the second crossing of the children of Israel at the Jordan, during which a striking wonder appeared to them, and thanks to which they penetrated into the promised land, it represents the figure of the psychic stage, that in which the knowledge of the intellect is differentiated from that of all others. Let us therefore leave aside, O beloved of my soul, (our) discourse on the psychic stage, and let us speak now of the stay in the cell, as we promised your Charity; we will then resume the order of the stages, and we will speak of them according to the suitability of your request. Feast of the consecration of the cell 65 When the practicing monk has completed the arduous exercise at the monastery, with humility, obedience, and patience in the face of all the reproaches that come to him from others, and he has fulfilled the rule of the community as is fitting, then permission is given to him by his spiritual fathers to depart to a cell, which is a laborious stay in silence. || designates a day, as for a spiritual feast, to all his spiritual fathers and brothers, so that they may clothe him with the spiritual armor of their prayers, so that he may fight the foreign peoples who inhabit the promised land.
ܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܥ̇ܡܪܝܢ ܒܐܪܥܐ ܕܡܘܠܟܢܐ ܀ ܐܟܙܢܐ ܕܝܫܘܥܒܪܢܘܢ ܡܛܠ ܕܐܫܬܡܼܥ ܠܗ ܠܛܘܒܢܐ ܡܘܫܐ ܣܿܡ ܐܝܼܕܗ ܥܠ ܪܝܫܗ ܘܒܲܪܟܗ ܐܝܟ ܕܢܥܼܘܠ ܘܢܐܪܬ ܐܪܥܐ ܕܡܘܠܟܢܐ ܗܟܢܐ ܐܦ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܦ̇ܠܚܝܢ ܒܩܢܘܒܝܢ ܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܕܡܟܝܟܘܬܐ ܘܕܡܫܬܡܥܢܘܬܐ ܇ ܡܵܓܸܢ ܥܠܝܗܘܢ ܚܲܝܠܐ ܐܠܗܝܐ ܒܝܕ ܒܥܘܬܐ ܘܨܠܘܬܐ ܕܗ̈ܘܝܐ ܥܠܝܗܘܢ ܡܢ ܟܠܗ ܟܢܘܫܝܐ ܩܕܝܫܐ
Just as, because Joshua, son of Nun, had submitted to the blessed Moses, the latter laid his hands upon his head and blessed him so that he might enter to inherit the promised land, so likewise for all those who have exercised themselves in the monastery in the practice of humility and obedience, the divine strength rests upon them, thanks to the supplication and prayer made for


[p. 336] {#po.45.p336}
ܕܐܒ̈ܗܝܗܘܢ ܘܕܐܚ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܐ ܒܝܘܡܐ ܕܥܿܒܕܝܢ ܚܓܐ ܕܥܐܕܐ ܕܩܘ̇ܕܫܐ ܕܩܠܝܬܐ ܀ ܘܐܟܙܢܐ ܕܝܫܘܥܒܪܢܘܢ ܟܕ ܐܥܼܒܪܗ ܠܥܡܐ ܝܘܪܕܢܢ ܐܡܼܪ ܠܗ ܐܠܗܐ ܕܢܥܼܒܕ ܚܓܐ ܕܝܘܡܐ ܕܓܙܘܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܒܝܕ ܓܙܘܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܒܣܪܐ ܢܓܙܘܪ̈ ܡܢ ܠܒ̈ܘܬܗܘܢ ܟܠܗ ܥܘܗܕܢܐ ܕܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܬܥܝܼܫܐ ܕܒܡܨܪܝܢ ܘܢܿܠܒܫ ܐܢܘܢ ܚܲܝܠܐ ܐܠܗܝܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܢܬܟܿܬܫܘܢ ܥܡ ܥܡ̈ܡܐ ܕܥܿܡܪܝܢ ܒܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܡܘܠܟܢܐ ܗܟܢܐ ܐܦ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܢܿܦܼܩܝܢ ܠܡܬܼܒ ܚܼܕܬܐܝܬ ܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܫܘܡܠܝܐ ܕܒܩܢܘܒܝܢ ܒܝܕ ܗܿܘ ܕܒ̣ܚܐ ܚܲܝܐ ܕܦܓܪܗ ܘܕܡܗ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ ܕܡܬܩܲܪܒ ܚܠܦܝܗܘܢ ܠܒ̇ܫܝܢ
them by the whole holy assembly of their spiritual fathers and brothers, on the day they celebrate the solemnity of the feast of the consecration of the cell. 66 Just as when Joshua, son of Nun, made the people cross the Jordan, God told him to celebrate the feast of the day of circumcision, so that, by the circumcision of the flesh, he might circumcise from their hearts every memory of the arduous exercises (endured) in Egypt, and so that the divine strength might clothe them, so that they might wage battle against the peoples who inhabited the promised land, so likewise all those who depart to settle newly in a cell, after the completion of the (stay) at the monastery, are clothed with the divine strength, by means of this living sacrifice of the body and blood of Christ which is offered for them, and go off to fight against the passions of sin.
ܚܲܝܠܐ ܐܠܗܝܐ ܘܡܬܿܟ̇ܬܫܝܢ ܥܡ ܚ̈ܫܐ ܕܚܛܝܼܬܐ ܀ ܠܐ ܐܢܫ ܢܣܿܒܪ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܚܒ̈ܢܢܐ ܘܡܩ̈ܛܥܐ ܇ ܕܡܛܠ ܫܗܖܪ̈ܐ ܕܩܘ̇ܕܫܐ ܕܥܿܒܕ ܐܚܐ ܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܇ ܡܫܼܟܚ ܢܝܚܐ ܘܝ̇ܬܒ ܒܗ̇ ܟܕ ܒܩܢܘܒܝܢ ܒܫܘܡܠܝܐ ܠܐ ܦܠܼܚ ܇ ܒܡܟܝܟܘܬܐ ܘܒܡܫܬܡܥܢܘܬܐ ܕܠܐܒܗ̈ܘܝ ܘܠܐܚ̈ܘܗܝ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ. ܚܿܣ ܠܗ ܓܝܪ ܠܗ̇ܘ ܕܒܩܢܘܒܝܢ ܠܐ ܦܠܼܚ ܇ ܕܢܝܿܚܐ ܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܢܗܘܼܐ ܠܗ. ܘܢܥܗܘܕ ܡܿܢ ܕܐܡܪ ܗܟܢܐ ܠܥܟܪ ܕܐܦ ܗܼܘ ܓܼܙܪ ܐܝܟ ܟܠܗ ܐܝܣܪܐܝܠ. ܐܠܐ ܠܐ ܐܘܬܪ̈ܬܗ ܓܙܘܪ̈ܬܗ ܡܕܡ ܒܗ̇ܝ ܕܠܐ ܢܼܛܪ ܡܠܬ ܦܘܡܗ ܕܡܪܝܐ. ܐܠܐ ܗܘܼܐ ܐܬܿܐ ܕܣܘܪ̈ܕܐ ܠܟܠܗ ܐܝܣܪܐܝܠ ܀
But let no one imagine, among the lazy and the negligent, that, because of the vigil of consecration performed by the brother in his cell, he will find rest and will settle there, if in the monastery he has not perfectly exercised himself in humility and obedience toward his spiritual fathers and brothers. God forbid, indeed, that he who has not exercised himself in the monastery should find rest in the cell! Let him who speaks thus remember Achar who was circumcised, he too, like all Israel, but to whom the circumcision was of no profit, because he had not kept the word from the mouth of the Lord; he was, on the contrary, a sign of terror for all Israel.
ܐܦ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܓܝܪ ܕܦ̇ܠܚܝܢ ܒܩܢܘܒܝܢ ܘܐܝܟ ܙܕܩܐ ܠܐ ܦ̇ܠܚܝܢ ܡܐ ܕܢ̇ܦܩܝܢ ܠܩܠܝܬܐ ܡܬܟ̇ܢܫ ܘܣܿܡܟ ܥܠܝܗܘܢ. ܝܓܪܐ ܕܟ̈ܐܦܐ ܕܚ̈ܘܫܒܐ ܒܝ̈ܫܼܐ. ܘܫܿܕܝܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܩܠܝܬܐ ܡܢܗ̇ ܠܒܪ. ܐܝܟ ܟܐܦܐ ܕܡܫܬܕܝܐ ܒܩܠܼܥܐ. ܘܗ̇ܘܝܢ ܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܐ ܠܟܠܗ ܕܘܒܪܐ. ܕܝܚܝܕܝܘܬܼܐ ܘܠܛܒ̈ܬܐ ܕܒܗܿ. ܐܝܟ ܥ̇ܟܪ ܕܐܬܦܼܪܫ ܡܢ ܟܠܗ̇ ܟܢܘܫܬܗ ܕܡܪܝܐ ܇ ܘܗܼܘܐ ܢܘܟܪܝܐ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܡܘܠܟܢܐ ܘܠܛܒ̈ܬܐ ܕܒܗ̇ ܀
Those also who exercise themselves in the monastery without doing (it) as they should, as soon as they depart to a cell, a heap of stones of evil thoughts gathers and weighs upon them; the cell casts them outside far from it, like a stone thrown by a sling; and they become strangers to all the conduct of the solitary life and to the goods that are (found) therein, like Achar who was separated from the whole assembly of the Lord, and who became a stranger to the promised land and to the goods that are (found) therein.

### Reminder of general advice
ܗ̇ܘ ܓܝܪ ܕܢܦܿܩ ܚܼܕܬܐܝܬ ܠܩܠܝܬܐ ܗܿܠܝܢ ܙܿܕܩ ܠܗ ܠܡܛܪ ܫܬܩܼܐ ܐܡܝܢܐ. ܗ̇ܝ ܕܠܐ ܬܗܼܘܐ ܠܗ ܦܪܗܝܣܝܐ ܓܡܝܪܐܝܬ ܥܡ ܐܢܫ. ܪ̈ܚܝܼܩܘܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܓܢܣܐ ܘܛܘܗܡܐ. ܘܒܐܝܡܡܐ ܠܒܪ ܡܢ ܬܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܩܠܝܬܗ ܠܐ ܢܬܚܙܐ. ܣܛܪ ܡܢ ܐܢܢܩܐ ܕܓܘܐ ܐܘ ܟܘܪ̈ܗܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܬܣܝܡ ܥܠܝܗܘܢ ܢܡܘܣܐ. ܡܶܢ ܓܝܪ ܕܨܒܐ ܕܢܛܥܸܡ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܚ̈ܠܝܐ ܕܒܫܼܠܝܐ ܇ ܒܫ̇ܢܬܐ ܩܕܡܝܬܐ ܕܝܿܬܒ ܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܠܐ ܢܥܼܒܕ ܒܘܬܐ ܠܒܪ
67 He who has departed newly to a cell must observe the following: continual silence; having absolutely no familiarity with anyone whatsoever; distancing oneself from family and kin; that one not be seen by day outside the door of one's cell, except for natural necessity, or for illness, for which there is no prescription. He, indeed, who wants to taste the savory fruits of silence, must not spend, the first year of the stay in the cell, any night outside of it. Let his fasting and his vigil be


[p. 338] {#po.45.p338}
ܡܢܗܿ. ܨܘܡܗ ܕܝܢ ܘܫܼܗܪܗ ܇ ܒܛܟܼܣܐ ܘܒܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܢܗܼܘܐ. ܗ̇ܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܨܘܡܐ ܕܪܡܫܝܐ. ܘܦܠܓܗ ܕܠܝܠܝܐ ܠܫܗܖܪܐ ܘܦܠܓܗ ܠܢܝ̇ܚܐ ܕܦܓܪܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܠܬܗ ܕܛܘܒܢܐ ܐܒܐ ܐܫܥܝܐ ܀
accomplished with order and measure, that is to say, the evening fast, and one half of the night in vigil, and (the other) half in bodily rest, according to the word of the blessed Abba Isaiah.
ܠܐ ܬܬܝܥܢ ܥܠ ܥܡ̈ܠܐ ܝܬܝܪ̈ܐ ܐܘ ܐܚܐ ܕܢܦܼܩ ܚܕܬܐܝܬ ܠܩܠܝܬܐ ܆ ܕܠܐ ܬܬܓܚܟ ܡܢ ܫ̈ܺܐܕܐ, ܡܛܠ ܕܣܿܓܝ ܡܿܪܬܚܝܢ ܫܺܐܕܐ ܠܐܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܒܫܿܢܬܐ ܩܕܡܝܬܐ ܕܢܿܦܩ ܠܩܠܝܬܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܝܿܕܥܝܢ ܫܺܐܕܐ ܠܝ̈ܛܐ. ܕܐܢܗܼܘ ܕܡܫܬܼܡܥ ܠܗܘܢ ܐܚܐ ܒܫܢܬܐ ܩܕܡܝܬܐ ܇ ܘܥ̇ܒܕ ܨܒܝܢܗܘܢ ܒܥܡ̈ܠܐ ܝܬܝܪ̈ܐ ܆ ܡܚܒܠܝܢ ܠܦܓܪܗ ܘܠܢܦܫܗ. ܠܦܓܪܗ ܡܚܒܠܝܢ ܒܟ̈ܐܒܐ ܘܒܟܘܪ̈ܗܢܐ. ܘܠܢܦܫܗ ܡܪܝܡܝܢ ܠܗܿ. ܒܫܘܒܚܐ ܣܪܝܩܐ. ܘܡܬܒܥܝܢ ܠܗ̇ ܒܗܘܬܐ ܕܚܫܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ ܀
Do not be greedy for excessive labors, brother who has departed newly to a cell, so as not to become the laughingstock of the demons. For the demons excite the monk greatly the first year he has departed to a cell. The cursed demons know, indeed, that if the brother obeys them the first year, and does their will, (by indulging) in excessive labors, they destroy his body and his soul: they destroy his body through pains and illnesses; they exalt his soul through vainglory, then they engulf him in the abyss of the passion of fornication.
ܡܛܠ ܕܦܓܪܐ ܐܢ ܠܐ ܩܿܢܐ ܬܪ̈ܒܝܬܐ ܒܥܡ̈ܠܐ ܝܘܡ ܡܢ ܝܘܡ ܆ ܡܬܚܿܒܠ ܘܫܿܪܟ ܠܟܐ̈ܒܐ ܘܠܟܘܪ̈ܗܢܐ ܥܕܡܐ ܕܢܿܦܠ ܒܐܝܼܕܐ ܕܒܢ̈ܝ ܓܠܝܢܘܣ ܘܥ̇ܒܕܝܢ ܒܗ ܫܐ̈ܕܐ ܟܠܗ ܨܒܝܢܗܘܢ ܒܝܫܐ. ܡܛܠܗܕܐ ܐܡܿܪܬ. ܕܒܫܿܢܬܐ ܩܕܡܝܬܐ ܒܛܟܣܼܐ ܘܒܡܫܘܚܼܬܐ ܢܗܼܘܘܢ ܥܡ̈ܠܐ ܕܨܘܡܐ ܘܕܫܗܖܪ̈ܐ ܘܕܩܪܝܢܐ. ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܓܝܪ ܬܠܬܐ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܡܝܬܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܇ ܬܚܿܒ ܦܓܪܐ ܘܡܬܚܲܒܠ ܡܢ ܩܕܡ ܕܡܬܥܿܝܕ ܒܗܽܘܢ. ܨܘܡܐ ܓܝܪ ܥ̇ܒܕ ܢܟܝܢܝܐ ܒܐܣܛܘܡܟܐ. ܘܒܟܒܕܐ ܘܒܛܚܼܠܐ. ܘܫܗܖܪ̈ܐ ܥܿܒܕ ܢܟܝܢܐ ܒܫ̈ܩܐ ܘܒܟܘܠ̈ܝܬܐ. ܘܡܢ ܩܪܝܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܫܘܚܬܐ. ܡܬܚܿܒܠܐ
68 If the body, indeed, does not acquire resistance, day after day, through labors, it destroys itself—and it is subject to pains and illnesses until it falls into the hands of the sons of Galen, and the demons accomplish in him all their perverse will. That is why I said that, the first year, the labors of fasting, of vigil, and of reading should be done with order and moderation. For, because of these three virtuous actions, the body would weaken and destroy itself before having become accustomed to them. Fasting, indeed, can harm the stomach, the liver, and the spleen; the vigil can harm the legs and the kidneys; and through immoderate reading a part of the brain can be altered, and pain can follow in the eyes.
ܡܢ̇̈ܬܐ ܕܡܘܚܼܐ ܘܗܘ̇ܐ ܢܟܝܢܐ ܒܥܝ̈ܢܐ ܀ ܡܐܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܦܚܪܐ ܡܐ ܕܡܬܥܒܕ ܐܢܗܼܘ ܕܠܐ ܡܫܬܓ݂ܪ ܒܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܘܪܡ̇ܐ ܐܢܬ ܒܗ ܡ̈ܝܼܐ ܡܬܚܲܒܠ, ܐܢܕܝܢ ܣܿܒܥ ܪܬܚܗܿ ܕܢܘܪ̈ܐ. ܟܠ ܨܒܘ ܕܒܿܥܐ ܐܢܬ ܪܡܿܐ ܐܢܬ ܒܗ. ܘܠܐ ܡܬܚܿܒܠ ܐܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܐܢ ܚܡܪܐ ܘܐܢ ܡܫܚܐ ܀
When an earthen vessel has been modeled, if it has not been baked in the fire and you pour water into it, it is unusable; but if it has been saturated with the heat of the fire, you will pour into it whatever you want, whether it be water, wine, or oil, and it will not be unusable.
ܗܟܢܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܦ ܦܓܪܐ. ܕܐܢܗܼܘ ܕܡܿܘܣܦ ܐܢܬ ܠܗ ܥܡ̈ܠܐ ܒܫܿܢܬܐ ܩܕܡܝܬܐ ܩܕܡ ܕܡܬܥ̇ܝܕ ܒܐܬܘܢܐ ܕܩܠܝܬܐ ܫܢܿܬܐ ܟܠܗ ܡܬܚܿܒܠ ܘܠܐ ܚܿܫܚ ܠܥܡ̈ܠܐ. ܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܐܬܥܿܝܕ ܒܥܡ̈ܠܐ: ܐܘܣܼܦ ܠܗܼ ܟܡܐ ܕܒ̇ܥܐ ܐܢܬ ܐܢ ܨܘܡܐ ܕܫܒܼ̈ܐ ܘܐܢ ܫܗܖܐ ܕܠܝ̈ܠܘܬܐ. ܘܐܢ ܩܪܝܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܦܣܿܩ. ܘܠܐ ܡܬܟܼܪܗ ܦܓܪܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܐܬܥ̇ܝܕ ܒܗܘܢ ܀ ܙܥܼܪ ܐܣܛܘܡܟܐ. ܐܬܩܲܛܢ ܡܘܚܐ ܡܢ ܟܐܡܼܘ ܝܬܝܪܐ. ܢܗܪ ܥܝ̈ܢܐ. ܟܒܕܐ ܘܛܚܠܐ ܩܢܼܘ ܥ̇ܝܕܐ ܒܫܩܘܬܐ ܕܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܠܐ ܡ̇ܫܦܠܝܢ ܡܢ
69 It is the same with the body: if, the first year, you add to it labors before it has been accustomed to the crucible of the cell for an entire year, it destroys itself and is no longer fit for labors. If, on the contrary, it has been accustomed to labors, you will add as much as you want, whether it be the fasting of weeks, or the vigil of nights, or (even) uninterrupted reading, and the body will not fall ill, because it has been accustomed to it. The stomach has thus, indeed, shrunk, the brain has been lightened of the excess of humor, the eyes have cleared, the liver and the spleen have acquired the habit of drinking only water and they no longer weaken due to the impoverishment


[p. 340] {#po.45.p340}
ܓܠܝܙܘܬܐ ܕܕܡܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܐܬܩ̇ܿܛܢܘ ܫ̈ܒܹܝܠܐ ܕܕܡܐ. ܘܒܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܬܒܿܥܝܢ. ܟܘ̈ܠܝܬܐ ܩܢܼ̈ܝ ܚܡܝܼܡܘܬ̣ܐ ܟܝܿܢܝܬܐ ܕܝܠܗܝܢ. ܘܠܐ ܬܒ̈ܥܝܢ ܚܡܝܡܘܬܐ ܝܬܝܪܬܐ. ܐܬܢܿܦܨܘ ܦܠܓܡܐ ܕܡܠܝܘܬܐ ܕܫ̈ܩܼܐ. ܘܡܛܠ ܩܠܝܠܘܬܗ ܕܦܓܪܐ ܠܐ ܡܲܫܦܠܝܢ ܐܘ̇ ܡܬܢܼܟܝܢ. ܡܢ ܫܗܖ̈ܐ ܝܬܝܪܐ ܀
of the blood; [for the blood vessels have tightened and demand only moderation. The kidneys have acquired their natural heat and do not require additional heat; the inflammation of the swelling of the legs has subsided and, because of the lightness of the body, (the legs) are neither weakened nor tired by the excess of vigil.
ܛܘܒܝܗܘܢ ܠܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܦܿܠܚܝܢ ܒܝܕܥܬܐ ܡܫܶܢܬܐ ܩܕܡܝܬܐ ܘܡܨܥܝܬܐ ܘܐܚܪܝܬܐ. ܕܟܡܐ ܕܡܿܘܣܦܝܢ ܥܡ̈ܠܐ ܕܡܝܬܪ̈ܘܬܐ. ܬܘܒ ܬܒ̇ܥ ܦܓܪܗܘܢ ܘܟܡܐ ܕܥܿ̇ܡܠܝܢ ܥܡ̈ܠܐ ܡܬܢܿܗܖ ܘܡܬܩܲܛܢ ܗܘܢܗܘܢ. ܘܡܬܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܢܦܫܗܘܢ. ܘܝ ܠܗ̇ܿܐ ܠܠܐ ܝܕܥܬܐ ܕܥܕ ܡܗܒܒܐ ܒܥܡ̈ܠܐ ܚܡܬ ܠܗ̇. ܘܥܕܬܐ ܒ̇ܥܝܐ ܕܬܚܼܕܐ ܢܩܦܬ ܥܩܼܬܐ ܘܟܪܝܘܬܐ ܘܦܣܿܩ ܣܒܪܐ ܠܚܕܘܬܗܿ. ܘܟܕ ܣ̇ܿܒܪ ܗܿܘ ܕܐܚܼܝܕ ܒܠܐ ܝܕܥܬܐ ܕܗܘܢܗ ܡܬܢܿܗܪܘ ܡܬܢܼܲܗܖ ܘܢܦܫܗ ܡܬܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܒܥܡ̈ܠܘܗܝ ܡܬܥܲܒܝܘ ܡܬܥܿܒܐ ܗܘܢܗ ܘܡܬܡܠܐ ܠܒܗ ܚܫܼܟܐ. ܘܙܥ̇ܪܐ ܢܦܫܗ. ܘܡܣܬܡܝܐ ܦܪܘܫܘܬܗ̇ ܡܢ ܫ̣ܘܒܚܐ ܣܪܝܩܐ ܕܡܿܠܐ ܠܗ̇ ܚܘ̈ܫܒܐ ܒܝ̈ܫܹܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܝܕܥܬܐ ܟܠܗ̇ ܢܘܗܼܪ̈ܐ ܗܝ ܘܠܐ ܝܕܥܬܐ ܟܠܗ̇ ܚܫܟܐ
70 Blessed are those who devote themselves to exercises with knowledge, during the (three) years, the first, the middle, and the final. For, the more they increase the labors of virtue, the more their body demands them; and, as they devote themselves to labors, their intellect brightens and refines itself, and their soul blossoms. Woe to non-knowledge! For hardly has (the monk) blossomed in labors, than he is withered; and, while he is in search of joy, it is anguish, sadness, and despair that have taken the place of his joy; and while he who is a prisoner of ignorance deems that his intellect is truly enlightened, and his soul blossomed by his labors, his intellect has thickened, his heart completely darkened, his soul shriveled, and the discernment of the latter blinded by the vainglory that fills it with perverse thoughts. Knowledge, indeed, is all light, but ignorance is all darkness.
ܗ ܀ ܠܝܬܼ ܓܝܪ ܟܠܝܬܐ ܡܐ ܕܩܢܐ ܦܓܪܐ ܬܪ̈ܒܝܬܐ ܒܥܡ̈ܠܐ ܕܐܢ ܨܒܐ ܩܐܡ ܡܬܚܐ ܕܝܘ̈ܡܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܣܝܒܪܬܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܦܓܪܐ ܡܐ ܕܐܬܥܨܪ ܒܥܡ̈ܠܐ ܕܥܢܘܝܘܬܐ ܙܒܢܐ ܣܓܝܐܐ ܠܐ ܡܫܬܠܛܝܢ ܒܗ ܟ̈ܐܒܐ ܘܟܘܪ̈ܗܢܐ. ܐܠܐ ܐܢ ܡܫܬܒܩܢܘܬܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܐ ܒܗ ܇ ܡܢ ܐܠܗܐ ܒܐܝܕܐ ܕܫ̈ܐܕܐ ܡܪ̈ܘܕܐ ܇ ܐܝܟ ܕܥܒܕ ܣܛܢܐ ܠܘܬ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܐܝܘܒ ܇ ܕܟܕ ܠܐ ܐܫܟܚ ܒܗ ܐܬܪܐ ܕܒܝܫܬܐ ܇ ܡܚܝܗܝ ܠܦܓܪܗ ܡܢ ܦܣܬ ܪܓܠܗ ܘܥܕܡܐ ܠܡܘܚܗ ܇ ܘܠܐ ܫܒܩ ܒܦܓܪܗ ܕܘܟܬܐ ܕܫܪܝܪܐ ܇ ܘܗܘ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܩܘܝ ܒܬܡܝܡܘܬܗ ܘܠܐ ܐܬܪ̈ܦܝ ܡܢ ܚܘܡܣܢܗ ܘܚܘܒܗ
71 For, once the body has acquired more resistance thanks to labors, nothing prevents it, if it wishes, from remaining for days without food; because the body, once tamed by the labors of asceticism for a long time, can no longer be subject to pains and illnesses; except, however, if, through the intermediary of rebellious demons, a permission is granted by God regarding it. This is what Satan did with regard to the blessed Job: having found in him no defective point, he struck his body from the heel of the foot to the brain without leaving any healthy spot in his body. And this blessed one persevered in his righteousness and he did not slacken, neither in his endurance, nor in his love for God.
ܕܠܘܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܀ ܗܟܢܐ ܐܘ ܚܒܝܒܗ ܪ̈ܚܝܡܐ ܕܢܦܫܝ ܐܦ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܒܐܘܪ̈ܚܐ ܬܪ̈ܝܨܬܐ ܕܦܘܩ̈ܕܢܘܗܝ ܕܡܚܝܢܢ ܪ̈ܕܝܢ ܇ ܘܐܬܢܦܩܘ ܦܓܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܒܥܡܠܐ ܕܥܢܘܝܘܬܐ ܆ ܟܡܐ ܙܒܢܐ ܕܬܟܬܘܫܗܘܢ ܥܡ ܚ̈ܫܐ ܗܘ ܠܐ ܡܬܟܪܗܝܢ ܦܓܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܡܢ ܥܡ̈ܠܢܐ ܥܕܡܐ ܕܡܛܐ ܙܒܢܐ ܕܣܝܒܘܬܐ ܇ ܕܠܝܬ ܦܪܘܣ ܡܢ ܗܕܐ ܀
It is thus, O very dear friend of my soul, for those who advance on the straight path of the commandments of our Life-giver, and whose bodies exercise themselves in the labors of asceticism; whatever the duration of their combat with the passions, their bodies are not struck by illness, due to the labors, until arrives the time of old age from which there is no way (to escape).


[p. 342] {#po.45.p342}
ܐܘܪ̈ܚܐ ܓܝܪ ܩܠܝܠܬܐ ܕܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܕܐܫܠܡܘ ܠܢ ܐܒ̈ܗܝܢ. ܗܕܐ ܗܝ ܨܘܡܐ ܕܡܢ ܡܫܐ ܠܪܡܫܐ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܡܠܐ ܐܢܫ ܟܪܣܗ. ܠܟܪܣܐ ܕܝܢ ܥܒܝܬ ܐܡܝܢܐܝܬ ܢܩܝܦܝܢ ܠܗ̇ ܟ̈ܐܒܐ ܘܟܘܪ̈ܗܢܐ. ܘܝܘܩܪܐ ܣܓܝܐܐ ܕܫܢܬܐ. ܘܠܣܓܝܐܘܬܐ ܕܫܢܬܐ ܢܩܝܦܝܢ ܠܗ̇ ܚ̈ܠܡܐ ܘܚܙܘ̈ܢܐ ܕܚ̈ܝܠܐ ܕܦܢ̈ܛܣܝܐ ܢܕܝ̈ܕܬܐ. ܘܠܗܠܝܢ ܢܩܝܦ ܫܐܕܐ ܕܩܘܛܥܐ ܘܗܘ ܕܦܗܝܐ ܕܚܘܫ̈ܒܐ ܘܕܒܝܫܐ ܡܢ ܟܠܗܝܢ ܦܣܩ ܣܒܪܐ ܘܥܩܬܐ ܘܟܪܝܘܬܐ. ܗܠܝܢ ܕܡܚܒ̈ܠܝܢ ܠܬܩܢܘܬܗ ܕܗܘܢܐ ܘܡܣ̈ܡܝܢ
72 The rapid path of the Kingdom that our Fathers have bequeathed to us, here it is: fasting (going) from evening to evening without filling the belly, for to a full belly are always added for it pains, illnesses, and an abundant and overwhelming sleep. And, to the great overwhelming of sleep, are added dreams and frightful visions of abominable images. To these is linked the demon of acedia and that of the wandering of thoughts; and, what is worse than all, despair, anguish, and sadness which harm the balance of the intellect and blind the discernment of the soul.
ܠܦܪܘܫܘܬܗ ܕܢܦܫܐ ܀ ܗܝ ܕܝܢ ܕܢܨܘܡ ܐܢܫ ܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܕܡܬܥܝܕ ܒܥܡ̈ܠܐ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܝܘ̈ܡܝܢ ܐܘ ܬܠܬܐ ܐܘ ܫܒܬܐ ܟܠܗ̇ ܥܠ ܗܠܝܢ ܠܐ ܣܝܡ ܢܡܘܣܐ. ܟܠܢܫ ܐܝܟ ܕܨܒܐ ܢܕܒܪ ܚܝ̈ܘܗܝ. ܒܪܡ ܒܝܕܥܬܐ ܘܒܡܠܟܐ ܕܝܕܘ̈ܥܬܢܝܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܦܠܚ ܗܘ ܕܝܢ ܕܕܠܐ ܡܠܟܐ ܥܡܠ. ܠܐܐܪ ܟܬܫ. ܘܕܠܐ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܗܘ ܟܠܗ ܦܘܠܚܢܗ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܡܪ ܟܬܒܐ. ܕܚܟܝܡܐ ܡܬܡܠܟ ܘܥܒܕ. ܘܣܟܠܐ ܠܘܩܒܠ ܐܦܘ̈ܗܝ ܐܙܠ. ܡܛܠ ܕܟܠ ܡܝܬܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܒܡܠܟܐ ܕܝܕܘܥ̈ܬܢܐ ܠܐ ܡܬܦܠܚܐ ܠܚܨ ܒܐ ܢܩܝܒܐ ܕܡܝܐ. ܕܟܠ ܡ̈ܝܐ ܕܪ̈ܡܝܢ ܒܗ ܠܐܒܕܢܐ ܫܪܟܝܢ. ܛܘܒܘܗܝ ܠܗܘ ܕܡܬܡܠܟ ܘܥܒܕ. ܕܡܢ ܩܕܡ ܕܦܠܚ ܠܗ̇ ܠܡܝܬܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܡܬܒܣܡ ܗܘܢܗ ܒܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܕܝܕܥܬܗ̇ ܀
As for the fact that someone, after having been accustomed to labors, fasts every two or three days, or even the whole week, for these things there is no established law. Let each of them conduct his life as he sees fit, provided that he does it with knowledge and following the counsel of those who are knowledgeable. For he who works without being counseled, struggles with the air, and all his (ascetic) exercise is without fruits, as the Scripture says: The wise man asks for counsel and acts; and the fool goes to the opposite. Every virtue, indeed, which is not exercised with the counsel of the knowledgeable, is like a pierced vessel. All the water that one pours into it goes off to be lost. Happy is he who asks for counsel and acts; for, even before exercising the virtue, his intellect savors the fruits of the knowledge (of it). Regulation of a day
ܒܫܢܬܐ ܓܝܪ ܩܕܡܝܬܐ ܕܝܬܒ ܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܐܚܐ ܦܠܘܚܐ ܒܝܬ ܠܠܝܐ ܠܐܝܡܡܐ ܕܘܝܕ ܢܫܡܠܐ. ܟܕ ܠܐ ܡܘܣܦ ܘܠܐ ܡܒܨܪ ܡܢܗ. ܡܢ ܒܬܪ̈ܟܢ ܕܝܢ ܟܡܐ ܕܨܒܐ ܢܘܣܦ. ܘܟܡܐ ܕܨܒܐ ܢܒܨܪ. ܠܦܘܬ ܥ̈ܠܬܐ ܕܡܣܬܩ̈ܒܠܢ ܠܗ ܠܐܚܐ. ܕܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܢܫܡܫ ܐܠܨܐ ܠܡܐܡܪ. ܐܦܢ ܐܡܝܪܐ ܠܐܒ̈ܗܬܐ ܩܕ̈ܝܫܐ ܀
73 Therefore, during the first year of his stay in the cell, the practicing brother will recite in its entirety (his) David between (the end of) the night and (the rising of) the day, without adding anything to it or subtracting anything from it. In the following, let him add to it as much as he wants, and let him subtract from it as much as he wants, according to the motives that will present themselves to the brother. As for the manner of celebrating, it is necessary (for us) to speak of it, although this has (already) been indicated by the holy Fathers.
ܡܐ ܕܢܗܪ̈ ܨܦܪܐ ܢܫܝܓ ܐܝ̈ܕܘܗܝ ܘܢܥܒܕ ܡܛܘܢܝܐ ܩܕܡ ܨܠܝܒܐ ܥܕܡܐ ܕܡܬܟܢܫܝܢ ܚܘܫ̈ܒܘܗܝ ܡܢ ܦܗܝܐ ܇ ܘܪܬܚ ܠܗ ܒܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܪ̈ܚܡܬܗ ܕܡܪܢ. ܟܕ ܡܬܟܫܦ ܠܗ ܠܡܪܢ ܒܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܚܢܝ̈ܓܬܐ ܘܐܡܪ. ܐܠܗܐ ܐܫܘܢܝ ܕܢܬܒܣܡ ܡܕܥܝ ܒܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܕܡܕܒܪܢܘܬܗ ܕܒܠܓܟ ܚܒܝܒܐ. ܡܪܢ ܓܠܝ ܬܚܦܝܬܐ ܕܚ̈ܫܐ ܕܩܪܝܡܐ ܥܠ ܐܦܘ̈ܗܝ ܕܡܕܥܝ. ܘܐܙܠܓ ܢܘܗܪܟ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܒܠܒܝ ܕܢܥܘܠ ܡܕܥܝ ܠܓܘ ܡܢ ܓܘܫܡܐ ܒܪܝܐ
When the dawn rises, let him wash his hands, let him make the metania before the cross, until his thoughts gather themselves far from wandering, and he warms himself with the fire of the love of Our Lord, begging Him with tears of lamentation and let him say: "God, make me worthy so that my spirit may find its delights in the intellections of the Economy of your beloved Son. O Our Lord, remove the veil of the passions spread over the face of my spirit. Kindle your holy light in my heart, so that my spirit may penetrate to the intimacy of the material text (written) in ink, and that with the eye


[p. 344] {#po.45.p344}
ܕܕܝܘܬܐ ܘܐܚܼܙܐ ܒܥܝܢܐ ܢܗܼܝܪܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܝ ܠܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܩܕܝ̈ܫܐ ܕܟܣܝܢ ܒܣܒܪܬܟ. ܗܼܒ ܠܝ ܡܪܝ ܒܛܝܒܘܬܟ ܘܐܫܘܼܢܝ ܒܡܪܚܡܢܘܬܟ; ܕܠܐ ܢܥܼܢܕ ܥܕܢܟ ܡܢ ܠܒܝ ܠܐ ܒܠܠܝܐ ܘܠܐ ܒܐܝܡܡܐ ܀ ܘܡܐ ܕܟܲܢܫܬ ܚܘ̈ܫܒܝܟ ܐܚܐ ܦܲܠܘܚܐ ܒܡ̈ܠܐ ܕܦܝܿܣܐ ܕܐܝܟ ܗܠܝܢ ܫܠܡ ܨܠܝܒܐ ܘܣܼܒ ܐܘܢܓܠܝܘܢ ܒܐܝܕܝܟ ܘܣܝܼܡܝܗܝ ܥܠ ܥܝ̈ܢܝܟ. ܘܥܠ ܠܒܟ. ܘܩܘܡ ܩܕܡ ܨܠܝܒܐ ܥܠ ܕ̈ܓܠܝܟ. ܟܕ ܠܐ ܝܿܬܒ ܐܢܬ ܥܠ ܐܪܥܐ. ܘܟܠ ܨܚܚܐ ܕܩܿܪܐ ܐܢܬ ܒܗ ܣܝܡܝܗ ܥܠ ܒܣܕܝܐ. ܘܐܬܓܪܓܚ ܩܕܡܘܗܝ ܥܕܡܐ ܠܥܣܪ ܙܒ̈ܢܝܢ. ܟܕ ܡܲܣܩ ܐܢܬ ܩܘܒܠܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܇ ܠܗܿܘ ܕܐܫܘܝܟ ܠܡ̣ܗܪܲܓ ܘܠܡܼܩܪܐ ܇ ܒܪܐܙܐ ܕܟܣܐ ܗܘܐ ܡܢ ܕܪ̈ܐ ܘܡܢ ܫܖ̈ܒܬܐ ܇ ܐܝܟ ܡܠܬ ܐܠܗܝܐ ܦܘܠܘܣ. ܘܡܢ ܗܕܐ ܣܓܕܬܐ ܓܠܼܝܬܐ ܕܥܿܒܕ ܐܢܬ ܩܕܡܘܗܝ ܡܬܝܿܠܕܐ ܒܠܒܟ ܗܺܝ ܣܓܕܬܐ ܡܬܝܕܥܢܝܬܐ ܘܩܘܒܠܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܇ ܕܠܐ ܡܨܐ ܠܫܢܐ ܕܒܣܪܐ
illuminated of my soul, I may see the holy mysteries hidden in your message. Grant me, My Lord, by your grace, and make me worthy, by your mercy, that your memory may not leave my heart, neither by day nor by night."
ܕܢܐܡܪܝܗܿ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܿ ܀ ܛܘܒܘܗܝ ܠܗ̇ܘ ܕܐܫܼܬܘܝ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܡܥܒ̈ܕܢܘܬܐ ܡܝܬܪ̈ܬܐ. ܕܦܘܠܚܢܗ ܥܡ ܕܒܝܬ ܓܒܪܐܝܠ ܘܡܝܟܝܠ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ. ܫܪܝܪܐܝܬ ܐܘܿ ܚܒܝܒ. ܕܝܲܕܥ ܐܢܐ ܐܢܫ ܡܢ ܐܚ̈ܐ ܕܒܚܕ ܡܢ ܝܘܡ̈ܝܢ ܟܕ ܡܬܓܲܪܓܚ ܩܕܡܘܗܝ ܕܐܘܢܓܠܝܘܢ ܣܓܝܕܐ ܟܕ ܡܬܚ̈ܝܢ ܐܝ̈ܕܘܗܝ ܘܥܝ̈ܢܘܗܝ ܠܫܡܝܐ ܡܢܫܠ ܐܬܦܬܼܚ ܠܒܗ ܘܐܬܡܼܠܝ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܡܿܠܠ. ܘܐܫܬܼܘܝ ܠܗܿܝ ܣܓܕܬܐ ܡܬܝܕܥܢܝܬܐ ܘܠܗܽܘ ܩܘܒܠܛܝܒܘܬܐ. ܘܐܡ̇ܪ ܗܘܐ ܩܕܡܝ ܗ̇ܘ ܐܚܐ. ܕܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܝܘܡ̈ܝܢ ܗ̇ܘܝܬ ܕܠܐ ܣܝܒܪܬܐ ܢܣܿܒܬ ܘܠܐ ܫܢܬܐ ܕܡܿܟܬ. ܘܐܫܬܒܼܝ ܚܘܫܒܝ ܡܢ ܥܠܡܐ ܘܡܢ ܟܠܡܕܡ ܕܐܝܬ ܒܗ. ܘܐܡ̇ܿܪ ܗܘܐ. ܕܒܠܚܘܕ ܕܐܬܥܿܠܝ ܡܕܥܝ ܝ̇ܕܥܬ. ܕܚܰܙܝܬ ܕܝܢ ܘܫ̇ܡܥܬ ܝܿܕܥ ܐܢܐ. ܕܡܿܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܚ̇ܙܝܬ ܐܘ̇ ܡ̈ܢܐ ܫܡܿܥܬ ܠܐ ܝܿܕܥ ܐܢܐ ܕܐܡܿܪ ܩܕܡܝܟ. ܡܛܠ ܕܖ̈ܐܙܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܡܿܠܠܝܢ ܒܠܫܢܐ ܕܒܣܪܐ. ܐܘ̇ ܡܬܟ̇ܬܒܝܢ ܒܩܢܝܐ ܘܕܝܘܬܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܪ̈ܗܒܘܢܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܗܿܢܘܢ ܛܘ̈ܒܐ ܠܐ ܡܬܡ̈ܠܠܝܐ. ܕܝ̇ܗܒ ܐܠܗܐ ܡܪܐ ܟܠ ܡܢ ܒܬ ܩܝܡܼܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܒܝܬ ܡܝܼܬ̈ܐ. ܗ̇ܿܢܘܢ ܕܛܘܒܢܐ ܦܘܠܘܣ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܬܗܪܐ ܚܿܐܪ ܗܘܐ ܒܗܘܢ. ܘܐܡܿܪ. ܕܡܕܡ ܕܥܝܢܐ ܠܐ ܚܙܬ ܘܐܕܢܐ ܠܐ ܫܡܥܼܬ. ܘܥܠ ܠܒܐ ܕܒܪܢܫܐ ܠܐ ܣܠܩ ܡܕܡ ܕܛܲܝܼܒ ܐܠܗܐ ܠܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܪ̈ܚܿܡܝܢ ܠܗ ܀
Reading of the Scripture and the Fathers 74 When you have gathered your thoughts, practicing brother, through words of supplication of this sort, salute the cross and take the Gospel into your hands. Place it on your eyes and on your heart. Stand before the cross, on your feet, without sitting on the ground and, at each chapter that you read there, place the (book) on a cushion and prostrate yourself before it up to ten times, sending up acts of thanksgiving toward Him who has made you worthy to meditate and to read the mystery that had been hidden since ages and generations, according to the word of the divine Paul. Thanks to this exterior adoration that you do before it, there will be born in your heart this adoration in spirit and this act of thanksgiving that a tongue of flesh cannot express as it is.


[p. 346] {#po.45.p346}
ܘܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܩ̇ܪܐ ܐܢܬ ܒܐܘܢܓܠܝܘܢ ܐܘ̇ ܐܚܐ ܦ̇ܠܘܚܐ ܇ ܘܢܣܒ ܐܢܬ ܡܢܗ ܒܘܪ̈ܟܬܐ ܗܝ ܕܡܩܲܕܫܐ ܠܢܦܫܟ ܒܪܘܚܐ ܕܩܘܕܫܐ ܕܟܣܐ ܒܡ̈ܠܘܗܝ ܚܝ̈ܬܐ ܕܡܪܢ ܝܫܘܥ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܇ ܗܿܝܕܝܢ ܩܼܪܝ ܒܫܠܝܚܼܐ ܘܒܦܪܟܣܝܣ. ܥܕܡܐ ܕܡܿܛܐ ܥܕܢܐ ܕܬܠܬ ܫ̈ܥܝܢ. ܟܕ ܩܢܘܢܝܐ ܕܡܛܘܢܝܐ ܗܘܐ ܕܐܘܢܓܠܝܘܢ ܡܫ̇ܡܠܐ ܐܢܬ. ܡܛܠ ܕܟܡܐ ܙܒ̈ܢܝܢ ܐܦ ܡܢ ܩܪܝܢܐ ܕܗܿܠܝܢ ܡܫܬܟ̇ܚܐ ܠܟ ܡܪܓܢܝܬܐ ܗܺܝ ܡܿܠܝܬ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܕܡܢ ܠܥܠ
Blessed is he who has been deemed worthy of these two virtuous operations, for his service is done in the company of the (angels) of the house of Gabriel and Michael. In truth, O very dear one, I know one of the brothers who, one day when he had prostrated himself before the adorable Gospel, his hands and eyes stretched toward heaven, his heart opened suddenly and was filled with an ineffable light. He was made worthy of this adoration in spirit and of this act of thanksgiving. And this brother said before me: "Here are two days that I have not taken food and that I have not lain down to sleep. My thought has been held prisoner outside the world and everything that is found therein." And he said: "That my spirit alone has been exalted, I know. The fact that I have seen and that I have heard, I know. But what I have seen or what I have heard, I do not know how to say before you, for they are mysteries inexpressible by a tongue of flesh, or which cannot be written with a reed and ink, because they are the earnest of those ineffable goods that God, the Lord, Master of the Universe, grants after the resurrection from the dead, those (goods) that the blessed Paul contemplated as in ecstasy by saying: What the eye has not seen, nor the ear heard, and what has not mounted to the heart of man, God has prepared for those who love Him."
ܐܡܪܬ. ܘܡܐ ܕܡܼܛܐ ܥܕܢܐ ܕܬܠܬ ܫ̈ܥܝܢ. ܩܘܡ ܩܕܡ ܨܠܝܒܐ ܘܟܲܢܫ ܚܘ̈ܫܒܝܟ ܡܢ ܣܘܟ̇ܠܐ ܕܩܪܝܢܐ ܕܩܪܝܬ. ܘܣܼܝܡ ܒܘܪ̈ܟܐ ܘܐܬܟܿܫܦ ܠܡܪܢ ܒܚܫܐ ܘܕܡ̈ܥܐ: ܕܢܼܬܠ ܠܟ ܩܠܝܕܐ ܕܣܘܟܠܐ ܕܙܡܝܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܛܘܒܢܐ ܕܘܝܕ. ܘܡܐ ܕܫ̇ܪܝܬ ܒܡܙܡܘܪܐ. ܠܐ ܬܣܬܪܗܒ ܥܠ ܣܓܝܐܘܬ ܡܙܡܘܪ̈ܐ. ܐܠܐ ܥܠ ܒܼܨܬܐ ܕܣܘܟܠܐ ܕܟܼܣܐ ܒܗܘܢ ܡܛܠ ܕܠܝܬ ܒܗ̇ ܒܗܕܐ ܚܘܣܪܢܐ ܇ ܐܢ ܒܚܕ ܦܬܓܡܐ ܕܡܙܡܘܪ̈ܐ ܢܬܥܢܐ ܗܘܢܗ ܕܐܢܫ ܫܒܥܐ ܠܝܠܝܐ ܘܐܝܡܡܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܐܡܼܝܪܐ ܠܐܒܗ̈ܝܢ
75 When you read the Gospel, O practicing brother, and you receive from it this blessing which sanctifies your soul through the Holy Spirit which is hidden in the living words of Our Lord Jesus Christ, read then the Apostle and the Acts until the third hour arrives, while accomplishing the rule of the metania for the Gospel. For how many times also by the reading of these (books) will you find this pearl full of life, of which I spoke above.
ܩܕܝܫ̈ܐ ܕܛܿܒ ܗܘ ܚܕ ܦܬܓܡܐ ܒܩܘܪ̈ܒܐ ܡܢ ܐܠܦ ܒܪܘܚܩܐ. ܚܙܝ ܐܘ̇ ܐܚܝ ܕܗܘܢܟ ܡܐ ܕܡܫܲܡܫ ܐܢܬ ܡܬܝܕܥܢܐܝܬ ܢܗܠܠ ܒܣܘܟܠܐ ܕܡܙܡܘܪ̈ܐ. ܘܦܘܡܟ ܡܬܪ̈ܓܫܢܐܝܬ. ܣܼܪܝܩ ܗܘ ܓܝܪ ܗܘܠܠܐ ܡܬܪ̈ܓܫܢܐ ܒܠܥܕ ܗܿܘ ܡܬܝܿܕܥܢܐ. ܒܪܡ ܗ̇ܘ ܡܬܪ̈ܓܫܢܐ ܗܼܘܝܘ ܥܠܼܬܐ ܕܗܿܘ ܡܬܝܕܥܢܐ. ܐܟܙܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܦܓܪܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܥܼܠܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܐ ܘܗܼܘ ܩܕܝܡ ܠܗܿ ܒܗܘ̇ܢܐ. ܗܟܢܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܙܘܡܪܐ ܡܬܪ̈ܓܫܢܐ ܥܠܬܐ ܕܗ̇ܘ ܗܘܠܠܐ ܡܬܝܕܥܢܐ. ܣܓ̈ܺܝܐܐ ܚܟ̈ܝܡܐ ܐܝܬ ܕܐܡܿܪܝܢ ܒܠܐ ܝܕܥܬܗܘܢ ܕܠܐ ܣܢܼܝܩܝܢܢ ܥܠ ܗ̇ܘ ܙܘܡܪܐ ܡܬܪ̈ܓܫܢܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܐܝܬ ܠܢ ܗܘܠܠܐ ܡܬܝܕܥܢܐ. ܘܒܠܒܐ ܡܙܡܿܪܝܢܢ ܘܠܐ ܒܿܥܝܢܢ ܙܘܡܪܐ
And when the third hour has arrived, stand before the cross and gather your thoughts from the meaning of the reading that you have just done. Bend the knee and beg Our Lord with passion and with tears, that He may grant you the key to the meaning of the psalms of the blessed David. And when you have begun a psalm, do not hasten to (read) a large number of psalms, but apply yourself to the search for the meaning that is hidden therein. For, there is no lack of gain in someone occupying his intellect on a single verse of a psalm for seven nights and seven days, since it has been said by our holy Fathers: Better a verse nearby than a thousand far away.
ܕܦܘܡܐ ܀ ܘܗܕܐ ܗܟܝܠ ܐܡܼܪܘ ܗ̇ܝ ܡܛܠ ܕܐܬܡܿܚܝܘ ܡܢ ܫܘܒܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܫܘܒܚܐ ܣܪܝܩܐ. ܘܙܿܕܩ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܠܚܟܝ̈ܡܐ ܠܐ ܕܢܣܬܟܿܠܘܢ ܕܐܢ ܐܝܬ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܠܢܦܫܐ ܒܠܥܕ ܡܢ ܦܓܪܐ ܇ ܐܘ̇ ܡܲܨܝܐ ܕܢܬܩܲܝܡ ܒܪܢܫܐ ܡܢ ܚܕܐ ܡܢܿܬܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ. ܘܐܢ ܗܕܐ ܠܐ ܡܲܨܝܐ ܐܦܠܐ ܗܢܼܘܢ ܕܘ̈ܝܐ ܡܗܠܠܝܢ ܒܗܿܘ ܩܘܕܫܐ ܡܬܝܕܥܢܐ: ܒܠܥܕ ܡܢ ܙܘܡܿܪܐ ܡܬܪ̈ܓܫܢܐ ܀
Watch therefore, O my brother, so that your intellect, when you celebrate (the office), may praise in spirit through the understanding of the psalms, while your mouth (praises) in a sensible way. For vain is a sensible praise, without that which is in spirit. Indeed, the sensible is the cause of the intellectual. Just as the body is a cause for the soul and precedes it in the intellect, so the sensible psalmody is the cause of the praise in spirit. There have been many wise men who say in their ignorance: "We have no need of this sensible psalmody, since we have the praise in spirit. We psalmody from the heart and we do not seek the psalmody of the mouth."
ܐܢܕܝܢ ܒܪܢܫܐ ܠܐ ܒܗܢܐ ܥܠܡܐ ܐܦܠܐ ܒܗ̇ܘ ܕܥܬܝܕ ܡܬܩܲܝܡ ܒܠܥܕ ܡܢ ܦܓܪܐ ܘܢܦܫܐ ܡܕܝܢ ܐܦܠܐ ܗܢ̣ܘܢ ܢܐܡܪܘܢ ܒܨܒܝܪܘܬܗܘܢ ܕܐܝܬ ܠܢ ܗܘܠܠܐ ܡܬܝܕܥܢܐ ܒܠܥܕ ܡܢ ܙܘܡܪܐ ܡܬܪ̈ܓܫܢܐ ܐܠܐ
That is therefore what they said, because they were struck by the overwhelming heat of the wind of vainglory. It was necessary for these senseless wise men to recognize, either that the soul possesses life without a body—then man could subsist with only one (of these two) components—; or else, if that is not possible, that then these wretches do not praise either by a praise in spirit without the sensible psalmody.


[p. 348] {#po.45.p348}
ܡܛܠ ܕܠܘ ܠܘܩܒܠ ܦܩܩܘܬܗܘܢ ܕܗܿܢܘܢ ܣܝܼܡܐ ܠܢ ܗܫܐ ܠܡܐܡܪ ܫ̇ܬܩܝܢܢ ܡܢ ܗܕܐ ܀
given that it is not against the chatter of these people that we have decided to speak now, we remain silent on this subject.
ܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܫܡܼܠܝܬ ܐܘ̇ ܐܚܐ ܥܕܢܐ ܕܬܠܬ ܐܝܟ ܥܰܝܕܐ ܘܩܢܘܢܐ ܕܣܼܝܡ ܠܐܒ̈ܗܝܢ ܣܼܒ ܨ ܟܬܒܐ ܕܐܒ̈ܗܬܐ ܘܩܼܪܺܝ ܥܕܡܐ ܠܥܕܢܐ ܕܦܠܓܗ ܕܝܘܡܐ. ܟܕ ܠܐ ܫܿܠܐ. ܡܕܥܟ ܡܢ ܗܪܓܐ ܕܣ̈ܘܟܠܐ ܡܬܝ̈ܕܥܢܐ. ܠܐ ܓܝܪ ܬܬܼܠ ܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܠܠܫܼܝܟ ܡܬܪ̈ܓܫܢܐ ܇ ܕܢ̣ܩܕܘܡ ܒܩܪܝܢܗ ܠܩܪܝܢܐ ܕܠܫܢܗ܀6ܕ ܕܡܕܥܟ ܡܬܝܕܥܢܐ. ܐܠܐ ܛܿܟܼܣ ܠܬܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ. ܕܒܚܕܐ ܫܲܘܝܘ ܢܪܕܘܢ. ܩܢܘܢܐ ܕܒܝܬ ܥܕܢܐ ܠܥܕܢܐ ܗܘ ܗܿܘ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܢܸܗܘܐ ܠܟ. ܟܕ ܣܿܐܡ ܐܢܬ ܠܗ ܠܟܬܒܐ ܘܩܿܐܡ ܐܢܬܼ ܘܢܿܦܠ ܐܢܬ ܩܕܡ ܨܠܝܒܐ. ܘܚܿܐܪ ܐܢܬ ܒܗ ܒܡܪܢ ܘܡܢܲܫܩ. ܐܢܬ ܪ̈ܓܠܘܗܝ ܩܕܝ̈ܫܬܐ ܒܝܕ ܐܬܐ ܕܨܠܝܒܗ. ܐܣܬܟܿܠ ܡܕܡ ܕܐܡܿܪ ܐܢܐ. ܕܠܘ ܥܠ ܗ̇ܝ ܚܼܙܬܐ ܫܒ̣ܝܚܬܐ ܐܡܿܪ ܐܢܐ ܗ̇ܝ ܕܠܐ ܣܢܝܩܐ ܥܠ ܦܘܪ̈ܣܐ ܘܥܠ ܝ̈ܕܥܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܝܚܝܕܝܐܝܬ܀܀ ܕܠܐ ܡܨܥܝܐ܀ ܕܢ݇ܚܐ ܒܠܒܐ ܕܡܩܲܒܠܢܝܗ̇. ܐܠܐ ܐܢܿܐ ܠܡܚ̈ܝܠܐ ܡܓܪܓ ܐܢܐ. ܕܒܝܕ ܚܿܝܪܐ ܕܒܨܠܝܒܗ ܕܡܪܢ ܢܫܬ̣ܘܘܢ ܠܗ̇ܝ ܚܙܬܐ ܫܒ̣ܝܚܬܐ ܕܠܝܬ ܠܗܿ ܕܡܘ ܀
76 Then, when you have accomplished, brother, the hour of terce according to the custom and the rule established by our Fathers, take the Book of the Fathers and read it until the hour of noon, without your spirit ceasing to meditate on the "intellectual" meanings. Do not therefore give your "sensible" tongue the opportunity to outpace in its reading the reading of the tongue of your "intellectual" understanding; but coordinate the two, so that they walk with the same step. Let the rule, for what is done between each hour, be for you the same as previously: you put down the book; you stand upright, you prostrate yourself before the cross; you fix your gaze on Our Lord, and you kiss his sacred feet at the sign of his cross. Understand what I say: I am not speaking of this glorious vision, that which requires neither modes nor knowledge: this one indeed appears in a singular manner, without intermediary, in the heart of the one who receives it; as for me, it is the weak that I exhort, so that through the vision of the cross of Our Lord, they may become worthy of this glorious vision which is without form. Manual labor
ܘܡܢ ܥܕܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܦܠܓܗ» ܠܬܫܥ" ܐܢ ܐܝܬ ܠܟ ܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܕܐܝ̈ܕܝܐ ܥܒܼܕܝܘܗܝ. ܟܕ ܩܢܘܢܐ ܕܡܛܘܢܝܐ° ܠܐ ܫܿܪܐ ܐܢܬ ܇ ܡܛܠ ܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܕܐܝ̈ܕܝܐ ܕܡܬܝ̇ܥܢ ܐܢܬ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܐܠܐ ܥܡܗ ܕܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܫܲܡܠܐ ܐܦ ܩܢܘܢܐ ܕܡܛܘܢܝܐ. ܐܒܗ̈ܝܢ ܓܝܪ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ?܀ ܇ ܠܥܒ̇ܕܐ܀܀ ܕܐܝ̈ܕܝܐ ܕܡܬܦܠܼܚ ܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܒܕܚܠܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܇ ܒܠܥܕ ܡܢ ܝܥܢܘܬܐ ܗܿܝ ܕܗܼܝ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܐܡܐ ܕܟܠܗܝܢ ܒܝܫ̈ܬܐ ܆ ܐܝܟ ܚܕܐ ܡܢ ܡܝܬܖ̈ܬܐ ܚܿܫܒܝܢ ܠܗ. ܐܦܢ ܕܝܢ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܡܢ ܐܒ̈ܗܬܐ ܐܡܲܪܘ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܒܼܥܐ ܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܕܐܝ̈ܕܝܐ ܠܘ ܟܕ ܫܿܪܝܢ ܠܢܡ̈ܘܣܐ ܕܗܢܘܢ ¢ ܕܡܢ ܩܕܡܝܗܘܢ ܚܿܣ :ܐܠܐ ܡܛܠ ܡܦܘܠܬܐ ܕܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܡܛܠ ܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܕܐܝ̈ܕܝܐ ܡܡܲܠܝܢ ܗܘܘ
77 From the hour of noon until the ninth hour (3 PM), if you have manual labor, do it, without breaking the rule of the metania for the benefit of a manual labor through which you would become greedy, but, at the same time as the work, accomplish also the rule of the metania. Our spiritual Fathers, indeed, count, as one of the virtues, the manual labor which is exercised in the cell in the fear of God without this greed which is the mother of all perversities. Even if other Fathers have said that manual labor is not necessary, they do not repudiate for all that the law of their predecessors; God forbid! But it is because of the fall of those who, through manual labor, satisfied the passion of their greed.
ܚܫܐ ܕܝܥܢܘܬܗܘܢ ܀ ܐܒ̈ܗܝܢ ܓܝܪ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܐ ܡܛܠ ܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܥܠܠܝܢ ܣܿܡܘܗܝ ܠܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܕܐܝ̈ܕܝܐ. ܐܟ ܩܢܘܢܐ ܚܪܝܩܐ ܘܚܫܒܘܗܝ ܐܝܟ ܚܕܐ ܡܢ ܡܝܬܪ̈ܬܐ. ܚܕܐ" ܡܛܠ ܕܡ̇ܩܠ ܝܘܩܪܐ ܕܩܘܛܥܐ¢¢ ܡܢܗ ܕܐܚܐ ܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܡܐ
As for our spiritual Fathers, it is for two reasons that they established manual labor as a fixed rule and considered it one of the virtues: firstly because, for the brother in his cell, it lightens the burden of acedia when it weighs upon him, just as when an angel appeared to the blessed Mar Anthony in the desert: while he was overwhelmed by thoughts of acedia and
ܕܥܠܘܗܝ. ܐܝܟ ܡܐ ܕܐܬܚܼܙܝ ܡܠܐܟܐ ܠܛܘܒܢܐ ܡܪܝ ܐܢܛܘܢܝܘܣ ܐܢܛܘܢܝܘܣ ܒܡܕܒܪܐ ܟܕ ܐܬܩ̇ܛܥܬ' ܠܗ ܡܢ ܚܘܫ̈ܒܐ ܕܩܘܛܥܐ
a voice came to him from heaven saying: "Anthony, go outside." Having gone out, he saw a man like himself who was weaving a basket. (The man) would set it down, then he would rise and pray; and again he would sit and work.
ܘܐܬܼܐ ܠܗ ܩܠܐ ܡܢ ܫܡܝܐ ܕܐܡܿܪ: ܕܐܢܛܘܢܝܣ" ܦܘܩ ܠܒܪ. ܘܢܦܼܩ ܘܚܼܙܐ ܓܒܪܐ ܒܕܡܘܬܗ ܟܕܓܲܕܠ ܣܝܪܐ ' ܘܣܿܐܡ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܘܩܿܐܡ ܗܘܐ ܘܡܿܨܠܐ ܘܬܘܒ ܝܬܰܒ ܗܘܐ ܘܦ̇ܛܠܚ ܘܐܡܪ ܠܗ ܗܿܘ ܡܿܢ
The one who had called to him said: "Do the same and you will live."
ܕܩܪܝܗܝ. ܕܗܟܢܐ ܥܒܕ ܘܚܿ̇ܝܐ ܐܢܬ ܀ 7% ܥܠܬܐ ܕܝܢ ܐܚܪܬܐ ܕܡܬܒܼܥܐ ܥܒ̇ܕܐ ܕܐܝ̈ܕܝܐ ܠܐܚܐ "ܗܕܐ ܗ݀ܝ'7 ܕܡܢ ܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܕܐܝ̈ܕܘܗܝ ܢܡܿܠܐ ܣܘܢܩܢܗܼ' ܐܦ ܢܬܼܠ ܡܢܗ7°¢ ܙܕܩܬ'7 ܠܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ. ܕܠܐ ܒܥܼܠܬ ܚܣܝܪܘܬܐ ܢܗܼܘܐ ܠܗ ܡܼܦܩܐ ܐܡܼܝܢܐ ܕܡܢ ܩܠܝܬܗ ܇ ܘܡܛܠܗܕܐ79 ܢܩܼܢܐ ܦܪܗܝܣܝܐ ”9 ܥܡ ܩܘܪ̈ܝܝܐ ܘܢܬܓܠܼܙ ܡܢ ܡܘܬܒܼܐ ܫܼܲܠܝܐ ܕܒܩܠܝܬ ܗ ܘܬܗܼܘܐ ܡܢ ܗܕܐ ܡܦܘܠܬܐ ܗܿܝ ܕܠܝܬ ܠܗ̇ ܩܝܿܡܐ ܀
78 The other reason why manual activity is required of the brother is this: it is so that he may be able, through the work of his hands, to provide for his needs, and also to give alms to others from what is his own, without having to constantly leave his cell under the pretext of indigence and, because of this, to create relationships of familiarity with the people of the village and thus be deprived of the silent stay in his cell: a fall from which one does not recover would result from this.
ܠܣ̈ܓܝܐܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܛܥܼܝ ܣܛܢܐ ܒܝܕ ܦܚܐ ܗܢܐ ܕܡ̇ܘܬܐ ܘܡܿܘܒܕܢܐ. ܕܡܛܠܗ̇ܝ ܕܠܐ ܦ̇ܠܚܝܢ ܒܐܝ̈ܕܝܗܘܢ ܝܗܒܼܘ ܐܬܪ̈ܐ "ܠܡܿܥܠܐ ܘܡܲܦܩܐ ܕܩܘܪ̈ܝܝܐ ܕܠܘܬܗܘܢ ܘܠܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܕܘܓܒܪ̈ܐ ܐܠܐ ܐܦ ܕܢܫ̈ܐ. ܘܡܛܠܗܕܐ6 ܐܬܓܠܼܙܘ ܡܢ ܒܘܣܿܡܐ ܕܡܿܘܬܒܐ ܫܠܝܐ" ܕܒܩܠܝܬܐ9,. ܗܿܘ ܕܨܵܐܪ ܛܘܦܣܐ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܕܥܬܝܕ ܘܐܬܓܚܟܘ ܡ̣ܢ ܫ̈ܐܕܐ ܇ ܘܐܫܬܥܿ̇ܒܕܘ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܐܪ̈ܒܥܐ ܚܫ̈ܐ ܒܝ̈ܫܐ. ܗ̇ܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܠܚܫܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ ܘܠܚܫܐ ܕܝܥܢܘܬܐ ܘܕܫܘܒܚܐ ܣܪܝܩܐ ܘܕܚܣܡܐ. ܗܠܝܢ ܒܫܘܡܠܝܐ ܐܫܬܟ̇ܚܘ ܠܘܬ ܗܢܘܢ ܕܫܒܼܩܘ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܕܣܼܡܘ ܐܒܗ̈ܝܢ °° ܡܢ ܪܘܚܐ
It is through this deadly and pernicious trap that Satan has deceived many (brothers); because they did not work with their hands, they gave occasion for villagers to come and go to them, not only men, but also women, and because of this they were deprived of the delights of the silent stay in the cell, which represents the figure of the world to come. They became the laughingstock of the demons and were subjected to the following four evil passions: the passion of fornication, the passion of greed, (that) of vainglory, and (that) of jealousy. These (passions) were found in their entirety in those who had neglected the precept established by our Fathers through the Holy Spirit and had followed the desires of their perverse heart.
ܩܕܝܫܐ. ܘܐܙܠܼܘ ܒܬܪ ܨܒܝ̈ܢܝ ܠܒܗܘܢ ܒܝܫܐ ܀ ܐܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܐܝܟ ܪ̈ܥܹܝܢܝ ܡܿܠܟ ܐܢܼܐ ܠܗܽܘ ܡܿܢ ܕܡܫܬܡܼܥ ܠܝܐܘ ܒܚܘܒܐ. ܕܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܕܐܝ̈ܕܝܐ ܕܡܢ ܥܕܢܐ ܕܦܠܓܗ ܠܬܫܥ ܐܝܟ ܩܢܘܢܐ ܚܪܝܩܐ ܢܬܬܣܹܝܡ ܠܟ. ܠܘ ܡܛܠ ܝܥܢܘܬܐ ܐܡ̇ܪ ܐܢܐ ܕܬܥܒܕ܀9 ܗܟܢܐ ܚܿܣ. ܐܠܐ ܕܬܬܚܲܣܟ ܡܢ ܒܝ̈ܫܬܐ ܗܿܠܝܢ ܕܡܢ ° ܠܥܠ ܐܡܿܪܬ ܕܡܿܘܠܕ”9° ܒܛܠܢܝܐ. ܠܘ ܓܝܪ ܠܓܡܝܪ̈ܐ ܣܺܐܡ ܐܢܐ ܢܡ=]ܘ̈ܣܐ ܗܢܘܢ ° ܕܠܥܠ ܡܢ ܢܛܘܪܬܗ ܕܢܡܘܣܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܦܘܠܚܢܗܘܢ. ܐܠܐ ܠܗܿܢܘܢ ܐ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܠܬܚܬ ܡ̣ܢ ܓܡܝܪܘܬܐ ܐܡܿܪ ܐܢܐ ܘܡ ܕܢܐܨܦܘܢ” ܕܩܢܘܢܝܐ ܗܢܐܼ ܗ̇ܿܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܠܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܘܢܒܡ[ܫܘ]ܚܬܐ ܕܦܓܪܢܘܬܐ ܘܕܢܦܫܢܘܬܐ3. ܗܿܠܝܢ ܡܪܢܐܝܬ܀ ܢܐܨܦܘܢ ܕܦܘܠܚܢܐ
79 As for me, in my opinion, I would advise anyone who would obey me with love that manual labor be imposed upon him from midday until the ninth hour, just as it is for you as a fixed rule. It is not for greed that I say to act thus; God forbid! But it is so that you may be preserved from these perversities that I cited above, and which idleness engenders. Indeed, it is not for the perfect that I establish these laws—those whose practice is above the observation of the law—but it is for those who are below perfection that I say they should pay attention to this rule, that is to say, those who are (still) at the somatic or psychic stage. Let these, then, truly devote themselves to manual


[p. 350] {#po.45.p350}


[p. 352] {#po.45.p352}
ܕܐܝ̈ܕܝܐ. ܗܢܘܢ ܕܝܢ ܕܐܬܡܿܛܝܘ ܠܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܢܘܬܐ ܠܐ ܣܢܼܝܩܝܢ ܥܠ ܗܕܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܐܦܢ ܡܬܩܼܲܝܡܐ ܚܫܼܚܬܐ ܕܣܘܢܩܢܗܘܢ ܡܢ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܠܐ ܡܬܢܟܝܢ ܡܢ ܗܕܐ ܀
labor. On the other hand, those who have reached the pneumatic stage have no need of this, for, even if the provision of what is necessary for their needs is done by others, it causes them no harm.

### Vespers, meal, compline
ܡܢ ܥܕܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܬܫܥ܀ ܠܪܡܫܐ ܢܐܨܦ ܕܣܝܒܪܬܗ. ܐܝܟ ܕܐܡܼܪܘ ܐܒܗ̈ܝܢ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ. ܟܕ ܥܘܗܕܢܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܒܠܒܗ ܘܩܢܘܢܝܐ ܕܡܛܘܢܝܐ ܗ̇ܘ ܕܡܢ ܠܥܠ ܐܡܼܪܝܢ. ܘܡܐ ܕܡ̇ܿܛܝ ܥܕܢܐ ܕܪ̈ܡܫܐ. ܩܘܡ ܒܚܝܼܨܘܬܐ ܕܠܒܐ ܘܒܥܝܼܪܘܬܐ ܕܚܘܫ̈ܒܐ. ܘܐܣܼܩ ܩܘܒܠܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܇ ܥܠ ܐܦ̈ܝ ܛܝܒܘܬܗ ܕܣܼܥܪ ܠܘܬܟ. ܡܬܚܼܐ ܕܟܠܗ ܝܘܡܐ ܀
80 From the ninth hour until evening, let (the exercitant) occupy himself with his food, as our spiritual Fathers have said, while having the remembrance of God in his heart, and (observing) the rule of the prostration of which we spoke above. And when the hour of vespers arrives, rise, with energy in your heart and vigilance in your thoughts, and offer up thanksgiving to God for the goodness He has shown you all day long.
ܘܝܬܝܪ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܥܕܢ̈ܝܢ ܒܗܢܐ ܥܕܢܐ ܬܗܼܘܐ ܠܟ ܙܗܝܪܘܬܐ ܥܠ ܚܘܫܒܝܓܟܪ. ܡܛܠ ܕܝܬܝܪ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܕܒ̈ܚܐ ܕܒܢܡܘܣܐ ܝܲܩܕܐ ܫܼܲܠܡܐ ܕܪ̈ܡܫܐ ܡܩܲܒܠ ܗܘܐ ܥܠ ܐܠܗܐ. ܐܝܟ ܕܐܦ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܕܘܝܕ ܐܡܼܪ, ܕܩܘܪ̈ܒܢܐ ܕܐܝ̈ܕܝ ܐܝܟ ܩܘܪ̈ܒܢܗ ܕܪܡܫܐ ܢܬܩܲܒܠ ܩܕܡܝܟ. ܡܛܠ ܓܝܪ ܕܚܠܬܗ ܕܠܠܝܐ ܇ ܦܪܝܼܫ ܗܘܐ ܗܢܐ ܩܘܪ̈ܒܢܐ ܒܢܡܘܣܐ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܩܘܪ̈ܒܢܐ ܕܡܬܩܲܪܒܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܩܕܡ ܐܠܗܐ. ܒܗ̇ܝ ܕܗܼܘ ܢܿܛܪ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܠܥܡܐ ܡܢ ܕܚܠܬܐ ܕܒܥܠܕܒ̈ܒܐ ܟܠܗ ܠܠܝܐ. ܐܦ ܐܢܬ ܗܟܝܠ ܐܘܿ̇ ܐܚܘ ܦܿܠܘܚܐ ܐܬܟ̇ܬܫ ܥܡ ܚܘܫܒܝܓܪ. ܕܒܕܟܝܘܬܐ ܬܩܲܪܒ ܩܘܪܪܒܢܐ ܗܢܐ ܩܕܡ ܐܠܗܐ. ܕܡܢܗ ܠ̈ܒܫ ܐܢܬ ܙܝܢܐ ܠܐ ܡܬܚܲܣܢܢܐ
At this hour more than any other, let there be vigilance on your part over your thoughts, because the evening holocaust, more than all the sacrifices (imposed) by the Law, is accepted by God, as the blessed David also said: Let the offering of my hands be accepted before you as the evening offering. It is indeed because of the fear of the night that this offering was distinguished by the Law from all the (others) that were offered before God, because it is He who, throughout the night, protected the people against the fear of enemies. You too, therefore, O our brother exercitant, struggle with your thoughts in order to offer this offering in purity before God, so that thanks to it, you may clothe yourself in an invincible armor all night long against the "spiritual" enemies.
ܠܘܩܒܠ ܒܥܠܕܒ̈ܒܐ ܡܬܝ̈ܕܥܢܐ ܟܠܗ ܠܠܝܐ ܀ ܘܡܐ ܕܫܡܼܫܬ ܪܕܡܫܐ ܐܝܟ ܥܝܕܐ ܇ ܣܹܝܡ ܦܬܘܪܐ ܠܘܩܒܠ ܨܠܝܒܐ. ܘܐܣܿܩ ܩܘܒܠܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܠܐܠܗܐ. ܟܕ ܦܓܪܟ ܢܿܣܒ ܣܝܒܪܬܐ ܡܬܪ̈ܓܫܢܝܬܐ ܆ ܘܢܦܫܟ ܡܣܬܝܒܪܐ ܣܝܒܪܬܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܬܐ. ܚܼܙܝ ܕܠܡܐ ܒܥܠܬ ܣܝܒܪܬܐ ܡܬܪ̈ܓܫܢܝܬܐ ܬܬܓܠܙ ܢܦܫܟ ܡ̣ܢ ܣܝܒܪܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܘܢܗܼܘܐ ܟܬܘܪܟ ܐܝܟ ܕܚܝ̈ܘܬܐ ܇ ܕܠܐ ܣܿܠܩ ܡܢܗ ܩܘܒܠܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܠܐܠܗܐ. ܒܪܢܫܐ ܕܦܓܪܗ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܡܣܬܝܿܒܪ ܟܕ ܢܦܫܗ ܓܠܝܼܙܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܣܝܒܪܬܐ ܪܘܚܢܝܬܐ ܠܐ ܦܪܝܼܫ ܡܸܢ ܒܥܝܪܐ. ܣܝܒܪܬܟ ܗܟܝܠ ܒܡܙܠܗܙܘܬܐ ܬܗܼܘܐ ܘܠܐ ܕܡܲܬܩܢܐ ܒܛ̈ܥܘܡܐ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܐ. ܡܐ ܗܟܝܠ ܕܝ̇ܬܒ ܐܢܬ ܥܠܼ ܦܬܘܪܐ ܥܼܗܕ ܠܒܥܠܕܒܿܒ̈ܐ ܕܠܠܝܐ ܘܡܬܟܠܐ ܚܘܫܒܟ ܡ̣ܢ ܝܬܝܪ̈ܬܐ. ܠܐ ܗܟܝܠ ܬܬܼܠ ܠܗ ܠܦܓܪܟ ܣܝܒܪܬܐ ܝܬܝܪܬܐ. ܐܦܠܐ ܣܘܢܩܢܗ ܀
81 And when you have celebrated vespers according to custom, place the table in front of the cross, and offer up thanksgiving to God, your body taking bodily nourishment and your soul feeding on spiritual nourishment. Watch that through bodily nourishment your soul is not deprived of spiritual nourishment, and that your table does not become like that of animals from which no thanksgiving rises to God. The man whose body alone is nourished, while his soul is deprived of spiritual nourishment, does not distinguish himself from the brute. Therefore, let your repast be light and not contain many foods. When, therefore, you sit at the table, remember the enemies of the night, and your thought will be restrained from excesses. Do not, therefore, give your body overabundant food, not even what it needs.


[p. 354] {#po.45.p354}
ܡܛܠ ܕܐܡ̇ܪܝܢ ܐܒܗ̈ܬܐ. ܕܠܝܬ ܐܬܘܢܐ ܒܒܠܝܬܐ ܕܒܝܼܫܐ ܠܢ ܡܢ ܦܓܪܐ ܡܐ ܕܫ̇ܩܠ ܣܝܒܪܬܐ ܝܬܝܪܬܐ. ܐܠܐ ܟܿܒ̣ܫܝܗܝ ܒܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ ܫܚܝܼܡܬܐ ܘܒܫܩܘܬܐ ܕܡܝ̈ܐ. ܕܡܐ ܕܡܫܪܝܢ ܒܒ̈ܠܝܐ ܠܡܫܓܪ ܢܘܪ̈ܖܐ ܕܐܬܘܢܐ ܕܦܓܪܐ ܒܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܚ̈ܫܐ ܟܝ̈ܢܝܐ ܢܩܘܡ ܡܛܠ ܥܝܪܘܬܗ ܕܨܘܡܐ ܚܢܢܝܐ ܘܥܙܖܪܝܐ ܘܡܝܟܐܝܠ ܗܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܗܘܢܐ ܘܡܕܥܐ ܘܪ̈ܥܝܢܐ ܕܪ̈ܡܝܢ ܒܐܬܘܢܐ ܕܦܓܪܐ. ܘܡܫܬܓܿܪܝܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܚܫ̈ܐ ܟܝ̈ܢܝܐ. ܘܢܨܿܠܘܢ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܕܐܒܗ̈ܝܗܘܢ. ܕܢܪܘܣ ܛܐܠܐ ܕܒܣܝܼܡܘܬܗ ܗ̇ܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܚܲܝܠܐ ܕܥܘܕܪ̈ܢܐ ܐܠܺܗܝܳܐ ܢܿܓܢ ܥܠ ܦܓܪܐ ܘܢܦܫܐ ܘܬܕܥܲܟ ܢܘܪܐ ܕܚܫ̈ܐ ܟܝܢ̈ܝܐ. ܘܒܒ̈ܠܝܐ ܢܬܛܲܠܩܘܢ. ܘܫܕܪܟ ܘܡܫܢܢ ܝܐ ܘܥܒܕܢܓܘ ܢܙܠܓ ܒܗܘܢ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܕܪ̈ܚܡܼܬܗ ܕܦܪܘܩܢ ܘܢܬܡܼܠܘܢ ܚܕܘܬܐ ܘܪ̈ܘܙܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܠܐ ܫܒ̣ܩܘ ܣܓܕܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܕܐܒ̈ܗܝܗܘܢ ܘܣܓܕܘ ܠܨܠܡܐ ܥܒܕ ܢܒܘܟܕܢܨܪ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܪ̈ܟܘܢܗ̇ ܕܒܝܫܬܐ. ܘܡܐ ܕܫܩ̣ܠܬ ܣܝܒܪܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܥܝ̇ܕܐ ܫܲܡܠܐ ܩܢܘܢܐ ܕܒܬܪ ܦܬܘܪܪܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܕܡ ܕܣܼܝܡ ܠܐܒ̈ܗܝܢ ܩܕܝܫܐ. ܩܢܘܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܩܪܝܢܝܐ ܕܡܛܘܢܝܐ ܘܡܢ ܨܦܪܐ ܘܥܕܡܐ ܠܣܘܒܿܥܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܐ ܕܡܢ ܠܥܠ ܐܡܪܢ
82 Since the Fathers say: "There is no furnace in Babylon more pernicious for us than the body when it takes too much food," submit it, however, (by using) simple food and drinking water, so that, when the Babylonians begin to light the fire of the furnace of the body by means of the fire of natural passions, there may then rise, thanks to the vigilance of fasting, Ananias, Azarias, and Michael—that is to say, the intellect, the spirit, and the thought—who lie in the furnace of the body and are burned by natural passions, and that they may pray to the God of their Fathers to spread the dew of His delights; it is then that the strength of divine help will cover the body and the soul and extinguish the fire of natural passions. The Babylonians will be put to flight; in Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego will be lit the holy light of the love of our Savior, and they will be filled with joy and gladness, because they will not have abandoned the worship of the God of their Fathers, nor adored the idol made by Nebuchadnezzar who is the archon of evil. And when you have taken your food as usual, perform the rule (of prayer) "after the table," according to what is prescribed by our holy Fathers. Let the rule of reading and prostration, from matins until compline, be as we said above. Vigil and sleep
ܢܗܘܐ ܀ ܠܠܝܐ ܕܝܢ ܟܠܗ ܠܫܗܪ̈ܐ ܥܝܪܐ ܕܦܓܪܐ ܘܕܢܦܫܐ ܐܦܩܼܝܗܝ ܟܕ ܡܦܠܓ ܐܢܬ ܠܗ ܠܬ݀ܠܬ ܡ̈ܢܘܢ. ܚܕܐ ܕܡܙܡܘܪ̈ܐ ܘܕܣܝܿܡ ܒܘܪ̈ܟܐ. ܘܐܚܪܬܐ ܠܩܪܝܢܐ. ܘܕܬܠܬ ܠܗܪ̈ܓܐ ܕܪ̈ܥܝܢܐ. ܘܠܩ̈ܠܐ ܚ̈ܠܝܐ ܕܥܘ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܘܡܕܪ̈ܫܐ ܕܣܝܼܡܝܢ ܠܪܘܚܐ ܩܕܝܫܐ. ܘܢܗܘܘܢ ܠܟܐ ܠܦܘܪ̈ܓܝܐ ܕܟܠܗ ܡܬܚܐ ܐܪ̈ܝܟܐ ܕܠܠܝܐ. ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܕܫ̇ܗܪ̈ ܡ̣ܢ ܡܫܐ ܠܨܦܪܐ ܒܥܝܪܘܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܥܝܢܐ ܆ ܕܘܡܿܝܐ ܗ̇ܘ ܕܡ̈ܠܐܟܝ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܇ ܗܢܘܢ ܕܐܡܝܢܐܝܬ ܩܘܕܫܐ ܬܠܝܬܝܐ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܚܕܬܐ ܡܩܲܕܫܝܢ܀
83 As for the night, pass it entirely in a vigilant vigil of body and soul, dividing it into three parts: the first in psalms and kneelings, the second in readings, and the third in a meditation of the spirit and in the sweet melodies of the antiphons and hymns composed by the Holy Spirit: they will be for you a rejoicing for the whole long duration of the night. The monk who keeps vigil from evening until morning in the vigilance of the understanding is like the angels of light, those who incessantly sing the mystery of the Trisagion of the new world.
ܛܘܒܘܗܝ ܠܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܕܐܫܬܼܘܝ ܠܗܿܢܘܢ܀܀ ܛܘ̈ܒܐ ܠܐ ܡܬܡ̈ܠܠܝܐ ܇ ܕܒܫܗܖܐ ܕܠܝ̈ܠܘܬܐ ܡܬܝܿܗܒܝܢ ܠܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܠܗܕܐ ܬܐܓܘܖ̈ܬܐ ܙܡܲܢܘ ܢܦܫܘܢ. ܠܝܬ ܓܝܪ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܕܥ̇ܡܠ ܠܠܝܐ ܟܠܗ ܒܫܗܪ̈ܐ ܇ ܕܠܘ ܚܘܫ̈ܒ̈ܘܗܝ ܒܐܝܡܡܐ ܒܛܒ̈ܬܗ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܚܕܬܐ ܗܿܪ̈ܓܝܢ. ܫܗܪ̈ܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܠܝ̈ܠܘܬܐ.
Blessed is the monk who has been judged worthy of these ineffable goods which are granted during the vigil of the nights to those who have prepared themselves for this commerce. There is, indeed, no monk who, having labored all night in vigils, does not have, during the day, his thoughts occupied with the goods of the


[p. 356] {#po.45.p356}
ܠܚܫ̈ܝ ܦܓܪܐ ܡܫܲܦܠ. ܘܠܙܘ̈ܥܝ ܢܦܫܐ ܡܩܲܛܢ. ܘܠܬܘ̈ܢܘܗܝ ܓܘܝ̈ܐ ܕܗܘܢܐ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܡܿܠܐ. ܘܫܐܕܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ ܘܕܪ̈ܘܓܙܐ ܬܚܝܼܬ ܪ̈ܓܠܘܗܝ ܕܗܘܢܐ ܣܿܐܡ. ܟܠ ܓܝܪ ܕܒܠܠܝܐ ܫܗܖܿܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܗ ܒܐܝܡܡܐ ܠܝܬ ܠܗ ܩܪ̈ܒܐ܀ ܥܡ ܫ̈ܐܕܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܙܟܼܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܒܫܗܪܗ ܥܝܪܐ܀ ܕܒܠܠܝܐ. ܘܗܿܘ ܕܒܠܠܝܐ ܠܐ ܫ̇ܗܖ ܒܐܝܡܡܐ ܩܪܒܐ ܘܬܟܬܘܫܐ ܠܫܐܕܐ ܕܦܼܗܝܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܗ ܥܡܗ.
new world. The fact of keeping vigil during the nights, indeed, weakens the passions of the body, refines the movements of the soul, fills with light the interior folds of the intellect, and puts under the feet of the intellect the demon of fornication and (that) of anger. For whoever keeps vigil at night does not have, during the day, battles to wage against the demons, because he has conquered them by his vigilant vigil of the night. He who does not keep vigil at night has, during the day, war and struggle with the demon of wandering.
ܡܪܢ ܓܝܪ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܇ ܡܛܠ ܕܗܼܘ ܗܘܼܐ ܠܢ ܕܘܡܝܐ ܒܟܠܡܕܡ ܐܝܟ ܡܠܬ ܫܠܝܚܐ ܒܠܝ̈ܠܘܬܐ ܡܿܓܗ ܗܘܐ ܒܨܠܘܬܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܚܠܦ ܦܘܪܩܢܗ ܕܓܢܣܢ. ܘܥܠ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܦܘܠܘܣ ܫܠܝܚܐ ܟܬܝܼܒ. ܕܝܿܬܒ ܗ̇ܘܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܪܡܫܐ ܠܨܦܪܐ ܘܡܡܿܠܠ ܗܘܐ ܡܠܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܥܡ ܡܗܝ̈ܡܢܐ. ܘܥܠ ܗܿܘ ܐܬܠܝܛܐ ܙܖܝܙܐ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܐܢܛܢܝܘܣ ܐܡܼܝܪ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܕܠܝܠܘܬܐ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܢܼܬܐ ܡܿܓܗ ܗܘܐ. ܘܥܠ ܐܒܐ ܐܪܿܣܢܝܣ ܐܡܼܪ ܦܠܕܝܣ. ܕܫܿܒܩ ܗܘܐ ܠܫܡܼܫܐ ܡܢ ܒܣܼܬܪܗ ܘܡܬܿܚ ܗܘܐ ܐܝ̈ܕܘܗܝ ܠܫܡܝܐ ܟܕ ܡܨܲܠܐ
84 Christ Our Lord, indeed, because He is like us in all things, according to the word of the apostle, spent entire nights in prayer with God for the salvation of our race. And, concerning the blessed apostle Paul, it is written that he remained from evening until morning conversing on the word of God with the faithful. Concerning that zealous athlete who was the blessed Anthony, it is said that he spent many nights without sleep. About Abba Arsenius, Palladius says that he would let the sun (set) behind him and that he would stretch his hands toward heaven in prayer, until the sun shone upon his face.
ܥܕܡܐ ܕܕܢܿܚ ܗܘܐ ܫܡܼܫܐ ܒܐܦ̈ܘܗܝ ܀ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܓܝܪ ܐܒܗ̈ܬܐ ܩܕܝ̈ܫܐ ܒܥܡܼܠܐ ܕܫܗܖ̈ܐ ܫܲܠܚܘܗܝ ܠܒܪܢܫܐ ܥܬܝܩܐ ܘܠܒܫܘ ܠܗ̇ܘ ܚܕܬܐ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܟܠܗ ܒܟܠܗ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܫܒܝ̣ܼܚܐ ܕܚܼܙܬܗ ܕܦܪܘܩܢ. ܘܛܘܒܢܐ ܕܘܝܕ ܟܕ ܡܛܠ ܚܛܝܼܬܗ ܡܬܟ̇ܫܦ ܗܘܼܐ ܒܠܝ̈ܠܘܬܐ ܡܨܒܿܥ ܗܘܐ ܬܫܘܝܼܬܗ ܒܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܒܕܗܼܘ ܐܡܼܪ. ܕܨܒܿܥܬ ܒܟܠ ܠܲܠܝܐ ܥܪܣܝ ܘܒܕܡ̈ܥܬܝ ܬܫܘܝܬܝ ܐܡܣܼܝܬ ܀
It is, indeed, through the labor of vigils that all the holy Fathers stripped off the old man and put on the new, who is, in his totality, glorious light of the vision of our Savior. Thus the blessed David, when he supplicated for his sin during the nights, soaked his bed with tears as he said: Every night I bathed my bed with my tears, with my weeping I watered my couch. Advice and examples
ܚܙܝ ܐܘ̇ ܐܚܐ ܦܿܠܘܚܐ ܇ ܠܐ ܬܒܲܛܠ܀ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܫܒܥ ܬܫܡ̈ܫܬܐ ܕܣܝ̈ܡܢ ܠܐܒ̈ܗܝܢ ܩܕܝ̈ܫܐ ܡܛܠ ܢܘ̇ܛܪܐ ܕܚ̈ܝܝܢ ܇ ܘܬܫܬܠܡ ܒܐܝ̈ܕܝ ܫܐ̈ܕܐ. ܣܛܪ ܡܸܢ ܥܠܼܬܐ ܕܟܘܪ̈ܗܢܐ. ܕܠܐ ܣܼܝܡܐ ܬܚܝܼܬ ܩܢܘܢܐ. ܐܡܝܼܢܘܬܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܡܘܬܒܐ ܕܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܫܘܡܠܝܐ ܕܥ̈ܕܢܐ ܬܒ̇ܥܐ. ܗ̇ܘ ܓܝܪ ܕܡܿܗܡܐ ܒܬܫܡܫܬܐ ܕܥ̈ܕܢܐ ܣܼܪܝܩ ܗܘ ܡܘܬܒܗ ܕܒܩܠܝܬܐ. ܘܠܚ̈ܫܘܗܝ ܦܿܠܚ ܒܐܝܡܡܐ ܘܒܠܠܝܐ܀. ܗܿܘ ܓܝܪ ܕܝܨܲܦ ܕܫܘܡܠܝܐ ܕܬܫܡܫܬܗ ܝܨܦ ܕܝܠܗ ܐܠܗܐ ܘܡܢܲܛܪ ܠܗ ܡܢ ܫ̈ܐܕܐ. ܘܝܗܿܒ ܠܗ ܒܘܣܿܡ ܛܒ̈ܬܐ ܕܢܝܿܚܐ ܕܩܠܝܬܐ ܀
85 See then, O brother exercitant, do not neglect any of these seven offices established by our holy Fathers for the safeguard of our life—except for reason of illness, which is not subject to the rule—; otherwise, you will be delivered into the hands of the demons. For the perseverance of the stay in the cell requires the fulfillment of the hours. He, indeed, who neglects the office of the hours, his stay in the cell is vain; and it is for his passions that he exercises himself day and night. He, on the other hand, who takes care to fulfill his office, God takes care of him, preserves him from the demons, and grants him the delights of the


[p. 358] {#po.45.p358}
ܚܙܝ ܐܚܝ ܕܠܡܐ ܡܛܠ ܣܓܝܐܘܬ ܡܙܡܘܖ̈ܐ ܬܬܩܲ̇ܛܥ ܠܟ ܡܢ ܗܕܐ. ܘܬܒܲܛܠ ܡܢܗ. ܠܘ ܓܝܪ ܚܟܡܬܐ ܗܝ ܗܕܐ ܕܠܢܡ̈ܘܣܐ ܥܒ̈ܕܢܝܐ ܬܫܬܥ̇ܒܕ. ܘܚܪܝܩܘܬܐ ܕܣܓܝܐܘܬܐ ܕܡܙܡܘܪ̈ܐ ܬܣܹܝܡ ܥܠܝܟ ܇ ܘܡܢ ܥܠܬ ܝܘܩܪܐ ܣܓܝܐܐ ܬܬܩܲܛܥ ܘܬܗܡܐ ܡܢ ܬܫܡ̈ܫܬܝܟ. ܐܠܐ ܝܗܝܼܒܐ ܠܟ ܚܐܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܡܢ ܡܦܣܢܘܬܐ ܕܪܘܚܐ ܕܩܘܕܫܐ: ܥܕܡܐ ܠܚܕ ܗܘܠܠܐ ܘܥܕܡܐ ܠܚܕܐ ܡܪܡܝܬܐ. ܒܠܚܘܕ ܠܐ ܬܫܒܼܘܩ ܥܕܢ̈ܝܟ. ܘܐܢܗܘ ܕܥܲܪܝ ܥܠܝܟ ܫܐܕܐ ܕܩܘܛܟܐ ܘܠܐ ܫܿܒܩ ܠܟ ܠܡܩܡ. ܥܶܨܝ ܢܦܫܟ ܡܛܠ ܐܠܗܐ܆ ܘܩܘܡ ܥܠ ܕ̈ܓܠܝܟ ܘܐܡܼܪ. ܫܒܲܚܘ ܠܡܪܝܐ ܟܠܟܘܢ ܥܡ̈ܡܐ ܘܫܲܒܚܘܼܗܝ ܥܲܠܡ. ܘܐܡܼܪ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܘܥܒܕ ܡܛܘܢܝܐ ܩܕܡ ܨܠܝܒܐ. ܘܗܐ ܡܩܲܒܠܐ
benefits of the rest of the cell. Watch, my brother, that, as a result of an excess of psalms, you do not experience acedia, and that you let your hours drop. For it is not wisdom to subject yourself to the laws of a servant and to take upon yourself to abandon too great a number of psalms: because of this too great weight you would experience acedia and neglect your offices. But, by the permission of the Holy Spirit, you have the freedom to impose upon yourself only one hymn and only one marmita; do not neglect your hours completely, and if the demon of acedia arises against you and prevents you from rising, constrain yourself for God, stand on your feet and say: Praise the Lord all peoples, praise Him, forever; and say: Holy... Then, make a prostration before the cross, and this is your acceptable sacrifice before God.
ܕܒܚܬܟ ܩܕܡ ܐܠܗܐ ܀ ܚܘܒܼܬܐ ܓܝܪ ܣܝܼܡܐ ܥܠܝܟ. ܘܐܢ ܝܿܗܒ ܐܢܬ ܕܝܢܪܐ ܡܩܲܒܠܝܢ ܡܢܟ. ܘܐܢ ܝܗ̇ܿܒ ܐܢܬ ܐܣܬܝܪܐ ܠܡܪܐ ܚܘܒܐ. ܢܿܣܒ ܠܗܿ ܡܢܓܢ ܒܚܕܘܬܐ. ܘܐܢ ܝܿܗܒ ܐܢܬ ܠܗ ܫܡܘܢܐ ܒܥܕܢܗ ܚ̈ܕܐ ܒܗ ܐܟ ܕܝܢܪܐ ܡܫܲܠܡܢܐ, ܒܠܚܘܕ ܡܦܼܪܥ ܦܪܘܥ. ܠܗ ܡܢ ܚܘܒܬܗ ܘܗܼܘ ܠܐ ܪܐܢ ܥܡܟ ܒܣܓܝܐܐ ܘܒܒܼܨܝܪܐ. ܒܪܡ ܟܠ ܐܡܬܝ ܕܡܗܡܝܢܘܬܐ ܠܐ ܡܫܟܚ ܒܟ ܐܓܪܐ ܚܕ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ
86 Indeed, a debt is incumbent upon you: If you give a denarius, it will be accepted from you; if you give a stater to the creditor, he will receive it from you with joy; and if you give him an obol at its due time, he will rejoice over it as if it were a whole denarius. Only pay him truly a part of what you owe him, and, for his part, he will not bring a lawsuit against you, (whether you give him) much or little. In reality, each time he does not find negligence in you, there will be (as) a wage for him.
ܠܘܬܗ ܀ ܘܬܚܘܝܬܐ ܕܗܕܐ ܚܲܘܝ ܠܢ ܕܘܡܝܐ ܒܢܦܫܗ. ܕܒܚܕܐ ܫܦܝܥ̣ܘܬܐ ܕܚܘܒܐ ܕܠܐ ܐܫܬܟܼܚ ܒܗ̇ ܡܐܣܒ ܒܐܦ̈ܐ ܝܗܼܒ ܐܓܪܐ ܕܦܘܼܠܚܢ ܟܪܡܗ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܠܗܿܘ ܕܒܚܕܥܣܪ̈ܐ ܫ̈ܥܝܢ ܥ̣ܠ ܠܟܪܡܗ. ܐܝܟ ܗܘ ܕܡܢ ܦܪܐ ܠܪܡܫܐ ܦܠܼܲܚ ܒܗ ܘܫܩܼܠ ܝܘܩܪܗ ܕܝܘܡܐ ܘܚ̣ܘܡܹܗ. ܘܡܛܠ ܐ ܕܐܦܢ ܒܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܒܨܝܼܪܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܚܕ ܡܢ ܚܕ ܐܠܐ ܒܡܛܝܒܼܘܬ ܨܒܝܢܐ ܫܘܝܢ ܗܘܘ. ܘܗܼܘ ܒܬܪ̈ܝܨܘܬ ܕܝܢܼܗ ܟܐܢܐ ܝܗܒ ܟܣܦܗ ܠܦܘܼܬ ܡܛܝܒܘܬ ܨܒܝܢܐ. ܠܘ ܠܦܘܬ ܦܘܠܚܢܐ.
As proof of this, (Christ) showed Himself as a model to us, He who, through a unique superabundance of love in which there is no respect of persons, gave the wage for the work in His holy Vineyard to him who entered His vineyard at the eleventh hour, just as to him who worked there from morning until evening and who bore the burden of the day and the heat. For, although, as far as the work is concerned, one is inferior to the other, however, by good will, they were equal. And He, in the righteousness of His just judgment, gave His money according to the good will, and not according to the work.
.ܢ ܡܛܠ ܕܐܝܬ ܕܦܿܠܚ ܡܢ ܨܦܪܐ܀ ܠܪܡܫܐ ܘܢܝܼܫܐ ܕܦܘܠܚܢܗܼ ܠܘ ܠܨܒܝܢܗ ܕܡܪܐ ܟܪܡܐ ܚܢܐ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܗܘ ܛܪܕ ܐܢܘܢ ܡܢ ܒܝܬܝܘܬܗ ܟܕ ܐܡܿܪ. ܕܡܿܢܐ ܩ̇ܪܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܠܝ ܡܪܝ ܡܪܝ ܘܡܕܡ ܕܐܡܿܪ ܐܢܐ ܠܐ ܥ̇ܿܒܕܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܘܕܡ̇ܝ ܐܢܘܢ ܬܘܒ ܠܓܒܪܐ ܣܟܼܠܐ. ܕܒܼܢܐ
For there are those who work from morning until evening, but the goal of (their) work does not aim to give satisfaction to the will of the Master of the vineyard; (such as) those whom He drove from His company by saying: Why
ܒܝܬܗ ܥܠ ܚܠܐ ܀
do you call me: Lord, Lord, and do not do anything of what I


[p. 360] {#po.45.p360}
ܘܝܨܦ ܗܟܝܠ ܐܘ̇ ܐܚܐ ܕܐܡܝܢܘܬܐ ܒܙܥܘܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܗܡܝܢܘܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܒܣܓܝܘܬܐ ܕܠܘ ܒܢܝܼܫܐ ܬܪ̈ܝܨܐ ܗܘܝܐ. ܗܐ ܡܫܬܥܢܐ ܠܟ ܬܫܥܝܬܐ ܐܝܿܕܐ ܕܬܢܝ ܠܝ ܐܢܫ. ܡܢ ܝܕܘ̈ܥܬܢܐ. ܗܺܝ ܕܒܢܣܼܝܢܐ ܒܩܢܘܡܗ ܫܩܼܠܗ̇ ܘܠܐ ܗܘܐ ܡܢ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܩܼܲܒܠܗ̇ܿ. ܐܡܿܪ ܗܘܐ ܓܝܪ ܗܟܢܐ ܕܒܚܕ ܡܢ ܙܒ̈ܢܝܢ ܟܕ ܝܿܬܒ ܐܢܐ ܒܫܼܠܝܐ ܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܩܼܡ ܥܠܝ ܫܐܕܐ ܗܢܐ ܠܝܼܛܐ ܕܡܐܝܢܘܬܐ. ܘܠܐ ܫܒܿܩ ܗܘܐ ܠܝ ܕܡܫܡܫ ܗܘܝܬ ܐܠܐ ܠܐ ܒܠܠܝܐ ܘܠܐ ܒܐܝܡܡܐ. ܘܟܕ ܫܒܘܥܐ ܚܕ ܕܝܘ̈ܡܬܐ ܪܡܐ ܗܘܝܬ ܥܠ ܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܡܢ ܝܘܩܪܐ ܣܓܝܐܐ ܕܣܼܡܝܟ ܗܘܐ ܥܠܝ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܐܦܠܐ ܥܘܗܕܢܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܫܿܒܩ ܗܘܐ ܕܢܿܣܩ ܥܠ ܠܒܝ ܘܡܕܡ ܠܐ ܦܠܚ ܗܘܝܬ ܐܠܐ ܐܢ ܚܘܫ̈ܒܐ ܣܪ̈ܝܩܐ ܘܢܕܝ̈ܕܐ. ܘܟܕ ܙܒܢܝܐ ܗܢܐ ܟܠܗ ܩܿܡܬ ܒܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܗܿܘ ܬܥܝܼܫܐ ܦܣܿܩܬ ܣܒܪܐ ܥܠ ܚܳܝܝ. ܘܟܕ ܐܡܿܪ ܗܘܝܬ ܠܗ ܠܚܘܫܒܝ ܕܟܕ ܐܙܿܠ ܐܢܐ ܠܥܠܡܐ ܡܥܕܪܐ ܠܝ ܝܬܝܪ ܡܢ ܗܿܝ ܕܫܩܼܝܠ ܐܢܐ ܐܣܟܝܡܐ ܕܝܚ̈ܝܕܝܐ ܘܡܕܡ ܠܐ ܣܿܥܪ
say? And He compared them again to the foolish man who built his house on the sand. 87 Take care, therefore, O brother, to persevere in small things without negligence, and not to do many things without a right intention: I am going to tell you a story that one of the connoisseurs reported to me, who held it from his personal experience and had not received it from another. This is how he said: "Once, while I was sitting in silence in the cell, this cursed demon of boredom rose up before me and would not let me celebrate the office, neither at night nor by day. For a week, I lay on the ground under an enormous weight that pressed upon me in such a way that it did not even allow the remembrance of God to rise in my heart, and, as for exercises, I did nothing other than have vain and unclean thoughts. Having remained all this time in this painful exercise, I despaired of my life, saying in my thought: it would be better for me to go into the world rather than wear the habit of monks; I do nothing, except for laziness and vain thoughts. And I decided in my thought that it would be better for me to go into the world rather than be at the monastery."
ܐܢܐ ܐܢ ܚܒܢܼܢܘܬܐ ܘܚܘ̈ܫܒܐ ܣܪ̈ܝܩܐ ܦܣܩܿܬ ܒܚܘܫܒܝ ܕܗܿܝ ܕܐܙܿܠ ܠܥܠܡܐ ܡܥܕܖ̈ܐ ܠܝ ܡܢ ܗ̇ܝ ܕܐܗܘܼܐ ܒܥܘܡܪܐ ܀ ܘܟܕ ܩܿܡܬ ܠܡܟܲܢܫܘ ܟܪ̈ܦܬܐ ܕܩܠܝܬܝ. ܫܡܿܥܬ ܡܢܫܠܝ ܩܠܐ ܟܕ ܡܡܿܠܠ ܥܡܝ ܢܝ̣ܚܐܝܬ ܘܐܡܼܪ ܠܝ ܠܐ ܬܐܙܿܠ ܠܥܠܡܐ. ܐܠܐ ܟܬܪ ܒܩܠܝܬܟ. ܘܫܡܼܫ ܟܠ ܥܕܢܐ ܡܙܡܘܪ̈ܐ ܗܢܐ ܫܲܒܚܘ ܠܡܪܝܐ ܟܠܟܘܢ ܥܡ̈ܡܐ. ܘܟܕ ܗܕܐ ܒܪܬ ܩܠܐ ܢܦܠܬ ܒܐܕܢ̈ܝ ܒܗܿ ܒܫܥܬܐ ܩܿܡܬ ܘܨܠܺܝܬ ܐܝܟ ܥܝܕܐ ܕܩܫ̈ܝܫܐ. ܘܫ̇ܪܝܬ ܘܐܡܿܪܬ ܠܗܢܐ ܡܙܡܘܪܐ ܬܠܬܐ ܝܘ̈ܡܝܢ. ܟܕ ܠܐ ܣܿܒܥ ܐܢܐ ܡܢ ܬܢܝܼܗ ܠܐ ܠܠܝܐ ܘܠܐ ܐܝܡܿ̈ܡܐ.
But as I rose to gather the things of my cell, I suddenly heard a voice that spoke to me gently and said to me: Do not go into the world, but remain in your cell, and celebrate the office at every hour with this psalm: Praise the Lord, all peoples. When this voice had struck my ears, immediately I rose and prayed according to the custom of the priests and began to read this psalm for three days, without tiring of repeating it neither at night nor by day."
ܗܿܠܝܢ ܫܿܡܥܬ ܡܢܗ ܕܗ̇ܘ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܕܐܡܼܪ ܩܕܡܝ. ܘܐܡܿܪ ܗܘܐ ܠܝ ܕܩܖ̈ܒܐ ܕܐܝܟ ܗܠܝܢ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܡܫܬܒܩܢܘܬܐ ܐܬܝ̇ܢ ܡܢ ܐܠܗܐ. ܐܝܟ ܕܢܬܒܩܐ ܨܒܝܢܐ ܕܚܐܪ̈ܘܬܐ. ܕܠܐܝܟܐ ܢܿܬܠ ܀ ܗܘ ܗܟܝܠ ܕܗܿܘܝܢ ܥܡܗ ܗܠܝܢ ܩܪ̈ܒܐ ܠܐ ܙܕܩ̇ ܠܗ ܕܢܫܒܼܘܩ ܩܠܝܬܗ ܘܢܫܿܢܐ ܡܢܗ̇. ܘܠܐ ܢܒܲܛܠ ܚܕܐ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܫܒܥ ܬܫܡ̈ܫܬܐ ܕܣܝ̈ܡܢ
That is what I heard from this blessed one who spoke before me, and he said to me: "Combats like these come from a sort of abandonment on the part of God, so that the free will may be tested (to see) to which side it will lean." Therefore, for him who is struggling with these combats, he must not leave his cell and go away, but he must persevere and not omit any of these seven (hours) of the office established by the Holy Spirit through the intermediary


[p. 362] {#po.45.p362}
ܠܪܘܚܐ ܕܩܘܕܫܐ ܒܝܕ ܛܘܒ̈ܢܐ ܫܠܝ̈ܚܐ. ܘܡܢ ܗܿܘ ܩܠܝܠ ܕܥܼܲܨܐ ܐܚܐ ܢܦܫܗ ܘܡܫܲܡܫ ܆ ܒܡܨܥܝܘܬܗ ܦܿܬܚ ܡܪܢ ܩܕܡܘܗܝ ܗܘ ܒܝܬ ܓܙܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܟܼܝܠܐ ܡܘܗܒܬܗ ܀
of the blessed apostles; and the little prayer that the brother has set for himself, let him celebrate it! By means of which, Our Lord will open before him this immeasurable treasure of His grace.

### Combats with the demons
ܩܪܒܐ ܕܝܢ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܕܗ̇ܘܐ ܥܡ ܐܚܐ ܒܩܠܝܬܗ ܫܐܕܐ ܕܩܘܛܥܐ ܘܗ̇ܘ ܕܡܐܝܢܘܬܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ. ܘܡܐ ܕܐܙܕܟܼܝ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܫܺܐܕܐ: ܡܫܠܡܝܢ ܠܗ ܠܐܝ̈ܕܝ ܬܠܬܐ ܫ̈ܐܕܐ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܝܐ. ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܗܼܢܘܢ ܡ̇ܿܚܬܝܢ ܠܗ ܠܬܚ̈ܬܝܬܗ̇ ܕܫܝܘܠ ܕܠܐ ܝܕܥܬܐ. ܗܿܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܩܕܡܐܝܬ. ܠܫܐܕܐ ܕܫܘܢܼܝܐ ܗܿܘ ܕܡܿܦܗܐ ܠܗ ܠܚܘܫܒܗ ܕܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܡܢ ܥܘܡܪܐ ܠܥܘܡܪܐ ܘܡܢ ܕܝܪܐ ܠܕܝܪܐ. ܘܡܢ ܛܘܪ̈ܐ ܠܛܘܪ̈ܐ. ܗܿܢܘ ܫܐܕܐ ܗܘ ܕܐܡܼܪ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܡܪܝ ܐܘܓܪܝܣ ܕܚܿܕܪ ܠܗ̇ ܠܢܦܫܐ ܡܢ ܬܠܬ ܫ̈ܥܝܢ ܘܥܕܡܐ ܠܥܣܪ ܫ̈ܥܝܢ. ܘܐܠܘ ܠܐ ܡܬܟ̇ܪܝܢ ܗܘܘ
88 The first combat that the brother will have to wage in his cell will be with the demon of acedia and that of boredom, and when he is defeated by these two demons, they deliver him into the hands of three other demons who make him descend into the depths of the Sheol of ignorance. It is first a question of the demon of wandering who makes the monk's thought wander, from dwelling to dwelling, from monastery to monastery, and from mountain to mountain. This is the demon of whom the blessed Evagrius says that he prowls around the soul from the third hour until the tenth, and if the days of this (combat) were not shortened, no flesh would survive.
ܝܘܡ̈ܬܗ ܕܗܢܐ ܠܐ ܚ̇ܝܐ ܗܘܐ ܟܠ ܒܣܪ ܀ ܘܡܐ ܕ݁ܡܲܟܪܟ ܠܗ ܠܗܘܢܗ ܕܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܒܟܠܗܝܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܘ̈ܟܝܬܐ ܕܐܡܿܪܬ ܗܝܿܕܝܢ ܡܲܫܠܡ ܠܗܼ ܠܫܐܕܐ ܕܪܒܘܬ ܟܪܣܐ. ܘܡܿܠܟ ܠܗ ܕܢܐܟܘܠ ܣܒܥܐܝܬ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܨܐ ܠܡܼܚܙܩ ܒܐܘܪ̈ܚܐ. ܘܡܐ ܕܫܼܩܠ ܣܝܒܪܬܐ ܚܠܝܡܐܝܬ ܇ ܗܺܝܕܝܢ ܗ̇ܘ ܫܐܕܐ ܕܡܐܝܢܘܬܐ ܡܿܝܬܐ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܝܘܩܪܐ ܣܓܝܐܐ. ܘܡܛܒܥ ܠܗ ܒܫܼܢܬܐ ܝܩܝܪܬܐ. ܘܡܐ ܕܥܠܬ ܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ ܠܐܣܛܘܡܟܐ ܘܣܰܪܬ ܝܗܒܐ ܪ̈ܝ̣ܼܚܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܩܒܪܐ. ܘܡܟܝܠ ܡܲܫܠܡܝܢ ܠܗ ܫܐܕܐ ܕܖ̈ܒܘܬ ܟܪܣܐ ܘܗܘ
And when (this demon) of wandering has made the monk's intellect wander in all these places that I have mentioned, then he delivers him to the demon of gluttony, who advises him to eat to satiety in order to be able to set out on the road. Once (the monk) has taken food in abundance, then the demon of boredom brings an enormous weight upon him and plunges him into a heavy sleep. When the food has penetrated into the stomach and (has) decomposed (there), it spreads an almost sepulchral odor, and, from then on, the demon of gluttony and that of boredom deliver him to the demon of fornication.
ܕܡܐܝܢܘܬܐ: ܠܫܐܕܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ ܀ ܘܩ̇ܪܒ ܩܕܡܐܝܬ ܠܘܬܗ ܒܝܕ ܫܼܢܬܐ. ܘܡܪܟܒ ܩܕܡܘܗܝ ܦܢܛܣ̈ܝܣ ܢܕܝ̈ܕܐ ܥܕܡܐ ܕܡܛܢܦ ܠܦܓܪܗ ܒܫܚ̈ܠܐ ܕܪܕܐ ܡܢܗ ܡܢ ܥܠܬ ܟܐܡܘ ܣܓܝܐܐ ܕܐܝܬ ܒܗ ܒܦܓܪܐ ܇ ܡܢ ܝܘܩܪܐ ܕܣܝܒܪ̈ܬܐ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܬܐ. ܘܡܚܲܒܠ ܠܬܩܼܢܘܬܗ ܕܗܘܢܐ ܒܝܕ ܕܡ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܚܙܘ̈ܢܐ ܕܚ̈ܝܼܠܐ. ܘܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܐܬܐ ܠܥܝܪܘܬܐ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܚ̈ܙܘܢܐ ܘܦܢܛܣّܝܣ ܢܕܝ̈ܕܬܐ ܕܚܿܘܝ ܠܗ ܒܚܠܡܐ ܨܿܐܖ ܠܗܝܢ ܩܕܡܘܗܝ. ܘܠܐ ܠܘܬ ܡܙܡܘܪܐ ܘܠܐ ܠܘܬ ܩܪܝܢܐ ܘܠܐ ܠܘܬ ܥܘܗܕܢܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܫܿ̇ܒܩ ܠܗ ܠܐܚܐ ܠܡܩܪܒ. ܐܠܐ ܐܢ ܕܡܬܥܢܐ ܝܘܡܐ ܟܠܗ ܥܡ ܕܡ̈ܘܬܐ
89 To begin with, (the latter) approaches him by means of sleep and he composes before him repugnant phantasms, to the point of defiling his body by fluxes that flow from him, because of the great quantity of humors that he has in the body and because of the weight of abundant foods; to the point of breaking the balance of the intellect by forms of frightening visions. Once (the monk) comes to wake up, (the demon) represents before him all the repugnant visions and phantasms that he had shown him in a dream and he does not let the brother approach neither psalm, nor reading, nor remembrance of God; but (the brother) remains occupied all day long by the idolatrous forms that (the demon) represents before him.
ܦܬܟܪ̈ܝܬܐ. ܕܨܳܐܪ ܩܕܡܘܗܝ. ܘܡܐ ܕܫܡܠܝ ܗܢܐ ܟܠܗ̇ ܣܥܘܪ̈ܘܬܗ ܗܿܝܕܝܢ ܩ̇ܪܒ ܘܡܢܲܣܐ ܠܗ ܫܐܕܐ ܠܝܛܼܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܓܙܐ. ܘܡ̇ܿܠܐ ܠܗ ܒܪܘܓܙܐ ܘܫܿܓܪ ܠܗ ܒܚܼܡܬܐ
And when this (demon of fornication) has accomplished all his task, then the cursed demon of anger approaches and tempts him. He fills him with anger


[p. 364] {#po.45.p364}
ܐܝܟ ܕܒܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܠܘܩܒܠ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܐܚ̈ܐ ܕܒܥܘܡܪܐ. ܘܠܘܩܒܠ ܡܕܒܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܠܘ ܟܐܢܐܝܬ ܡܦܪܢܣܝܢ. ܘܟܠܡܕܡ ܕܥ̇ܒܕܝܢ ܒܡܣܒ ܒܐܦ̈ܐ ܥ̇ܒܕܝܢ. ܘܠܘ ܡܛܠ ܐܠܗܐ. ܘܡܢܐ ܐܠܨܐ ܕܢܣܝܒܪ ܐܢܫ ܚܘܣܪܢܐ ܗܢܝܐ ܒܝܫܐ. ܐܠܐ ܙܕܩ ܕܢܫܢܐ ܡܢ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܠܝܬ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܐܢܫ ܕܫܪܝܪ ܚܘܒܗ ܠܘܬ ܚܒܪܗ. ܐܠܐ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܒܢܟܠܐ ܩܝܡܝܢ ܥܡ ܚ̈ܕܕܐ. ܘܡܐ ܕܚܙܐ ܫܐܕܐ ܠܝܛܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܓܙܐ ܕܐܫܬܥܒܕ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܟܠܗܝܢ ܆ ܗܝܕܝܢ ܡܪܚܩ ܡܢܗ.
and burns him with fury, like fire, against all the brothers of the monastery and against the guides who do not administer with equity: "Everything they do, they do with hypocrisy and not for God: what need is there to tolerate this pernicious evil? It is better to go away from here, for there is no one here whose love is sincere toward his companion, but all behave with perfidy toward one another." And when the cursed demon of anger has seen that (the brother) has yielded to all this, then he moves away from him.
ܘܩܪܒ ܡܟܝܠ ܫܐܕܐ ܕܫܘܢܝܐ ܗ̇ܘ ܕܡܢ ܠܥܠ ܐܡܪܬ ܆ ܘܡܫܡܠܐ ܒܗ ܟܠܗ̇ ܪ̈ܓܬܐ ܕܨܒܝܢܗ ܒܝܫܐ ܟܕ ܡܟܪܟ ܠܗ ܒܟܠܗܝܢ ܕܘܟ̈ܬܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܡܢ ܩܕܝܡ ܩܘܝ ܠܗ ܒܝܕ ܚܘܫ̈ܒܘܗܝ. ܟܕ ܣܐܡ ܩܕܡܘܗܝ ܬܘܩ̈ܠܬܐ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܬܐ ܒܝܕ ܟܪܘܟܝܐ ܗܢܐ ܫܐܕܐ ܗ̇ܘ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ. ܐܢܕܝܢ ܐܚܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܕܠܫܢܢ ܒܡܠܬܐ ܕܐܬܢ̈ܝܐ ܩܠܝܠ ܆ ܐܦ ܫܐܕܐ ܕܫܘܒܚܐ ܣܪܝܩܐ ܣܦܩܐܝܬ ܡܫܡܠܐ ܒܗ ܟܠܗ̇ ܙܓܬܐ ܕܨܒܝܢܗ. ܐܢܕܝܢ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܕܒܘܪ̈ܝܢ ܒܡܠܬܐ ܆ ܡܛܐܒ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܫܡܐ ܕܙܕܝܩܘܬܐ. ܘܒܝܕ ܗܕܐ ܡܫܡܠܐ ܒܗ ܟܠܗ̇ ܙܓܬܗ ܒܝܫܬܐ ܗܘ ܗܢܐ ܫܐܕܐ ܕܫܘܒܚܐ ܣܪܝܩܐ. ܒܗܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܟܪܘܟܝܐ ܕܐܘܪ̈ܚܐ ܆ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ
90 Then approaches the demon of wandering that I mentioned above and who accomplishes in him all the desire of his perverse will, by walking him through all these places that he had shown him previously during his thoughts, while the demon of fornication puts many traps before him during this tour. If it is a brother of our language who is a little (versed) in the language of the Athenians, the demon of vainglory even succeeds in accomplishing in him all the desire of his will; if he is one of those who are gifted with eloquence, he gratifies him with a reputation for probity and thereby, this demon of vainglory accomplishes in him all his perverse desire. Therefore, in this tour of the road, two demons torment the brother: the demon of fornication and that of vainglory.
ܫܐܕܐ ܡܫܢܩܝܢ ܠܗ ܠܐܚܐ. ܫܐܕܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ ܘܕܫܘܒܚܐ ܣܪܝܩܐ. ܘܡܐ ܕܡܛܥܐ ܠܗ ܗܢܐ ܫܐܕܐ ܕܫܘܢܝܐ ܒܟܠܕܘܟ ܘܠܐ ܐܫܟܚ ܠܗ ܢܝܚܐ ܐܝܟ ܣܘܟܝܗ ܇ ܗܝܕܝܢ ܡܪܚܩ ܡܢܗ ܫܐܕܐ ܗܢܐ ܆ ܘܩܪܒ ܠܘܬܗ ܫܐܕܐ ܕܥܩܬܐ ܘܕܟܪܝܘܬܐ ܘܡܫܢܩܝܢ ܠܗ ܒܥܩܬܐ ܥܕܡܐ ܕܡܛܐ ܠܚܢܩ ܢܦܫܐ. ܟܕ ܡܥܗܕܝܢ ܠܗ ܟܠܗܝܢ ܬܘܩ̈ܠܬܐ ܘܡ̈ܦܠܬܐ ܕܗܘܝ ܠܗ. ܘܡܫܠܡܝܢ ܠܗ ܗܠܝܢ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܫܐܕܐ ܠܐܝܕܝ ܫܐܕܐ ܕܦܣܩ ܣܒܪܐ. ܘܡܐ ܕܐܦ ܗܢܐ ܡܫܢܩ ܠܗ ܟܡܐ ܕܨܒܐ ܆ ܗܝܕܝܢ ܡܠܟ ܠܗ ܒܚ̈ܘܫܒܘܗܝ ܆ ܕܡܢ ܝܚܝܕܝܘܬܐ ܡܕܡ ܠܐ ܡܛܐ ܒ ܐܠܐ ܐܢ ܫܡܐ ܣܒܝܪܐ. ܕܡܘܒܠ ܠܐܒܕܢܐ ܠܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܩܢܝܢ ܠܗ. ܐܠܐ ܗܦܘܟ ܠܟ ܠܥܠܡܐ. ܘܐܢ ܐܫܬܡܥ ܠܗ ܐܚܐ ܘܗܦܟ ܠܥܠܡܐ. ܗܝܕܝܢ ܡܣܢܐ ܠܗ ܠܕܘܒܪܐ ܕܝܚܝܕܝܘܬܐ ܒܥܝ̈ܢܘܗܝ. ܘܟܡܐ ܙܒ̈ܢܝܢ ܐܦ ܡܢ ܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܕܫܪܪܐ ܡܗܦܟ ܠܗ ܠܘܬ ܛܘܥܝܝ ܕܠܐ ܝܕܥܬܐ.
91 And when this demon of restlessness has made (the monk) wander everywhere and he finds rest nowhere as he had expected, then this demon departs from him, and it is the demon of anguish and (that) of sadness who approach him and torment him with anguish, until he arrives at the crushing of the soul, by reminding him of all the scandals and all the falls that have befallen him. And these two demons deliver him into the hands of the demon of despair. And after this one has tormented him as much as he wished, then he counsels him in his thoughts: "By the solitary life you will achieve nothing, except for an illusory reputation that leads those who acquire it to perdition. Return therefore to the world." And if the brother obeys him and returns to the world, (the demon) then makes the conduct of the solitary life odious in his eyes. How many times does he not even make him abandon the true faith for the error of ignorance! Remedies
ܚܙܝ ܐܘ ܐܚܝ ܡܢ ܐܝܟܐ ܫܪܝܬ ܡܗܡܝܢܘܬܐ ܩܠܝܠ ܕܫܐܕܐ. ܕܩܘܛܥܐ ܇ ܘܐܝܟܐ ܐ ܫܩܠܬ ܫܘܡܠܝܐ ܪ̈ܦܝܘܬ ܕܨܒܝܢܐ. ܡܛܠܗܕܐ
92 See then, O my brother, where a little negligence caused by the demon of acedia has begun and where the slackening of the


[p. 366] {#po.45.p366}
ܐܡܪܬ ܡܢ ܠܥܠ ܆ ܕܙܕܩ ܠܗܘܢ ܠܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܗܘܐ ܥܡܗܘܢ ܩܪܒܐ ܗܢܐ ܕܩܘܛܥܐ ܆ ܕܢܥܨܘܢ ܢܦܫܗܘܢ ܡܛܠ ܐܠܗܐ ܥܕܡܐ ܠܚܕ ܡܙܡܘܪܐ ܘܢܫܒܚܘܢ ܬܠܬ ܙܒ̈ܢܝܢ ܒܗܘ ܫܡܐ ܪ̈ܚܝܡܐ ܕܐܒܐ ܘܕܒܪܐ ܘܕܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܩܘܕܫܐ. ܘܢܐܡܪܘܢ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܘܢܣܝܡܘܢ ܒܘܪ̈ܟܐ. ܘܡܐ ܕܥܒܕܝܢ ܗܟܢܐ ܆ ܥܒܪ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܩܪܒܐ ܗܢܐ ܒܚܝܠ ܥܘܕܪ̈ܢܗ ܕܡܪܢ. ܘܐܢܗܘ ܕܥܐ ܥܠܝܟ ܐܘ ܐܚܐ ܩܪܒܐ ܗܢܐ ܆ ܘܣܟܪ ܠܗ ܠܦܘܡܟ ܘܠܐ ܫܒܩ ܠܟ ܠܡܫܡܫܐ ܐܦܠܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܢ ܠܥܠ ܐܡܪܬ ܆ ܥܨܝ ܢܦܫܟ ܘܩܘܡ ܥܠ ܕ̈ܓܠܝܟ ܇ ܘܙܠ ܘܬܐ ܒܩܠܝܬܟ ܇ ܟܕ ܡܫܠܡ ܐܢܬ ܨܠܝܒܐ ܘܥܒܕ ܐܢܬ ܐܪܥܐ ܩܕܡܘܗܝ ܡܛܘܢܝܐ ܘܡܪܢ ܒܛܝܒܘܬܗ ܡܥܒܪ ܠܗ ܡܢܟ.
will arrives at its completion. That is why I said above that those who wage this battle against acedia must force themselves for God to the point of saying a single psalm and proclaiming the praise three times: in the cherished name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, that they say "Holy" and that they make a genuflection. And when they have done this, this battle will depart from them thanks to the strength of the assistance of Our Lord. And if this battle weighs upon you, O brother, closes your mouth and does not even let you celebrate the office, not even as I said above, make an effort, stand on your feet, go and come in your cell while greeting the cross and making a prostration before it, and, by his grace, Our Lord will deliver you from it.
ܫܿܡܥܬ ܓܝܪ ܐܘܿ ܐܚܝ ܡܢ ܦܘܡܐ ܗܿܘ ܫܪܝܪܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܕܓܠ ܆ ܕܐܡܼܪ ܠܝ ܒܚܕ ܡܢ ܝܘܡ̈ܝܢ ܟܕ ܡܒܓܢ ܗܘܝܬ ܩܕܡܘܗܝ ܡܛܠ ܫܐܕܐ ܗܢܐ ܠܝܼܛܐ ܐܡܼܪ ܠܝ ܓܝܪ ܗܟܢܐ. ܕܒܚܕ ܡܢ ܙܒܢ̈ܝܢ ܩܼܡ ܥܠܝ ܫܐܕܐ ܗܢܐ ܕܩܘܛܥܐ ܘܠܒܼܟܗ ܠܠܫܢܝ ܘܠܐ ܫܒܿܩ ܗܘܐ ܠܝ ܠܡܫܲܡܫܘ. ܡܛܠ ܕܡܘܒܠܐ ܝܩܝܪܬܐ ܣܼܡ ܥܠ ܪܝܫܝ. ܘܟܘܪܪܗܢܐ ܕܝܘܩܪܐ ܥܠ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܗܕܡ̈ܝ ܘܟܕ ܒܗܝܐ ܐܘܠܨܢܐ ܩܫܝܐ ܐܝܬܝ ܗܘܝܬ ܥܨܝܶܬ ܢܦܫܝ ܘܩܿܡܬ. ܟܕ ܠܡܫܡܼܫܘ ܠܐ ܡܨܐ ܗܘܝܬ. ܡܛܠ ܕܠܐ ܡܿܪܦܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܝ. ܒܠܚܘܕ ܕܝܢ ܡܫܲܠܡ ܗܼܘܝܬ ܨܠܝܒܐ ܘܥܿܒܕ ܗܘܝܬ ܡܛܘܢܝܐ ܩܕܡܘܗܝ ܕܨܠܝܒܐ. ܘܟܕ ܐܓ̇ܪܬ ܥܕܢܐ ܩܠܝܠ ܐܙܕܪܒܼ ܠܗ ܡܢ ܚܲܝܠܗ ܕܨܠܝܒܐ ܘܫܒܼܩܗ ܠܠܫܢܝ. ܘܫܿܪܝܬ ܠܡܫܿܒܚܘ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܘܬܢܿܐ ܗܘܝܬ ܚܕ ܚܕ ܦܬܓܡܐ ܡܢ ܡܙܡܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܕܘܝܕ ܘܟܡܐ ܕܐܢ̈ܐ ܡܩܲܡܣܢ
93 I have indeed heard, O my brother, from that truthful mouth which does not lie, that he said to me one day when I was complaining before him about this cursed demon: he spoke to me thus: "Once, this demon of acedia rose up against me, he seized my tongue and would not let me say the office, for he placed a great weight upon my head and a deep malaise upon all my limbs. Now, as I found myself in this hard trial, I forced myself and stood up, even though I could not say the office because (this demon) would not let me go; I contented myself with greeting the cross and making the prostration before the cross. As I held firm for a little while, (the demon) was repelled by the power of the cross: he abandoned my tongue and I began to praise God, to recite one after the other the verses of the psalms of David; and the more I held firm in this hard tribulation that had fallen upon me, the more it disappeared and vanished before me."
ܗܘܝܬ ܒܐܘܠܨܢܐ ܗ̇ܘ ܩܫܝܐ ܕܣܹܝܡ ܗܘܐ ܥܠܝ ܗܼܘ ܡܬܛܲܠܸܩ ܗܘܼܐ ܘܡܿܘܦܐ ܡܢ ܩܕܡܝ ܀ ܘܡܢܫܼܠܝ ܚܿܙܝܬ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܦܣܬ ܐܝܼܕܐ ܕܫܩܠܬ ܡܢ ܪ̈ܝܫܝ ܗܘܬ ܒܕܡܼܘܬ ܟܐܦܐ ܝܩܝܪܬܐ. ܘܒܗ̇ ܒܫܥܬܐ ܩܠܼ ܡܢܝ ܗܿܘ ܝܘܩܪܐ. ܘܐܬܡܠܼܝܬ ܚܕܘܬܐ ܘܪ̈ܘ̇ܙܐ. ܕܠܐ ܡܬܡܲܠܠ. ܘܗܘܼܐ ܟܠܗ ܦܓܪܝ ܥܡ ܢܦܫܝ. ܚܕ ܢܘܗܖܐ ܥܙܼܝܙܐ ܗܘ ܕܠܐ ܡܲܨܝܐ ܕܐܡܿܠܠܝܗ ܒܠܫܢܐ ܕܒܣܪܐ. ܘܗܘܿܝܬ ܬܠ̈ܬܝܢ ܘܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܝܘܡ̈ܝܢ ܒܗ̇ܝ ܚܕܘܬܐ ܘܒܣܥܘܪ̈ܘܬܗ ܕܗ̇ܘ ܢܘܼܗܪܵܐ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܕܠܐ ܒܠܠܝܐ ܝܿܬܒ ܗܘ̇ܝܬ ܥܠ ܐܪܥܐ ܘܠܐ ܒܐܝܡܡܐ
94 Then all of a sudden, I saw like the palm of a hand that removed from off my head like a heavy stone, and, at the same instant, I was lightened of this weight and filled with an inexpressible joy and gladness, and my whole body as well as my soul became a single radiant light that one cannot express with a tongue of flesh. And, for thirty-two days, I was in this joy and under the action of this holy light; so much so


[p. 368] {#po.45.p368}
ܡܛܠ ܕܠܐ ܝܶܕܥ ܗܘܝܬ ܐܢ ܒܗܢܐ ܥܠܡܐ ܐܝܬܝ ܗܘܝܬ ܐܘ ܠܐ. ܟܕ ܐܝܟ ܪܵܘ̇ܝܐ ܗܟܢ ܩܐܿܡ ܗܘܝܬ ܀
that at night, I did not sit on the ground, nor by day, for I did not know if I was in this world or not: [I stood like a drunken (man)"
ܗܿܠܝܢ ܫܿܡܥܬ ܡܢ ܗ̇ܘ ܦܘܡܐ ܫܪܝܪܐ ܕܐܡܼܪ ܩܕܡܝ. ܘܐܡܼܪ ܠܝ. ܕܟܠ ܐܡܬܝ ܕܗ̈ܘܐ ܥܡܟ ܗܢܐ ܫܐܕܐ ܕܩܘܛܥܐ ܡܢ ܩܠܝܬܟ ܠܐ ܬܦܼܘܩ. ܘܥ̈ܕܢܝܟ ܠܐ ܬܫܼܒܘܩ. ܐܦܢ ܥܠ ܕ̈ܓܠܝܟ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܩܿܐܡ ܐܢܬ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܡܫܲܡܫ ܐܢܬ ܐܠܐ ܐܢ ܒܠܒܟ. ܘܐܠܗܐ ܕܚܿܙܐ ܡܣܝܒܪܢܘܬܟ ܗܼܘ ܡܫܕܪ ܠܟ ܚܼܲܝܠܐ ܘܡܿܠܐ ܠܠܒܟ ܚܕܘܬܐ ܀
That is what I heard from that truthful mouth which said it before me. And he said to me: "Every time this demon of acedia is with you, do not go out of your cell and do not abandon your hours, even if you only stand on your feet, saying the office only in your heart; and God who sees your endurance will send you strength and will fill your heart with joy."
ܐܢܿܐ ܕܝܢ ܐܘْ ܚܒܝܼܒ ܢܦܫܝ ܗܐ ܣ̇ܡܬ ܐܢܳܝܢ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܒܐܓܪܬܼܐ ܐܟ ܒܥܬܟ ܕܡܢܝ. ܕܡܐ ܕܗ̈ܘܐ ܥܡܟ ܗܢܐ ܩܪܒܐ ܡܚܲܡܣܢ ܐܢܬ ܒܓܘ ܩܠܝܬܟ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܢܿܦܩ ܐܢܬ ܠܒܪ ܡܢܗܿ. ܘܐܦ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܦ̇ܿܓܥܝܢ ܒܗܿ ܒܐܓܪܬܐ ܗܕܐ ܢܙܕܗܪܘܢ ܡܢ ܒܝܫܼܘܬܗ ܕܫܐܕܐ ܗܢܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܗܼܘܝܘ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܟܠܗܘܢ ܩܪ̈ܒܐ. ܘܗܼܘ ܦܿܬܚ ܬܪܥܐ ܠܟܠܗܘܢ ܫ̈ܐܕܐ ܚܒܪ̈ܘܗܝ. ܐܢܕܝܢ ܡܚܲܡܣܢ ܐܢܬ ܐܘ̇ ܐܚܝ ܒܓܘ ܩܠܝܬܟ ܒܙܲܒܢܐ ܕܟ̇ܬܫ ܫܐܕܐ ܗܢܐ ܇ ܙܟܿܐ ܐܢܬ ܠܗܼ ܒܚܹܝܠ ܥܘܬܪܢܗ ܕܡܪܢ. ܡ
95 As for me, O beloved of my soul, I have cited these (words) in (this) letter, according to what you asked of me, so that, when this battle against you occurs, you may hold firm inside your cell without going out; and, furthermore, that all those who come upon this letter may also be warned of the wickedness of this demon. For it is he who is at the origin of all these battles and who opens the door to all the demons his companions. But if you hold firm in your cell, O my brother, at the moment when this demon enters into struggle with you, you will defeat him by the strength of the help of Our Lord.
ܐܒ̈ܗܝܢ ܓܝܪ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܐ ܟܕ ܐܬܟܲܢܫܘ ܡܢ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܩܘܕܫܐ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܠܗܕܐ ܫܠܡܘ ܕܠܝܬ ܩܪܒܐ ܕܚܲܣܝܢ ܡܢ ܗܺܝ ܕܢܫܒܼܘܩ ܐܢܫ ܩܠܝܬܗ ܘܢܫܿܢܐ ܡܢ ܩܕܡ ܕܝܘܐ ܕܩܘܛܥܐ. ܟܕ ܗܢܐ ܡܙܕܟܐ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܕܫܪܟܼܐ ܕܠܝܼܠܐܝܬ ܚܿܝܒܝܢ. ܐܚܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܠܐ ܩܢܐ ܡܣܝܒܪܢܘܬܐ ܐܟ ܐܕܡܢܛܘܣ ܩܕܡܘܗܝ ܕܫܐܕܐ ܗܢܐ ܆ ܫܶ̇ܒܩ ܘܡܫܢܐ ܡܢ ܩܠܝܬܗ. ܘܟܠ ܚܫܐ ܕܡܿܩܪܒ ܥܡܗ ܗܼܘ ܚܝܲܒܐ ܗ̇ܘܐ ܩܕܡܘܗܝ ܀
For our spiritual Fathers, united as they were by the Holy Spirit, are all in agreement on this point: There is no battle more painful for someone than (the temptation) to abandon his cell and to flee before the demon of acedia. When this one is defeated, all the others are easily so. Therefore the brother who, in the face of this demon, does not possess a resistance like diamond, abandons and leaves his cell, and, whatever the passion against which he will have struggled, will be defeated before it.

### The singing demon
ܐܝܬ ܓܝܪ ܫܐܕܐ ܐܚܪܢܐ ܕܡ̇ܩܪܒ ܥܡ ܐܚܐ ܒܡܘܬܒܐ ܫܼܲܠܝܐ ܕܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܗܿܘ ܕܡܟ̇ܢܝܢ ܠܗ ܝܕܘܥܬܢ̈ܐ ܡܪܟܒ ܙܡܝܪ̈ܬܐ. ܗܢܐ ܒܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܫܘܚ̈ܠܦܐ ܡ̇ܩܪܒ ܥܡ ܐܚܐ ܦܠܘܚܐ. ܩܪܒܗ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܗ̇ܢܘ ܕܡܿܪܟܒ ܙܡܝܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܛܘܥܢܝ ܗܿܠܝܢ ܕܡܓܪ̈ܓܢ ܠܚܼܫܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ. ܘܗܟܘܬ ܢܿܬܦ ܠܗ̇ ܠܢܦܫܗ ܕܐܚܐ ܒܗܢܝܘܬܐ ܕܢܥ̈ܡܬܗ ܥܕܡܐ ܕܐܦ ܡܢܗ ܕܦܓܪܐ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܒܝܬ ܡܥܡܪܗܿ ܕܢܦܫܐ ܇ ܫܒ̇ܩܐ ܘܡܫܢܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܡܢܗ ܇ ܐܠܘ ܠܐ ܚܲܝܠܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܣܿܡܟ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ̇. ܐܝܠܝܢ ܡ̈ܠܐ ܫܟܝܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܡܥܝܪ̈ܢ ܠܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ ܇ ܕܠܘ ܗܢܐ ܫܐܕܐ ܠܝܼܛܐ
96 There is yet another demon that struggles against the brother during the silent stay in the cell, the one whom the connoisseurs call the composer of songs. This one struggles in two different ways against the exercising brother: the first battle is this one: he composes equivocal songs, those which excite the passion of fornication. Thus he would drag the soul of the brother by the charm of his melodies, until making him leave and abandon even the body which is the dwelling of the soul, if the power of God did not sustain it. What obscene words that awaken the fire of fornication does this cursed demon not compose in his songs, so that, (this one)


[p. 370] {#po.45.p370}
ܡܪܟܒ ܒܙܡܝܪ̈ܬܗ. ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܟܕ ܢܦ̈ܠܢ ܒܐܕܢܘ̈ܗܝ ܕܐܚܐ ܐܟ ܕܒܢܘܪܐ ܗܟܢܐ܀ ܡܫܬܓܪ ܠܒܗ ܒܚܫܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ. ܡܪܢ ܒܪ̈ܚܡܝܟ ܒܛܠܝܗܿ ܠܡܥܒܕܢܘܬܗ ܕܫܐܕܐ ܗܢܐ ܠܝܼܛܐ ܡܢ ܣܓܘܕܝܟ ܀
falling into the ears of the brother, as with fire, his heart is thus inflamed with the passion of fornication! O Our Lord, in your mercy, remove from your worshippers the machinations of this cursed demon!
