ܗܼܘ ܕܝܢ ܠܝܼܛܐ ܟܡܐ ܕܗ̈ܢܝܢ ܙܡܝܪ̈ܬܐ ܡܪܟܒ ܩܪܒ ܠܘܬ ܓܫܬܐ ܕܒܣܪܐ. ܘܡܚܬܚܬ. ܐܘ ܟܡܐ ܥܣܩܐ 'ܫܥܬܐ ܗܕܐ!” ܘܟܡܐ ܬܩܝܦ ܘܩܫܐ ܩܪܒܐ! ܕܣܕܝܪ ܠܩܘܒܠܗ ܕܐܚܐ ܒܥܕܢܐ ܗܢܐ. ܗܐ ܥܕܢܐ ܕܒܗ ܡܬܟܼܲܠܠܝܢ ܦ̈ܠܘܚܐ ܒܟܠܝܠܐ ܕܣܗܕܘܬܐ. ܫܪܝܪܐܝܬ ܐܘ ܚܒܝܒ ܕܗܝܡܢܝܢܝ ܡܕܡ ܕܐܡ̇ܪ ܐܢܐ ܠܟܘܐ ܕܝܬܝܪ ܡܢ ܢܘܪܐ ܘܡܢ ܓܠ ܡ̈ܐܢܝ ܫ̈ܢܕܐ ܕܡܬܒܲܣܒܣ ܒܗܘܢ ܦܓܪܐ ܒܚ̈ܫܐ ܕܣܗܕܘܬܐ ܕܡܛܠ ܡܫܝܚܐ: ܩܫܝܐ ܫܥܬܐ ܗܕܐ ܘܡܲܪܝܪ ܚܫܗ̇ ܀
97 Now the cursed one, as he composes these songs, approaches to touch the flesh and excites it. Oh! How difficult is this hour! How violent and harsh is the struggle engaged against the brother at this moment! It is the hour when the exercitants are endowed with the crown of martyrdom. O beloved, in truth, believe me for what I am going to tell you: more than the fire and all the instruments of torture with which the body is torn to pieces in the torments of martyrdom (endured) for Christ, this hour is hard and its suffering bitter.
ܛܘܒܘܗܝ ܠܗܘ ܕܐܫܿܠܡ ܢܦܫܗ ܠܡܿ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܡܛܠ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܘܣܝܒܪܗܿ ܠܫܥܬܐ ܗܕܐ ܘܠܐ ܝܗܼܒ ܐܬܪܐ ܠܪܦܝܼܘܬܐ ܘܫܒܼܩ ܘܫܲܢܝ ܡܢ ܒܝܬ ܐܓܘܢܐ. ܕܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܚܫܐ ܗܢܐ ܩܫܝܐ ܡܬܬܣܝܡ ܒܪܝܫܗܼ ܟܠܝܠܐ ܕܢܟܦܘܬܐ ܇ ܗܘ ܕܡܲܨܡܚ ܟܠܗ ܒܢܘܗܼܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܚܫܘܫܘܬܐ ܇ ܗܿܘ ܕܡܫܿܡܗܝܢ ܠܗ ܐܒ̈ܗܝܢ ܚܙܬܐ ܕܫܘܦܪܐ ܕܩܢܘܡܗ ܕܗܘܢܐ ܕܥܠܘܗܝ ܕܢܿܚ ܫܼܡܫܐ ܪ̈ܒܐ ܕܙܕܝܩܘܬܐ ' ܗܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܕܚܼܙܬܗ
Happy is he who has delivered himself to death for Christ and has endured this hour without giving way to cowardice, without abandoning or leaving the place of the battle. For, after this hard suffering, will be placed upon his head the crown of chastity, all resplendent with the light of impassibility, that which our Fathers call the vision of the beauty of the intellect itself upon which rises the great sun of justice, that is to say the light of the vision of Our Lord.
ܕܡܪܢ ܀ ܗܢܐ ܩܪܒܐ ܠܘ ܣܿܓܝ ܡܿܓܪ. ܡܛܠ ܕܠܐ ܫܒܿܩܐ ܠܗ ܛܝܒܘܬܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ. ܘܐܠܐ ܡܛܠܩ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ̇ ܠܢܦܫܗ ܕܐܚܐ. ܐܦ ܒܟܘ̈ܠܝܬܐ ܥܿܒܕ ܗܘܐ ܢܟܼܝܢܐ ܇ ܡܢ ܚܡܝܼܡܘܬܐ ܕܥܘܙܐ ܕܚܫܗ. ܟܡܐ ܙܒ̈ܢܝܢ ܐܦ ܡܣ̈ܩܢܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܢ ܡܢܗ ܕܩܪܒܐ ܗܢܐ ܆ ܘܥ̇ܒܕܝܢ ܢܟܼܝܢܝܐ ܒܡܘܚܐ. ܣܡ̈ܡܢܐ ܗܟܝܠ ܕܡܬܐܣܐ ܒܗܘܢ ܩܪܒܐ ܗܢܼܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ. ܨܘܡܐ ܕܫ̈ܒܥܐ. ܘܫܗܖ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܦܿܣܩ ܕܠܠܝܐ ܟܠܗ. ܘܢܙܝܪܘܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܟܠ ܡܐܟܠܼܐ ܕܣܼܛܪ ܡܢ ܠܚܡܐ ܫܚܝܼܡܐ. ܘܓܠܼܝܙܘܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܚܼܙܬܐ ܕܢ̈ܫܐ ܘܡܡܠܠܐ ܕܥܡܗܝܢ. ܘܩܪܝܢܐ ܐܡܝܼܢܐ. ܘܡܛܘܢܝܐ ܕܠܐ ܦܿܣܩ
98 This battle does not last long, for the grace of God does not permit it, otherwise it would destroy the soul of the brother; it would even damage his kidneys because of the heat of the flame of his passion. How many times do vapors even occur during this battle and lead to an ailment of the brain. Now the remedies by which this battle is healed are the following: the fasting of weeks, the uninterrupted vigils throughout the night, the abstention from all food except ordinary bread, the deprivation of the sight of women and of conversation with them, the continual reading and the uninterrupted prostrations night and day.
ܒܠܠܝܐ ܘܒܐܡܡܐ ܀ ܐ݈ܢܳܐ ܕܝܢ ܐܝܟ ܪܵܥܝܼܢܝ ܕܝܠܝ ܡܿܠܟ ܐܢܐ ܠܗ̇ܘ ܡܿܢ ܕܡܿܩܪܒ ܥܡܗ ܩܪܒܐ ܗܢܐ! ܕܟܡܐ ܕܗܢܐ ܫܐܕܐ ܟ̇ܬܫ ܥܡܟ ܠܒܪ ܡܢ ܬܪܥܐ ܕܩܠܝܬܟ. ܠܐ ܬܦܼܘܩ. ܘܠܐ ܬܫܒܼܘܩ ܠܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܢܟܼܠܘܢ ܠܘܬܟ.
As for me, according to my opinion, I would advise the one against whom this battle is engaged: "As long as this demon makes war on you, do not go out beyond the door of your cell, and do not let others enter to you during the


[p. 372] {#po.45.p372}
ܒܙܒܢܐ ܕܗܢܐ ܩܪܒܐ܀ ܐܣܿܓܐ ܬܢܼܝܐ ܕܡܙܡܘܪ̈ܐ ܘܩܪܝܢܐ ܕܚܫܐ ܕܣ̈ܗܕܐ. ܘܡܛܘܢܝܐ ܕܩܼܕܡ ܨܠܝܒܐ. ܐܢܕܝܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܣܿܥܪ ܐܢܬ ܇ ܙܟܿܐ ܐܢܬ ܠܗ 'ܒܚܹܝܠ ܥܘܕܪ̈ܢܗ ܕܡܪܢ !. ܘܢܿܣܒ ܐܢܬ ܡܢܗ ܝܪܬܘܬܐ ܗܿܝ ܕܡܠܝܟܐ ܗܘܬ ܗܿܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܢܟܦܘܬܐ ܘܩܕܝܫܘܬܐ. 'ܗܠܝܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܛܒ̈ܥܝܢ ܕܡܿܙܠܓܝܢ ܒܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܕܬܠܝܬܝܘܬܐ ܩܕܝܫܬܐ ܘܡܬܚܙܝܢ ܒܥܕܢ ܨܠܘܬܐ ܇ ܒܕܡܼܘܬ ܟܘܟܒܐ ܣܿܓܝ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܀
time of this battle. Multiply the recitation of the psalms and the reading of the passion of the martyrs, as well as the prostrations before the cross. If you do this, you will defeat the (demon) by the strength of the help of Our Lord, and you will receive from Him the inheritance that was promised to you, that is to say chastity and holiness. These are the two seals that resplend with the light of the Holy Trinity and that appear at the moment of prayer in the likeness of a star of great brilliance.

### Visions, joys and tears
"ܡܢ ܒܬܪܖ̈ ܕܝܢ'' ܬܟܬܘܫܐ ܕܥܡ ܗܢܐ ܩܪܒܐ ܡܬܝ̇ܗܒܐ ܠܗ ܠܗܘܢܐ ܚܼܙܬܐ ܕܩܢܘܡܗܼ ܘܣܘܿܟܠܐ ܕܟܝ̈ܢܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܪ ܘܐܡܝܢܐܝܬܼ ܚܕܘܬܐ ܕܚܠܼܝܛ ܒܗ̇ ܚܫܐ ܕܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܡܬܡܿܠܝܐ ܢܦܫܐ ܀
99 After the struggle during this battle are given to the intellect the vision of itself and the intellection of the natures of the creatures; the soul is filled continually with joy, to which is mingled the passion of tears.
ܗܼܘ ܕܝܢ ܠܝܼܛܐ ܐܦܢ ܩܿܦܣ ܠܗܿ ܠܡܥܒܕܢܘܬܗ܆ ܐܠܐ ܡܢ ܩܪܒܗ ܕܥܡ ܦܠܘܚܼܐ ܠܐ ܫܿܠܐ. ܐܠܐ ܠܦܘܬ ܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܕܦܿܠܚܐ ܒܗ ܢܦܫܐ ܗܟܘܬ ܡܫܚܠܦ ܢܣܝ̈ܘܢܘܗܝ. ܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܚܙܐ ܕܠܪܓܬܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ ܠܐ ܡܫܬܥܿ̇ܒܕܐ ܢܦܫܐ ܆ ܗܿܝܕܝܢ ܥܒܕ ܦܘܪ̈ܣܐ ܐܚܪܢܐ ܕܒܝܼܫ ܡܢ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܦܘܪ̈ܣܐ ܘܨܢ̈ܥܬܐ ܕܥܿܒܕ ܗܘܐ ܩܕܡܝܗ̇ ܕܢܦܫܐ. ܕܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܝܢ
As for the cursed one, although he moderates his way of acting, he does not for all that cease his battle with the exercitant, but he varies his temptations according to the exercise that the soul practices. When he sees that the soul does not obey the desire for fornication, then he has recourse to another procedure, worse than all the procedures and the snares that he has laid before the soul. In what way? Listen.
ܫܼܡܥ ܀ ܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܐܙܕܟܼܝ ܗܼܘ ܠܝܼܛܐ ܡܢ ܢܣܝܘܢܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܕܩܲܪܒ ܠܢܦܫܐ ܘܐܫܬܼܘܝ ܗܘܢܝܐ ܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܗܕܐ ܠܡܘܗܒܬܐ ܕܣ̈ܘܟܠܐ ܗܿܝܕܝܢ ܐܦ ܗܼܘ ܡܫܚܠܦ ܠܙܢܐ ܕܬܟܬܘܫܗ ܕܥܡ ܦܠܘܚܐ ܘܡܿܪܡܐ ܒܗ» ܒܐܚܐ ܣܘܟ̈ܠܐ ܬܗܼܝܪ̈ܐ ܘܬܡ̈ܝܗܐ. ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܟܕ ܡܙܕܪ̈ܥܝܢ ܒܠܒܐ ܢ̈ܓܕܝܢ ܠܗ ܠܗܘܢܝܐ ܘܡܿܦܩܝܢ ܠܗ ܡܢ ܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܕܫܦܝܘܬܐ ܗܿܘ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܘܐ ܒܗ. ܘܬܚ̈ܘܝܬܐ ܡܢ ܟܬܒ̈ܝ ܩܘܕܫܼܐ ܐܬܝܿܢ ܠܗ ܠܐܚܐ ܒܥܕܢܐ ܗܿܘ. ܢܦܫܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܘܝܬܐ ܟܡܐ ܕܗܼܘ ܡܪܡܐ ܙܪܥܗ ܒܝܼܫܐ 'ܥܿܪܩܐ ܘܡܛܿܫܝܼܐ̇' ܒܬܘ̈ܘܢܐ ܟܣܺܝܐ ܕܠܒܐ. ܘܠܒܐ ܡܬܡܼܠܐ
After the cursed one has been defeated in the first temptation that he had inflicted upon the soul, and that, consequently, the intellect has been judged worthy of the gift of intellections, then (the demon) also changes the tactic of his battle with the exercitant: he pours upon the brother astonishing and wonderful intellections which, once sown in the heart, drag the intellect and make it go out from the place of limpidity where it was. Examples (drawn) from the holy Scriptures return to the brother at this moment. But the soul, having become unhappy as (the demon) pours out his perverse seed, flees and hides in the secret folds of the heart, and the heart is filled with fear and convulsion(s).
ܕܚܼܠܬܐ ܘܡܥܝܼܣܘܬܐ ܀ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܡܬܒܼܥܐ ܡܫܲܒܠܝܐ ܚܟܼܝܡܐ ܕܡܨܐ ܕܢܸܦܪܘܫ ܙܐܦܐ ܡܢ ܫܪܪ̈ܐ. ܐܢܕܝܢ ܡܫܲܒܠܢܐ ܠܝܬ ܘܦܪܘܫܘܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܠܝܬ ܠܗ ܠܗܘܢܐ ܠܡܨܪ̈ܝܢ ܕܒܝ̣ܫܘܬܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܗ ܠܡܥܛܦ. ܡܛܠ ܕܐܢ ܬܫܒܘܚܬܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܗ̇ ܠܢܦܫܐ ܘܐܢ ܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܐܟܘܬܗܘܢ ܡܪܟܒ ܐܦ ܗܼܘ ܘܥ̇ܒܕ. ܕܡܐ ܕܡ̣ܓܪ̈ܓ ܠܗ̇ ܠܢܦܫܐ ܘܡܿܦܩ ܠܗ̇ ܡܢ ܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܕܫܼܦܝܘܬܐ ܕܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܒܗ ܆ ܗܿܝܕܝܢ ܡܚܿܬܚܬ ܘܡܿܘܫܛ ܠܗܿ ܚܘܫ̈ܒܐ
100 It is here that one needs a wise guide capable of discerning the false from the true. But if there is no guide, and if the intellect does not possess the discernment of spirits, it will have to return to the perverse Egypt; so that, the soul having a way of praising or intellections, (the demon) forms and produces similar ones, he who, provoking the soul and making it go out from the place of limpidity where it was, excites it and suggests to it


[p. 374] {#po.45.p374}
ܕܡܣܒܪܢܘܬܐ ܘܕܙܕܩ ܠܟܐ ܠܡ ܕܬܘܬܪ ܒܗܿ܀ ܒܝܕܥܬܟ. ܐܘ ܠܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ. ܐܢܕܝܢ ܡܫܬܡܼܥ ܠܗ ܐܚܐ ܒܗܕܐ ܬܼܐܚܼܙܝ ܡܟܝܠ ܓܢܝܚ̈ܬܐ ܘܬܡܼ̈ܝܗܬܐ ܕܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܕܠܝܬ ܠܗܘܢ ܩܘܡܐ!' ܟܕ ܥܡ ܫܪܪ̈ܐ ܢܬܦ̇ܚܡܘܢ.
thoughts of self-sufficiency: "You must also make others profit from your knowledge." On the other hand, if the brother obeys him in this, come then to see the prodigies and the wonders of the intellections that have no consistency when one compares them with reality!
ܗܖ̈ܟܐ ܡܬܐܘܝܢ ܗܢܐ ܫܐܕܐ ܘܗ̇ܘ ܕܫܘ[ܒܚܐ ܣܪܝܩܐ ܥܡ ܚ̈ܕܕܐ ' ܕܟܡܐ ܕܗܢܐ ܡ̇ܿܪܡܐ ܙܪܥܗ ܒܝܼܫܐ ܒܝܕ ܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܗ̇ܘ ܕܫܘ[ܒܚܐ ܣܪܝܩܐ ܡܓܪܓ ܠܣ̈ܓܝܐܐ ܕܢܿܩܠܣܘܢܝܗܝ. ܘܗܕܐ ܗܝ ܕܐܡܼܪ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܡܪܝ ܐܘܓܪܝܣ ܕܐܦ ܟܗܢܘܬܐ ܡܫܬܘܕܐ ܠܗ ܫܘ[ܒܚܐ ܣܪܝܩܐ ܘܐܝܟ ܗ̇ܘ" ܕܡܩܹܝܡ ܥܠ ܬܪܥܗ ܠܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܒܿܥܝܢ ܠܗ ܘܐܢ ܠܐ ° ܐܙܿܠ ܥܡܗܘܢ ܒܦܟܪ̈ܐ ܡ̇ܿܘܒܠܝܢ ܠܗ. ܒܚ̈ܠܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܣܿܓܝ ܡܿܛܥܐ ܫܐܕܐ ܗܢܐ ܡܪܟܒ ܙܡܝܪ̈ܬܐ ܒܐܚܐ ܐܝܢܐ ܕܡܿܩܪܒ ܥܡܗ. ' ܐܢ ܠܐ ܕܝܢ ܡܫܬܥܿ̇ܒܕ ܠܫܘܒܚܐ ܣܪܝܩܼܐ ܠܐ ܡܙܕܟܼܐ ܡܢܗ. ܐܢ ܕܝܢ ܡܫܬܡܼܥ ¢" ܠܗܢܐ ܡܿܪܚܩ ܡܢܗ 'ܚܲܝܠܐ ܗ̇ܘ ܐܠܗܝܐ ܕܠܘܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܝ ܒܩܪܒܗ ° ܕܥܡܗܘܢ. ܘܡܐ ܕܡܲܪܚܩ ܡܢܗ ܆ ܗܿܝܕܝܢ ܡܬܡܲܠܝܐ ܢܦܫܐ
It is here that this demon and that of vainglory agree among themselves: the more the former pours his perverse seed through intellections, the more the one of vainglory drags many people to flatter the (brother). This is what the blessed Mar Evagrius says: "Vainglory even promises him the priesthood" as if (the demon) were standing at his door in place of those who are asking for it. And if he does not come with them, they are going to lead him bound. It is especially through dreams that this composer of songs demon deceives the brother, whoever he may be, with whom he struggles. But if the (brother) does not obey vainglory, he is not defeated by him. If, on the other hand, he obeys this one, this divine strength that accompanied him in his struggle against them departs from him; and, once it has departed from him, then his soul is filled with thoughts of every kind.
ܚܘ̈ܫܒܐ ܕܟܠ ܓܢܣ ܀ ܘܝܬܝܪܐܝܬ "° ܚܘܫܒܐ ܕܚܣܡܐ””" ܡܫܲܢܩ ܠܗ̇ ܠܢܦܫܐ. ܒܗ̇ܝ ܕܠܐ ܡܿܨܝܐ ܫܡܿܥܐ” ܩܘܠܣܐ ܐܘ̇ ܬܫܒܘܚܬܐ ܕܐܢܫ ܐܚܪܝܢ. ܘܐܢ ܡܿܨܝܐ ܐܦܠܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܡܪܐ ܕܟܠ ܘܡܐ ܕܝܼܕܥܘ ܗܠܝܢ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܫܐܕܐ ܠܝ̈ܛܐ ܕܠܗܢܐ ܫܦܼܠܐ ܡܛܬ ܢܦܫܐ 'ܕܘܝܿܬܐ ܗܿܝܕܝܢ ܡܿܪܚܩܝܢ ܡܢܗ̇ ܫܐܕܐ ܕܫܘ[ܒܚܐ ܣܪܝܩܐ ܘܗ̇ܿܘ ܕܚܣܡܐ. ' ܘܗܼܢܐ ܡܪܟܒ '' ܙܡܝܪ̈ܬܐ܀ ܡܫܪܐ ܒܐܘܡܢܘܬܗ ܩܕܡܝܬܐ. ܗ̇ܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܕܡܪܟܒ7 ܙܡܝܪ̈ܬܗ° ܫܟܝܪ̈ܬܐ ܗ̈ܢܝܢ ° ܕܡܥܝܪܢ ܠܚܫܼܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ ܘܫܿܓܪ ܠܗܿ ܠܢܦܫܐ :'ܒܢܘܪܐ ܕܚܫܐ11 ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ. ܘܫ̇ܒܩ ܠܗ̇ ܠܢܦܫܐ ܒܐܝ̈ܕܘܗܝ ܘܡܫܡܠܐ ܒܗ̇ ܟܠܗ ܨܒܝܢܗ
101 And it is especially the thought of jealousy that torments the soul, in that it cannot hear praise or commendation about someone else, not even, if it were possible, about God, the Lord of the Universe. And when these two cursed demons perceive that the poor soul has arrived at this weakness, then the demon of vainglory and that of jealousy depart from it, and it is the composer of songs who begins again his first artifices, that is to say that he composes his obscene songs that awaken the passion of fornication, inflames the soul with the fire of the passion of fornication, abandons the soul into his hands, and accomplishes in it all his perverse will.
ܒܝܫܐ. ܐܠܐ ܐܢܬ ܗܟܝܠ ܐܘْ ܦܿܠܘܚܐ ܐܸܢ ܐܝܬ ܠܟ ܡܫܒܠܢܐ ܚܟܼܝܡܐ ܗܪܛ ܐܬܓܿܪܓܚ ܩܕܡܘܗܝ ܕܿܠܦܟ ܐܘܪ̈ܚܐ ܬܪ̈ܝܨܬܐ ܕܬܖ̈ܐ ܐܢܬ ܒܗ̇ܿ. ܐܢܕܝܢ ܡܫܒܠܢܐ ܠܝܬ ܠܟ ܘܦܪܘܫܘܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܐܠܵܐ ܩܼܢܐ ܐܢܬ ܣܹܝܡ ܠܟ ܝܕܥܐ ܗܢܐ ܕܐܡܿܪ ܐܢܐ3ܐ ܠܟܐ ܒܠܒܟ ܕܟܠ ܐܡܬܝ ܕܡܬܩܲܪܒ ܠܘܬܟ ܗܢܐ ܡܢܣܝܢܼܐ ܝܿܕܥ ܐܢܬ ܠܗ. ܣܿܓܝ ܥܣܩܐ ܕܘܟܬܐ ܗܕܐ. ܘܒܠܥܕ ܡܢ ܢܣܝܢܐ ܕܫܩܼܠ ܐܢܫ ܒܩܢܘܡܗ ܆ ܠܡܚܼܣܢ ܐܢܫ ܡܢ
But you, O exercising brother, if you have a wise guide, run to prostrate yourself before him, so that he may inform you of the right path that you will have to follow. If you do not have a guide and if you do not possess the discernment of spirits, put into your heart this sign that I am going to tell you, so that, every time this tempter approaches you, you recognize him. This passage is indeed very perilous and, unless one has had personal experience, there is hardly one among the connoisseurs who discerns it, for (the


[p. 376] {#po.45.p376}
ܝܕܘ̈ܥܐ' ܦܪܫ ܠܗܿ. ܡܛܠ ܕܫܩܼܝܠܐ ܦܪܨܘܦܐ ܕܐܡܪ̈ܐ ܬܡ̈ܝ̣ܼܡܐ ܡܢ ܠܒܪ ܘܡܢ ܠܓܘ ܡܠܼܝܐ ܡܪܬܐ ܕܕܐܒ̈ܐ ܚܿܛܘ̈ܦܐ. ܐܠܐ ܐܡܼܪ ܡܪܢ. ܕܡܢ ܦܐܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܬܕܥ̣ܘܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܀
demon) wears, on the outside, the mask of innocent lambs, and, on the inside, he is filled with the gall of ravenous wolves. But Our Lord said: It is by their fruits that you will recognize them.
ܐܝܠܝܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܝܢ ܦܐܪ̈ܝܗܘܼܢ ܫܡܼܥ. ܡܐ ܕܡܫܲܪܝܢ ܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܡܬܬܼܙܝܥܝܢ ±9 ܒܠܒܟ ܗܕܐ ܐܬܿܐ ܣܼܝܡ“ ܒܝܬ ܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܕܡܢ ܫܦܝܘܬܐ ܕܚܘܫ̈ܒܐ ܐܘܿ̇ ܕܡܢ ܬ ܡܠܐܟܐ ܕܒܛܝܼܠܘܬܐ ܇ ܐܘ̇ ܕܡܢ ”? ܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܩܘܕܫܐ. ܐܘ̇ ܕܡܢ'“ ܐܡܝܢܘܬܐ ܕܩܪܝܢܐ ܇ ܐܘ̇ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܡܢ ܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܟܝ̈ܢܝܐ ܕܢܝ̇ܚܢ ܒܠܒܟ»““ ܠܗܿܢܘܢ %? ܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܕܡܢ ܫܐܕܐ ܗܿܘ ܠܝܼܛܐ ܡܸܙܕܪ̈ܥܝܢ ܒܟ ܀
102 Listen then to what their fruits are: when the intellections begin to move in your heart, here is the sign that is placed (on one hand) between the intellections that (come) from the limpidity of thoughts, either by the Angel of Providence, or by the grace of the Holy Spirit, or from perseverance in reading, or those that appear in your heart, coming from natural seeds; and (on the other hand) between these intellections that are sown in you by this cursed demon.
ܟܠ ܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܕܡܐ ܕܡܫܲܪܝܢ ܡܬܬܙܝܥܝܢ '? ܒܠܒܟ ܠܘܬ ܟܢܝܫܘܬܐ ܕܚܘ̈ܫܒܐ ܢܿܬܦܝܢ °? ܠܗ ܠܡܕܥܟ ܘܚܕܘܬܐ ܘܚܫܐ ܕܕܡ̈ܥܐ "3 ܡܿܠܝܢ ܠܗܿ ܠܢܦܫܟ ܇ ܘܒܡܟܼܝܟܘܬܐ ܘܒܢܝܼܚܘܬܐ ܘܒܡܬܢܚܬܢܘܬܐ ܡܛܒܥܝܢ ܠܗ ܠܗܘܢܟ9: ܕܥܼ ܕܡܢ ܓܒܐ ܕܝܡܝܢܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܘܟܡܐ ܕܓܿܣܐ ܡ̇ܿܒܘܥܐ ܕܠܒܟ ܡܢܗܘܢ"3: ܗܒܼ ܠܗܘܢ ܐܬܪܐ ܠܡܥܒܕܢܘܬܐ. ܘܐܣܼܘܪ' ܬܪ̈ܥܐ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܕܩܠܝܬܟ. ܘܥܘܼܠ ܠܓܘ̈ܝܬܐ. ܘܬܒ ܒܚܫ̣ܟܐ ° ܒܫܠܼܝܐ ܐܝܟܐ ܕܐܦܿܠܐ ܩܠܐ ܕܨܦܼܪܐ ܐܫܡܿܥ ܐܢܬ. ܘܐܢ ܡܼܛܐ ܥܕܢܐ ܕܬܫܡܼܫܬܐ ܚܼܙܝ ܠܐ ܬܩܘܡ ܟܠ ܟܠܗ. ܕܕܠܡܐ ܬܗܘܼܐ ܐܝܟ ܫܒܪܐ ܕܡܚܿܠܦ ܒܠܐ ܝܕܥܬܗ ܇ ܟܟܪܐ ܕܕܗܒܐ ܒܬܬܐ ܕܒܡܠܐ"?'ܥܕܢܐ ܡܚܲܠܝܐ ܠܚܼܟܗ”'“, ܐܠܐ ܐܢܬ ܐܝܟ ܀܀ ܬܐܓܪܐ ܚܟܼܝܡܐ ܆ ܒܙ̈ܒܢܐ ܕܐܣܬܩܒܠܬ ܠܟܐ ܡܪܓܢܝܬܐ ܣܓܐܝܬ ܕܡ̈ܝܐ ܠܐ ܬܚܠܦܝܗ̇ ܒܨܒ̈ܘܬܐ ܫܝ̈ܛܬܐ ܕܒܟܠܙܒܢ ܣܺܝ̣ܡܢ ܩܕܡܝܟ ܘܬܗܼܘܐ ܚܪܬܟܙ + ܐܝܟ ' ܗܘ ܥܡܐ@+“ ܕܢܦܩ ܡܢ ܡܨܪ̈ܝܢ ܕܫܼܒܩ ܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ ܕܡܢܢܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܘܪܐܓ'܀ ܗܘܐ ܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ ܢܕܝܕܬܐ ܕܡܨܪ̈ܝܐ ܀
All the intellections which, in beginning to move in your heart, carry your spirit to the recollection of thoughts, fill your soul with joy and with the passion of tears, and which plunge your intellect into humility, appeasement and condescension, know that they are on the side of the right. And, as long as the source of your heart produces them, give them the occasion to operate; close all the doors of the cell, enter inside, and sit in the darkness, in silence, there where you can no longer even hear the song of a bird. And if the hour of the office arrives, take care not to stand up in any way, so as not to be like a child who, in his ignorance, would give a talent of gold in exchange for a fig that only softens his palate for an instant. But you, like a wise merchant, at the moment when a pearl of great price is presented to you, do not exchange it for the vile objects that are always before you, and may your end not be like (that) of this people who went out of Egypt and who abandoned the food of spiritual manna by coveting the abominable food of the Egyptians.
ܐܠܐ ܐܢܬ ܐܘܿ ܐܚܐ ܦܠܘܚܐ ܥܘܠ ܠܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܓܘ ܡܢܟ ܘܐܨܼܕ ܚܿܝܪܐ ܕܡܕܥܟ ܒܗܿܘ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܫܒܝܼܚܐ ܕܡܢ ܗܺܝ ܣܥܘܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܣ̈ܘܟܠܐ ܕܡܢ ܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܕܢܚܳܐ ܒܟ ܘܐܣܬܝܒܪ ܡܢܗܿ ܟܡܐ ܕܝܶܗܒ ܠܟ ܡܪܢ ܐܬܪ̈ܐ. ܡܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܢ̇ܦܩ ܐܢܬ ܡܢ ܬܡܢ ܝܨܦ ܕܩ̈ܢܘܢܐ ܕܩܠܝܬ̣ܼܐ ܛܿܒܐܝܬ. ܘܠܐ ܬܗܡܐ ܒܬܫܡ̈ܫܬܟ ܡܛܠ ܕܐܢ ܡܗܡܐ ܒܗܝܢ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܕܢܿܦܩ ܡܢ ܐܬܪܐ ܗ̇ܘ ܒܐܝ̈ܕܝ ܫܺܐܕܐ ܡܫܬܠܡ ܐܝܟ ܡܠܬܐ ܕܐܒ̈ܗܬܐ ܀
103 But you, O exercising brother, enter into the place that is in you and fix the gaze of your spirit upon this glorious light that appears in you thanks to this action of the intellections that manifests itself in you by grace, and nourish yourself with it, as long as Our Lord gives you the occasion. And when you go out from there, observe properly the rules of the cell, and do not neglect your hours of office, for if the monk neglects them after having gone out from this place, he will be delivered into the hands of the demons according to the word of the Fathers.


[p. 378] {#po.45.p378}
ܗܢܘ ܐܬܪܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܫܬܥܿܒܕ ܠܢܡ̈ܘܣܐ ܟܬܝܼܒ̈ܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܠܥܠ ܡܢ ܢܛܘܪ̈ܬܗ ܕܢܡܘܣܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܦܘܠܚܢܗ. ܘܟܡܐ ܕܬܡܢ ܩܿܐܡ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܠܝܬ ܠܗ ܠܡܫܬܥܿܒܕܘ ܠܚܕ ܡܢ ܢܡ̈ܘܣܐ ܕܣܝܼܡܝܢ ܠܥܒ̈ܝܕܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܗ̇ܢܘ ܐܬܪܐ ܗ̇ܿܘ ܕܐܡܼܪ ܫܠܝܚܐ ܕܠܝܬ ܒܗ ܥܒܼܕܐ ܘܠܐ ܒܪ ܚܐܪ̈ܐ. ܘܠܐ ܓܙܘܖ̈ܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܥܘܖ̈ܠܘܬܐ ܐܠܐ ܟܠ ܘܒܟܠܢܫ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܗܘ. ܚܡܝܼܡܘܬܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܠܐ ܡܨܐ ܡܕܥܐ ܡܣܲܝܒܪ ܠܗ̇ ܀ ܢܐܡܪ ܕܝܢ ܗܫܐ ܦܘܪ̈ܫܢܐ ܕܐܝܬ ܒܝܬ ܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܕܝܡܝܢܐ. ܘܩܕܡܐܝܬ ܢܐܡܪ ܥܠ ܗܢܘܢ ܕܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܟܝ̈ܢܝܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܗܼܢܘܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܕܡ̣ܙܕܪ̈ܥ ܒܢܦܫܐ. ܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܡܫܪܝܢ ܚܫܐ ܘܬܘܬܐ ܢ̇ܦܠܐ ܒܠܒܗ ܕܒܪܢܫܐ ܟܕ ܡܬܥܼܗܕ ܛܝ̈ܒܘܬܗ ܘܦܘܪ̈ܢܣܘܗܝ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܕܠܘܬܗ ܕܒܙܒܢ ܙܒܢ. ܘܕܟܡܐ ܝܿܩܪܗ ܐܠܗܐ ܠܓܢܣܢ ܒܡܪܚܡܢܘܬܗ ܇ ܘܝܿܨܦ ܕܝܠܢ ܒܟܠ ܙܒܼܢܐ ܒܙܒܢܗ. ܘܕܟܡܐ ܗܝ ܛܠܘܡܘܬܢ ܕܠܘܬܗ. ܕܠܘ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܡܐܨܦ ܠܐ ܨܲܦܝܢܢ ܕܢܿܛܘܪܘܬ ܦܘܩ̈ܕܢܘܗܝ ܐܠܐ ܐܦ ܡܿܪܓܙܘ ܡܪܓܙܝܢܢ ܠܗ ܒܝܕ ܥ̇ܒ̈ܕܝܢ ܒܝܼܫ̈ܐ. ܘܟܕ ܗܟܘܬ ܛܲܠܘ̈ܡܐ ܐܝܿܬܝܢ ܠܘܬܗ܆ ܗܿܠܝܢ ܟܠܗܝܢ ܛܒ̈ܬܐ ܣܿܥܪ ܗܼܘ ܠܘܬܢ. ܒܪܡ ܕܝܢ ܥܡܗܘܢ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܆ ܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܐܬܝ̈ܢ ܡܢ ܥܝ̈ܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܟܝܠܐ ܀ ܡܠܘܐܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܡܢ ܩܪܝܢܐ ܕܟ̈ܬܒܝ ܩܘ̈ܕܫܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ. ܒܙܒܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܣܥܘܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܕܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܟܝܢ̈ܝܐ ܐܦ ܡܐ ܕܕܡܿܟ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܒܫܢܼܬܐ ܕܠܠܝܐ ܘܕܐܝܡܡܐ ܠܐ ܫ̇ܠܐ ܡܿܕܥܗ ܡܢ ܬܢܼܝܐ ܕܡܙܡܘܪ̈ܐ ܘܗܪ̈ܓܐ ܕܟ̈ܬܒܝ ܩܘ̈ܕܫܐ. ܗܕܐ ܗܝ ܐܬܿܐ ܐܟ ܕܒܦܣ̈ܝܩܬܐ ܡܐ ܕܡܿܥܒܕܝܢ ܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܟܝ̈ܢܝܐ ܒܢܦܫܐ. ܘܗܠܝܢ ܗ̈ܘܝܢ ܡܐ ܕܫ̇ܪܝܬ ܢܦܫܐ ܡܬܡܲܛܝܐ ܠܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܕܫܦܝܘܬܐ ܗܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܒܫܽܘܪܝ ܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܢܘܬܐ. ܪ̈ܚܼܡܬܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܥܡ̈ܠܐ ܕܡܛܠ ܐܠܗܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܐ ܒܗܕܐ ܣܥܘܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܕܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܟܝ̈ܢܝܐ. ܕܐܢ ܡܲܨܝܐ. ܠܐ ܫܼܒܩ ܒܪܢܫܐ ܕܘܒܪܐ ܕܠܘ ܒܩܢܘܡܗ ܓܿܡܪ ܠܗ. ܗܿܢܘ ܝܕܥܳܐ ܕܡܢ ܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܕܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܟܝܢܝܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܢ ܒܢܦܫܐ ܀
It is (indeed) the place (interior) which is not subject to written laws, because its exercise is found above the observances of the law; now, as long as the monk stays there, he does not have to submit himself to any of the laws established for creatures, because it is there the place of which the Apostle speaks: There is neither slave, nor free man, nor circumcision, nor uncircumcision, but everything and in all, is Christ. However, the understanding cannot support the heat of these intellections. Of the discernment of thoughts. 104 Let us speak now of the distinction that there is between all the intellections of the right. And, firstly, let us speak of those (which come) from natural seeds, for they are the first seed that is sown in the soul. When (these intellections) set themselves in motion, suffering and repentance fall into the heart of man when he remembers the graces and the procedures of God toward him in various circumstances: how God, in his mercy, has honored our race and has taken care of what is ours always in its time: and how, (on the other hand) is (great) toward him our ingratitude, for not only do we not have great concern for the observance of his commandments, but also we only irritate him greatly by our bad actions. And while we are thus ungrateful toward Him, He is lavish toward us with all these benefits. However, at the same time as these intellections, tears flow without measure from our eyes. The reserve of (natural) seeds comes from the reading of the holy books, for, during the period of the action of the intellections (coming) from natural seeds, even when the monk falls asleep at night and by day, his spirit does not cease to recite the psalms and to meditate on the holy Scriptures. Such is as in summary the sign (of knowledge) of the action in the soul of natural seeds, and this happens when the soul begins to arrive at the place of (the state of) limpidity, that is to say at the beginning of the pneumatic stage. For the love of the labors that (are) for God is realized by this action of the intellections of natural seeds, to the point that, if it were possible, man would not abandon a conduct which in itself is not perfect; that is the sign that, assuredly, intellections of natural seeds are in the soul.


[p. 380] {#po.45.p380}
ܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܕܝܢ ܡܠܐܟܐ ܕܒܛܝܼܠܘܬܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܢ ܒܢܦܫܐ ܗܕܐ ܗܝ ܐܬ̈ܐ ܕܡܬܬܙܝܥܢܘܬܗܘܢ. ܢܘܪܐ ܓܝܪ ܢܿܦܠܐ ܒܠܒܗ ܕܒܪܢܫܐ ܆ ܘܝܿܩܕ ܘܡܫܬܿܠܗܒ ܒܗ̇ ܒܠܠܝܼܐ ܘܒܐܝܡܡܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܡܢ ܬܐܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܓ̈ܘܫܡܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܡܠܘܐܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܣ̈ܘܟܠܐ ܕܡܢ ܡܠܐܟܐ ܕܒܛܝܠܘܬܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܢ ܒܢܦܫܐ. ܟܕ ܢܓܕ ܠܗ ܡܲܠܐܟܐ ܕܒܛܝܼܠܘܬܐ ܠܗܘܢܐ. ܠܬܗܖܐ ܕܥܠ ܒܪܘܝܘܬܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܕܒܗܕܐ ܒܼܪܝܬܐ ܡܨܒܬܬ ܐ ܒܫܘܦܪ̈ܐ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܐ ܕܐܝܟܢܐ ܐܬܒ̇ܪܝܬ ܗܕܐ ܒܪܝܬܐ ܡܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ. ܘܕܐܝܟܢܐ ܣܦܼܩ ܚܲܝܠܐ ܗܿܘ ܐܚܼܝܕ ܟܠ ܆ ܠܡ̇ܝܬܝܘ ܠܗܕܐ ܒܪܝܬܐ ܡܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܠܗܘ̇ܝܐ. ܘܚܕܐ ܚܕܐ ܡܢ ܨܒ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܒܪܝܬܐ ܡܢܗ̇ ܘܠܗ̇ ܆ ܪܡܿܐ ܣܘܟܠܗ̇ ܒܡܕܥܗ ܕܒܪܢܫܐ. ܘܚܕ ܚܕ ܡܢ ܟܝ̈ܢܝܐ ܡܢܗ ܘܠܗ. ܢܓܕ ܠܗ
105 As for the intellections that are produced in the soul by the Angel of Providence, here is the sign (of knowledge) of their being set in motion: a fire indeed falls upon the heart of man, burns there, and flames night and day, for it is by the contemplation of corporeal substances that the filling of these intellections is done which by the Angel of Providence are produced in the soul, when (this) Angel of Providence draws the intellect until wonder before the creative work of God in this creation adorned with multiple beauties: how this creation was made from nothing, [how this all-powerful force sufficed to bring this creation from nothingness to existence, and (how) each of these elements of the creation taken separately spreads its intellection into the spirit of man, and (how) each of the natures taken separately draws the intellect to wonder about it].
ܠܗܘܢܐ ܒܬܗܪ̈ܐ ܕܥܠܘܗܝ ܀ ܘܕܟܡܐ ܒܛܝܼܠ ܠܗ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܛܒܐ ܘܡܪܚܡܢܐ ܥܠ ܡܦܪܢܣܢܘܬܗܿ ܕܒܪܝܬܐ. ܕܐܦ ܥܕܡܐ ܠܪܚܫܐ ܫܝܼܛܐ ܕܥܠ ܐܪܥܐ ܦܪܝܣܐ ܒܛܝܼܠܘܬܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܘܝ̇ܨܦ ܕܝܠܗ. ܘܟܡܐ ܕܡܠܐܟܐ ܕܒܛܝܼܠܘܬܐ ܡܪܡܐ ܢܘܪܐ ܒܠܒܐ ܡܢ ܪ̈ܬܚܼܐ ܕܣ̈ܘܟܠܐ ܕܡܕܒܪܢܘܬܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܕܒܗܢܐ ܟܠ ܪܵܘ̇ܐ ܠܒܗ ܕܒܪܢܫܐ ܒܚܕܘܬܐ. ܘܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܕܥܝ̈ܢܘܗܝ ܐܬܝ̈ܢ ܕܠܐ ܟܝܼܠܐ. ܒܪܡ ܠܘ ܡܢ ܚܼܫܐ ܘܟܪܝܘܬܐ ܐܠܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܚܕܘܬܐ ܕܬܗܪ̈ܐ ܕܣܿܘܟܠܐ ܀
How also the good and merciful God has the concern to govern the creature, since the Providence of God extends even to the vile reptile that is found on the earth and that He cares for it! And as the Angel of Providence pours the fire into the heart from the ardor of the intellections of the divine Economy that (is) in each one, the heart of man becomes intoxicated with joy, and the tears of his eyes occur without measure, not as a result of suffering and sadness, but of the joy of wonder (produced by) the intellections.
ܘܥܡ ܗܠܝܢ ܣ̈ܘܟܠܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܐ ܐܦ ܬܪ̈ܥܘܬ ܡܡܠܠܐ ܩܠܝܠ ܪܐܙܢܝܐ. ܒܪܡ ܠܘ ܪ̈ܺܐܙܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܠܥܠ ܡܢ ܥܠܡܐ. ܐܠܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܡܢ ܣ̈ܘܟܠܐ ܕܟܝ̈ܢܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܓܠܝܐ ܐܬܝܿܢ ܟܠܗܘܢ. ܒܪܡ ܗܕܐ ܬܪܥܘܬ ܡܡܠܠܐ ܠܘ ܣܿܓܝ ܡܿܓܪܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܦܣܿܩܐ ܠܗ̇ ܚܡܝܼܡܘܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܓܼܫܬܐ ܕܡܠܐܟܐ ܕܒܛܝܼܠܘܬܐ ܕܐܡܝܢܐܝܬܿ ܠܒܝܼܫ ܠܗ̇ ܠܢܦܫܐ ܒܗܕܐ ܣܥܘܪܘܬܐ ܀
106 And, at the same time as these intellections, there is also the favor of a somewhat mysterious conversation; because, however, it is not a question of mysteries above the world, but because they come from the natural intellections of the creatures, they all come to the light of day. Nevertheless, this favor of a conversation does not last long, because limited is the heat coming from the contact of the Angel of Providence who constantly envelops the soul in this operation.
ܐܬܿܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܕܡܢ ܡܠܐܟܐ ܕܒܛܝܼܠܘܬܐ ܗܕܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܿ. ܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܕܚܕܘܬܐ ܘܚܡܝܼܡܘܬܐ ܕܣ̈ܘܟܠܐ ܕܠܐ ܦ̇ܣܩܐ ܠܐ ܒܠܠܝܐ ܘܠܐ ܒܐܝܡܡܐ. ܡܬܬܙܼܝܥܢܘܬ ܗܘܢ ܕܝܢ ܕܣܿܘܟܠܐ ܕܡܢ ܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܢ ܒܫܼܠܝܐ ܠܘܬ ܐܚܐ ܦܠܘܚܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ. ܡܫܲܪܝܢ ܀
This is the sign of the intellections (coming) from the Angel of Providence: tears of joy and the warmth of intellections without interruption, neither by night nor by day. Here is how the setting in motion of the intellections occurs, which, (coming) from grace, are produced in silence in the practicing brother: (these intellections) begin and operate in the heart without involving tears, but it is joy, truly, and an unspeakable wonder before


[p. 382] {#po.45.p382}
ܘܡ̇ܥܒܕܝܢ ܒܠܒܐ ܟܕ ܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܠܝܬ ܒܗܘܢ. ܚܕܘܬܐ ܕܝܢ ܐܝܢ. ܘܬܗܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܡܠܠ ܕܗܿܘ ܚܙܘܐ ܫܒܝܚܐ ܕܡܢܗ ܕܢ̇ܚܝܢ. ܘܬܪ̈ܥܘܬ ܡܡܠܠܐ ܕܠܐ ܦܣܿܩܐ ܠܐ ܒܠܠܝܐ ܘܠܐ ܒܐܝܡܡܐ, ܘܪ̈ܐܙܐ ܘܫܒ̈ܝܚܬܐ ܕܡܬܓ̈ܠܝܢ ܒܗ̇ ܒܗܕܐ ܬܪ̈ܥܘܬ ܡܡܠܠܐ, ܘܠܘ ܡܢ ܟ̈ܬܒܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܝܢ
this glorious vision from which (the intellections) appear; it is the favor of a conversation that is interrupted neither by night nor by day, and (these are) mysteries and splendors that are revealed in this favor of a conversation, and not from books, that is to say, according to the teaching of another.
ܐܘܟܝܬ ܡܢ ܝܘܠܦܢܐ ܕܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ. ܒܪܡ ܗܠܝܢ ܣ̈ܘܟܠܐ ܕܡܢ ܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܗܿܘܝܢ: ܒܕܡܘܬ ܟܘܟܒܐ ܡܬܚܙܝܢ ܒܗܘܢܐ. ܘܡܿܕܥܐ ܕܝܢ ܡܬܒܲܣܡ ܒܗܘܢ ܟܕ ܠܪܘܟܒܐ ܕܡܠܬܐ ܠܐ ܐܬܝܢ: ܐܝܟ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܡܢ ܡܠܐܟܐ ܕܒܛܝܠܘܬܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܢ. ܒܪܡ ܢܦܫܐ ܒܗܠܝܢ ܬܠܬ ܡܥܒܕܢ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܣ̈ܘܟܠܐ ܗܟܢܐ ܡܬܚܿܙܝܐ. ܒܗܕܐ ܩܕܡܝܬ ܕܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܟܝ̈ܢܝܐ ܠܣܿܦܝܠܐ ܕܡ̈ܝܐ. ܘܒܗܿܝ ܕܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܕܡܠܐܟܐ ܕܒܛܝܠܘܬܐ ܢܘܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܗܿܘܝܐ. ܘܒܗܿܝ ܕܝܢ ܕܬܠܬ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܕܡܘ ܥܛܝܦܐ. ܗ̇ܠܝܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܦܣܼ̈ܝܩܬܐ: ܗܿܠܝܢ ܬܠܬܐ ܝܕ̈ܝ̈ܥܐ
107 However, these intellections which take place by grace appear in the intellect in the form of a star and the spirit finds its delights therein, whereas they do not come to formulate words, like those which take place by the Angel of Providence. But in these three operations of the intellections, the soul appears in the following way: in the first, that of natural seeds, it resembles a sapphire; in the second, that of the Angel of Providence, it becomes fire; and in the third, it is enveloped in a formless light. Such are, as in summary, the three signs (of knowledge) of the intellections which take place on the right side.
ܕܣ̈ܘܟܠܐ ܕܡܢ ܓܒܐ ܕܝܡܝܢܐ ܗܿܘܝܢ. ܢܐܿܡܪ ܗܫܐ ܕܐܝܢܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܙܘܥܐ ܕܢܩܼܝܦ ܠܗܕܐ ܡܥܒܕܢܘܬܐ ܕܓܒܐ ܕܝܡܝܢܐ ܘܡܢܐ ܗܝ ܐܬܗ̇. ܠܗܕܐ ܣܥܘܪܘܬܐ ܕܗܿܠܝܢ ܣ̈ܘܟܠܐ ܕܡܢ ܓܒܐ ܕܝܡܝܢܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܢ. ܙܘܥܐ ܕܡܟܝܟܘܬܐ ܢܩܼܝܦ ܠܗ̇: ܗܿܘ ܕܡܐ ܕܡܫܿܪܐ ܕܡܿܥܒܕ ܒܗ̇ ܒܢܦܫܐ. ܥܿܛܪ ܡܢܗ ܘܢܿܦܩ ܡܢ ܠܒܗ ܕܒܪܢܼܫܐ ܪ̈ܝܼܚܐ ܗܲܢܝܐܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܨܝܢ ܪ̈ܓܫܝ ܦܓܪܐ ܡܝܘܬܐ ܠܡܩܲܒܠܘܬܗ. ܥܕܡܐ ܕܡܐܢ̈ܘܗܝ ܘܟܠܗ ܓܘܫܡܗ ܕܒܪܢܫܐ ܡܢܗ ܕܗ̇ܘ ܪ̈ܝܼܚܐ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܗܘܐ: ܘܠܕܡܘܬܗ ܡܫܬܲܚܠܦ. ܘܟܡܐ ܕܗܢܐ ܙܘܥܐ ܕܡܟܝܟܘܬܐ ܢܛܝܪ ܒܗ̇ ܒܢܦܫܐ: ܐܝܟ ܫܠܗܒܝܬܐ ܕܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܝܿܩܕܐ ܒܠܒܗ ܕܒܪܢܫܐ. ܘܟܡܐ ܕܗܕܐ ܢܘܪܐ ܡܲܥܒܕܐ ܡܢ ܠܓܘ: ܪ̈ܕܝܐ ܕܣ̈ܘܟܠܹܐ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܡܒܘܥܐ ܕܡ̈ܝܐ. ܓܝ̇ܚܝܢ ܘܢ̇ܦܩܝܢ ܡܢ ܠܒܗ ܕܒܪܢܫܐ. ܘܟܡܐ ܕܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܕܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܡܿܙܠܓ ܒܠܒܐ: ܡܢ ܓܘ ܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܕܚܕܘܬܐ ܐܬܝ̈ܢ ܥܝ̈ܢܐ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܢܚ̈ܠܐ ܕܡ̈ܝܐ. ܘܟܡܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܪ̈ܕܝܢ ܡܢ ܥܝܢܐ: ܠܒܐ ܡܢ ܠܓܘ ܡܬܡܠܐ ܚܕܘܬܐ ܘܪܘܙܐ.
108 Let us now say what is the movement that joins this operation on the right side and what is its sign (of knowledge). To this action of these intellections which are on the right side is attached a movement of humility, which, when it begins to operate in the soul, exhales and releases from the heart of man a sweet perfume that the senses of a mortal body cannot perceive, to such an extent that the clothes and the whole body of the man are impregnated with this holy perfume and are changed to its likeness. And as long as this movement of humility is preserved in the soul, it burns in the heart of man like a flame of fire. And as long as this fire operates from within, a current of intellections bubbles and gushes forth like a spring of water and comes out of the heart of man. And as long as the light of the intellections shines in the heart within, tears of joy invade the eyes, like torrents of water. And the more these tears flow from the eyes, the more the heart is filled inwardly with joy, gladness, and the hope of the


[p. 384] {#po.45.p384}
ܘܣܿܒܪܐ ܕܚ̈ܝܐ ܥܬܝ̈ܕܐ: ܗ̇ܘ ܕܠܐ ܡܨܐ ܠܫܢܐ ܠܡܬܪ̈ܓܡܘ ܘܠܡܐܡܪ. ܪ̈ܐܙܐ ܕܣܘ̈ܟܠܘܗܝ. ܗܠܝܢ ܐܝܬܝܗܝܢ ܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܕܡܫܲܡܗܝܢ ܠܗܝܢ ܐܒܗ̈ܝܢ ܐܪܥܐ ܕܡܘܠܟܢܐ. ܕܡܐ ܕܡܬܡܲܛܐ ܠܗܝܢ ܒܪܢܫܐ: ܠܐ ܕܚܿܠ ܡܢ ܩܪܒܐ ܕܦܠ̈ܫܬܝܐ ܡܬܝ̈ܕܥܢܐ.
future life, because the tongue cannot translate or express the mysteries of its intellections. These are the tears that our Fathers call the "promised land," for when a man reaches them, he no longer fears the war of the "spiritual" Philistines.
ܟܡܐ ܕܗܕܐ ܣܥܘܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܣ̈ܘܟܠܐ: ܘܗܢܐ ܙܘܥܐ ܕܡܟܝܟܘܬܐ: ܘܗܠܝܢ ܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܗ ܠܐܝܚܝܕܝܐ: ܠܝܬ ܩܕܡ ܥܝܢ̈ܘܗܝ ܚ̇ܛܝܐ ܒܒܪܝܬܐ. ܐܠܐ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܒܢܝ̈ܢܫܐ ܙܕܝ̈ܩܐ ܚܫܝܼܒܝܢ ܠܗ. ܛܘܒܘܗܝ ܠܗܿܘ ܕܐܫܬܘܝ ܠܗܕܐ ܣܥܘ̈ܪܘܬܐ ܘܛܥܡ ܚܟܐ ܕܡܕܥܗ ܡܢ ܒܘܣܡܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܪ̈ܐܙܐ ܐܠܗ̈ܝܐ. ܙܒ̈ܢܬܐ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܬܐ ܡܢ ܚܐܦܐ ܕܥܘܫܢܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܣܥܘܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܢ̇ܦܠ ܒܪܢܫܐ ܥܠ ܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܗ̇ܘܐ ܝܘܡܐ ܚܕ ܘܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܕܠܐ ܡܫܟܼܚ ܠܡܩܼܡ. ܡܛܠ ܕܠܐ ܡܨܐ ܦܓܪܐ ܠܡܣܲܝܒܪܘ ܩܕܡ ܗܕܐ ܚܕܘܬܐ. ܘܠܐ ܣܝܒܪܬܐ ܢܣܒ ܘܠܐ ܫܢܬܐ ܗܘܿܝܐ ܠܗ
109 And as long as this action of the intellections, this movement of humility, and these tears take place in the monk, in his eyes there are no longer any sinners in creation, but all men are esteemed righteous by him. Happy is he who has been judged worthy of this action and who has tasted with the palate of the spirit a portion of the delights of these divine mysteries: often, by the violence of the strength of these actions, a man falls to the ground and remains there for a day or two without being able to rise, the body being unable to sustain itself before this joy; he does not take food and does not have his habitual sleep, for the body as well as the soul are nourished with a spiritual food coming from the sweetness of these intellections.
ܐܝܟ ܥܝܿܕܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܬܘܪ̈ܣܝܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܡܬܬܪ̈ܣܐ ܦܓܪܐ ܥܡ ܢܦܫܐ: ܡܢ ܚܠܝܘܬܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ. ܟܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܒܗܢܐ ܐܬܪܐ ܩ̇ܐܡ ܒܪܢܫܐ: ܢܘܗܪܐ ܕܢܿܚ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܒܠܠܝܐ ܘܒܐܝܡܡܐ. ܘܠܐ ܣܢܝܩ ܥܠ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܗ ܕܫܡܫܐ ܒܐܝܡܡܐ. ܘܐܦܠܐ ܥܠ ܣܗܪܐ ܒܠܠܝܐ, ܡܛܠ ܕܠܠܝܐ ܘܐܝܡܡܐ ܫܘܝܢ ܐܢܘܢ
110 As long as a man stands in this place, a light shines upon him, by night as by day; he has no need by day of the light of the sun, nor by night of the moon, for night and day are the same for him who has merited this gift.
ܥܠܘܗܝ: ܕܗ̇ܿܘ ܕܠܗܕܐ ܡܘܗܒܬܐ ܡܫܬܘܐ. ܟܡܐ ܕܒܗܕܐ ܣܥܘܪܘܬܐ ܩܿܐܡ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ. ܠܐ ܣܢܝܩ ܥܠ ܫܘܡܠܝܐ ܕܥ̈ܕܢܐ. ܘܠܐ ܥܠ ܥܡܠܐ ܕܩܪܝܢܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܟܠܗ ܥܡܠܹܗ ܕܬܓܪܐ ܗܿܢܘ: ܥܕܡܐ ܕܢ̇ܦܠܐ ܒܐܝ̈ܕܘܗܝ ܡܪܓܢܝܬܐ ܣܓܝܐܬ ܕܡ̈ܝܐ. ܚܙܝ ܐܘܿܐܪ ܐܚܘܢ: ܕܒܝܘ̈ܡܬܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܠܐ ܬܒܿܥ ܡܢܟ ܕܝܠܟ ܐܠܗܐ ܐܠܐ ܐܢ ܢܛܘܪ̈ܬܗ ܕܫܠܝܐ. ܘܕܠܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܠܟ ܡܿܥܠܐ ܘܡܿܦܩܐ ܠܘܬ ܐܢܫ. ܘܐܦܠܐ ܐܢܫ ܢܥܼܘܠ ܠܘܬܟ.
As long as the monk finds himself under this action, he has no need to perform the hours (of the office), nor to toil over readings, for it is entirely the labor of a merchant before the pearl of great price falls into his hands. See, O our brother, that in these days, God does not ask of you anything other than the observance of solitude: that you neither have to enter anyone's place, nor go out of your own, and that no one else enters yours. Examples of the Fathers
ܥܗܼܕ ܐܘܿ ܚܒܝܼܒ ܠܗܘ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܘܪ̈ܒܐ ܐܟܝܼܠܐ@. ܕܐܦܠܐ ܡܢ ܬܪ̈ܥܐ ܒܼܪܝܐ ܢܣܼܒ ܫܠܡܐ ܕܐܫܬܕܖ ܠܗ ܡܢ ܐܢܫ ܡܢ ܐܒܗ̈ܬܐ ܒܢ̈ܝ ܬܓܡܗ ܇ ܡܛܠ ܪ̈ܒܘܬܗ̇ ܕܗܕܐ ܡܘܗܒܬܐ ܕܒܗܿ ܩ̇ܐܡ ܗܼܘܐ. ܟܕ ܐܡܪ ܘ ܠܗ̇ܘ ܕܢܩܼܫ ܒܬܪ̈ܥܗ. ܕܐܠܘ ܗܘܘ ܗܿܠܝܢ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܚܙܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܕܗܒܐ :ܐܟ ܙܒܠܐ ܚܫܼܒܝܢ ܠܝ ܠܦܘܬ ܡܘܗܒܬܐ ܕܝܗܼܒ ܠܝ ܐܠܗܐ ܒܥܕܢܐ ܗܢܐ.
111 Remember, O beloved, this blessed and great Achilles, who, from his outer door, did not (wish) even to receive the greeting that was addressed to him by one of the Fathers, his colleagues, because of the greatness of this gift in which he was established, saying to him who had knocked at his door: "Even if all these apples were of gold, I would consider them as filth, in comparison with the gift that God has granted me at this moment."
ܗܟܢܐ ܐܦ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܐܒܐ ܐܪ̈ܣܢܝܣ ܇ ܥܼܒܕ ܠܘܬ ܗܘ ܕܐܬܼܐ ܠܫܠܡܗ. ܕܡܛܠ ܕܚܛܝܼܦ ܗܘܐ ܡܕܥܗ ܒܬܗܖ̈ܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܣ̈ܘܟܠܐ ܆ ܘܕܗܿܘ
The blessed Abba Arsenius also acted in the same way with him who had come to greet him, because his spirit was enraptured in the wonder of these


[p. 386] {#po.45.p386}
ܢܘܗܪܐ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܕܕܢܝܼܚ ܗܘܐ ܥܠܘܗܝ. ܠܐ ܦܬܼܚ ܦܘܡܗ ܘܡܿܠܠ ܥܡ ܗ̇ܘ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܕܐܬܼܐ ܠܚܼܙܬܗ. ܠܘ ܓܝܪ ܕܨܒܝܢܗ ܗܘܼܬ ܗܕܐ ܐܠܐ ܬܗܖܿܐ ܗܿܘ ܕܠܓܘ ܠܒܿܟ ܗܘܐ ܠܠܫܢܗ ܘܠܐ ܫܿܒܩ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܕܢܡܿܠܠ ܀ ܐܸܢ ܐܝܬ ܐܢܫ ܕܐܡܿܪ ܠܟ ܇ ܕܒܠܥܕ ܡܢ ܗܕܐ ܣܥܘܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܡܨܐ ܠܡܬܼܒ ܒܫܠܝܐ ܕܩܠܝܬܐ ܇ ܕܓܠܐ ܗܼܘ ܠܐ ܬܬܗܝܼܡܢܝܘܗܝ. ܡܛܠ ܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܥܼܠܠܢ ܡܚܲܡܣܢ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܒܫܼܠܝܐ ܕܩܠܝܬܐ. ܚܕܐ ܡܛܠ ܫܘܒܚܐ ܕܒܢܝ̈ܢܫܐ. ܕܡܐ ܕܡܬܩܲܠܣ ܡܢ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܐ ܡܛܠܗܕܐ ܇ ܡܚܲܡܣܢ ܘܝܿܬܒ. ܒܪܡ ܚܪܬܗ ܒܗܠܝܢ ܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܒܝ̈ܫܬܐ ܗܿܘܝܐ. ܐܘ̇ ܡܬܚܲܒܠ ܗܘܢܗ ܒܝܕ ܚܘ̈ܫܒܐ ܕܫܼܘܒܚܐ ܣܪܝܩܐ ܐܘ ܒܝܕ ܕܡ̈ܘܬܐ ܘܡܬܪܡܝܢ ܒܗ ܦܿܪ̈ܙܠܐ ܐܟ ܕܓܕܫ ܠܐܢܫ ܐܢܫ ܡܢ ܝܚܝ̈ܕܝܐ ܕܠܘ ܡܛܠ ܐܠܗܐ ܝܿܬܒܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܒܫܼܠܝܐ ܆ ܐܠܐ ܡܛܠ ܩܘܠܣܐ "ܕܡܢ ܒܢܝ̈ܢܫܐ”" ܘܥܠܗܕܐ ܐܫܬܒܩܘ ܬ ܡܢ ܒܛܝܼܠܘܬܐܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܘܢܦܼܠܘ ܒܐܝ̈ܕܝ ܫܺܐܕܐ ܘܗܘܘ ܐܬܿܐ ܕܣܘܪ̈ܕܐ ܠܟܠܗܘܢ ܒܢܝ̈ܢܫܐܼ ܐܘ̇ ܢܿܦܠܝܢ ܒܐܝ̈ܕܝ ܫܐܕܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ ܇ ܐܟ ܡܐ ܕܟܬܼܒ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܦܠܕܝܣ ܒܟܬܒܐ ܕܦܪܕܝܣܐ ܇ ܡܛܠ ܗ̇ܘ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܐܣܬܦܢܘܣ ܕܫܒܼܩ ܡܘܬܒܐ ܫܿܠܝܐ ܕܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܘܐܙܼܠ ܠܒܝܬ ܙܢܝ̈ܬܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܢܿܫܡܠܐ ܙ̈ܓܬܗ ܗ̇ܘ ܕܡܢܿܫܩ ܗܘܐ ܐ̈ܝ̈ܕܘܗܝ ܘܪ̈ܓܠܘܗܝ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܐܘܓܪܝܣ
intellections and of this holy light which had appeared to him. He did not open his mouth to speak with this blessed one who had come to see him; it was not deliberate, but the inner wonder had seized his tongue and prevented him from speaking. 112 If someone tells you that, without this action (of the intellections), one can establish oneself properly in the solitude of the cell, he is a liar; do not believe him, for there are two motives for which a monk perseveres in the solitude of the cell: the first is for human glory, because, when he is praised for it by a great number, he remains established with perseverance. But this ends in the two perversities that follow: either his intellect is spoiled by thoughts of vainglory or by images, and chains are cast upon him, just as it happened to one or another of the monks who had established themselves in solitude, not for God, but to be praised by men; that is why they were abandoned by divine Providence, fell into the hands of demons, and became a sign of terror for all men; or else they fall into the hands of the demon of fornication, as the blessed Palladius wrote in the Paradise Book concerning this blessed Stephen who abandoned the solitary stay in the cell and went to the house of courtesans to satisfy his concupiscence, he whose hands and feet Saint Mar Evagrius kissed, wanting to guide him from the fault of ignorance to the conduct of life where he stood.
ܟܕ ܨܿܒܐ ܗܘܐ ܕܢܿܦܢܝܘܗܝ ܡܢ ܛܘܥܝܝ ܕܠܐ ܝܕܥܬܐ ܠܘܬ ܕܘܒܿܪܐ ܕܩܿܐܡ ܗܘܐ ܒܗ ܀ ܘܐܝܟ ܗܺܝ ܕܓܕܫܬ ܠܛܘܒܢܐ ܝܥܩܘܒ ܡܫܿܢܝܢܐ ܕܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܡܘܬܒܐ ܗ̇ܘ ܥܡܝܼܠܐ ܕܫܠܼܝܐ ܘܡܘܗ̈ܒܬܐ ܕܩܲܒܠ ܡܢ ܐܠܗܐ ܠܐܝܢܐ ܥܘܡܩܐ ܕܒܝ̈ܫܬܐ ܐܬܢܿܚܼܬ. ܠܘ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܢܦܼܠ ܡܢ ܩܝ̇ܡܗ ܇ ܐܠܐ ܐܦ ܩܼܛܠܐ ܩܼܛܠ ܗܘܐ. ܘܗܕܐ ܓܕܫܬ ܠܗ ܡܛܠ ܕܝܗܼܒ ܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܠܚܘܫ̈ܒܐ ܕܫܼܘܒܚܐ ܣܪܝܩܐ ܒܬܪ̈ܥܝܼܬܗ. ܗܟܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܐܡܼܪ ܐܦ ܡܟܬܒܢܐ ܒܬܫܥܝܬܗ. ܕܡܛܠ ܚܘ̈ܫܒܐ ܕܪ̈ܡܘܬܐ ܓܕܫܬ ܠܗ ܗܕܐ ܡܦܘܠܬܐ. ܒܪܡ ܡܢ ܫܼܘܒܚܐ ܣܪܝܩܐ ܡܬܝܿܠܕܐ ܪܡܘܬܐ. ܗ̇ܘ ܕܠܘ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܡ̈ܦܘܠܬܗ ܥܗܕ ܡܚܝܼܠܘܬܗ ܘܛܝܒ̈ܘܬܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܕܠܘܬܗ ܐܠܐ ܘܐܦ ܫܼܕܐ ܡܢ ܪܼܥܝܢܗ ܚܘܫ̈ܒܐ ܕܫܘܒܚܐ ܣܪܝܩܐ.
113 Likewise concerning what happened to the blessed James the Wanderer after the laborious stay of solitude and the gifts he had received from God, into what abyss of evils he had descended! For not only did he fail in his pact, but he had also committed a murder, and this happened to him because he had given free rein in his spirit to thoughts of vainglory; but if the author also spoke thus in his account, (namely) that it was because of (his) thoughts of pride that this fall had happened to him, it is precisely because it is from vainglory that pride is born. Now he, after his falls, not only remembered his weakness and the graces of God toward him, but he also rejected from his spirit the thoughts


[p. 388] {#po.45.p388}
ܘܐܫܬܘܼܝ ܠܡܘܗܒܬܐ ܕܡܥܠܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܡܢ ܗ̇ܝ ܩܕܡܝܬܐ ܡܛܠ ܚܦܝܼܛܘܬܗ ܘܬܝܒܘܬܗ ܕܠܘܬ ܐܠܗܐ. ܗܿܝ ܕܠܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܒܗ ܙܐܦܐ ܡܕܡ. ܗܘ ܓܝܪ ܕܡܛܠ ܫܘܒܚܐ ܘܩܘܠܣܐ ܕܡܢ ܒܢܝ̈ܢܫܐ ܝܿܬܒ ܒܩܠܝܬܐ. ܒܗܠܝܢ ܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܒܝ̈ܫܼܬܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܐ ܚܪܬܗ. ܗ̇ܘ ܓܝܪ :ܐܐ ܕܡܛܠ ܐܠܗܐ ܡܚܲܡܣܢ ܒܫܠܼܝܐ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܛܘ̈ܒܐ ܘܫܘܘ̈ܟܢܐ ܕܡܢ ܠܥܠ ܐܡܿܪܬ ܡܫܬܘܐ. ܘܠܘ ܒܠܚܘܕ
of vainglory and merited a gift that was superior to the first, because of his zeal and his repentance toward God, which contained no falsehood. Indeed, he who establishes himself in a cell for the glory and praise of men ends in these two misfortunes, for he who perseveres in solitude for God merits these goods and these favors that I have cited above, and not only those, but others even greater than those.
ܗܠܝܢ ܐܠܐ ܘܠܐܚܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܪܘܪܒܝܢ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܀ ܦܘܪ̈ܫܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܥܒ̇ܕܬ ܡܢ ܠܥܠ ܒܝܬ ܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܕܡܢ ܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܢ ܒܡܕܥܐ ܐܘ̇ ܡܢ ܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܟܝ̈ܢܝܐ ܐܘܿ̇ ܕܡܢ ܡܠܐܟܐ ܕܒܛܝܼܠܘܬܐ. ܘܡܛܠ ܕܢܦܿܩܬ ܡܠܬܢ ܒܬܪ ܢܝܼܫܐ ܕܣܝܼܡ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ̇ ܟܕ ܗܼܘ ܫܪܒܐ ܬ݁ܒܿܥ ܗܘܐ ܢܥܲܛܘܦ ܠܫܪܒܢ ܆ ܥܠ ܣܥܘܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܕܗ̇ܘܝܢ ܠܘܬ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܝ̇ܿܬܒܝܢ ܒܩ̈ܠܝܬܗܘܢ ܒܫܼܠܝܐ ܡܢ ܓܒܐ ܕܝܡܝܢܐ ܗܕܐ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܗܺܝ ܕܡܢ ܠܥܠ ܐܡܿܪܬ. ܥܡ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܨܒܿܐ ܐܢܼܐ ܕܐܓܠܐ ܐܢ̈ܝܢ ܒܟܬܝ̈ܒܬܐ ܕܕܠܡܐ ܐܬܠܼ ܐܝܼܕܐ ܒܗܕܐ ܠܡܬܥ̈ܕܠܢܐ, ܐܝܬ ܕܝܢ ܬܘܒ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܕܡܣܬܥܪ̈ܢ ܠܘܬ ܦ̈ܠܘܚܐ ܒܫܠܼܝܐ ܆ ܘܐܡܼܝܪ ܡܛܠܬܗܝܢ ܠܐܒܗ̈ܬܐ. ܒܪܡ ܕܝܢ ܐܘ̇ ܚܒܝܒ ܢܦܫܝ ܇ ܟܠ ܐܝܟܐ ܕܬܒ̇ܿܥܐ ܘܿܠܝܬܐ ܠܡܐܡܪ ܐܢܝ̈ܢ ܆ ܐܡܵܪܝ̣ܢܢ ܠܗܝܢ ܐܝܟ ܫܘܘܕܝܐ ܕܠܘܬܟ ܘܦܝܣܿܗ ܕܚܘܒܟ ܪܘܚܢܐ ܕܠܘܬ ܡܚܝܠܘܬܢ ܀
114 I have indeed established above a distinction between the intellections that come to the mind by grace, or by natural seeds, or by the Angel of Providence. And because, the subject requiring it, our discourse has gone out of the goal assigned to it, let us return to our subject: the action of the intellections coming from the right side, which take place in those who are established in their cells in solitude; it is of this that I spoke above, with many other things that I do not want to reveal in writing, for fear of giving a hand in this to detractors. But there are still other (actions) that take place in those practicing in solitude of which the Fathers have spoken. However, O beloved of my soul, wherever it will be appropriate to speak of them, we will speak of them, according to the promise we have made to you, and [(according to) the request of your spiritual love (made) to our weakness. Traps of the demon
ܫܡܼܥ ܕ݁ܶܝܢ ܗܫܐ ܐܘ ܐܚܝ ܆ ܐܦ ܥܠ ܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܡܢ ܫܐܕܐ ܗ̇ܘ ܠܝܼܛܐ܀ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܡܥܒܕܢܘܬܗܘܢ܀ ܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܚ̇ܙܐ ܗܢܐ ܒܥܠܕܪ̈ܐ ܒܝܼܫܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܫܬܥܒܕܐ ܢܦܫܐ ܠܚܫܐ ܢܕܝܕܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܐ ܕܡ̣ܢ ܠܥܠ ܐܡܿܪܬ ܇ ܗ̇ܝܕܝܢ ܡܫܪܐ ܘܩ̇ܪܒ ܨܝܕ ܢܦܫܼܐ ܘܡܲܪܡܐ ܒܗ̇ ܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܕܙܢܝ̈ܢ ܙܢܝ̈ܢ. ܘܡܲܒܥ ܚ̈ܕܬܬܐ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܡܬܡ̈ܗܢ ܠܫܡܘ̈ܥܐ ܘܡܢܗܘܢ ܕܣܘܟ̈ܠܘܗܝ. ܫܪܪ̈ܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܘܡܢܗܘܢ ܕܓ̈ܠܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܠܐ ܡܿܨܐ ܕܢܬܼܠ ܣܡܐ ܕܡܿܘܬܐ ܒܠܥܕ
115 Listen then now, O my brother, also to what concerns these intellections which are sown by this cursed demon, (namely) how their action is done. When, therefore, this abominable adversary sees that the soul does not submit to the filthy passion of fornication, as I said above, he then begins to approach the soul, to pour into it intellections of all kinds, and to make these novelties gush forth which are surprising to those who hear them: of these intellections, some are true, others false, for (the demon) cannot administer deadly poison without a drop of honey.
ܡܢ ܒܟܪܝܬܐ ܕܕܒܫܐ ܀ ܒܪܡ ܕܝܢ ܐܬܿܐ ܗܕܐ ܬܗܼܘܐ ܠܟ ܥܠ ܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܡܢ ܫܐܕܐ ܗܢܐ ܡܙܕܪ̈ܥܝܢ. ܡܐ ܕܢܿܦܠܝܢ ܒܠܒܐ ܘܡܩܲܒܠ ܠܗܘܢ ܡܕܥܐ ܡܬܡܠܐ ܠܒܐ ܚܘ̈ܫܒܐ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܦܗܼܝܐ ܡܫܬܠܛ ܒܗ. ܘܟܡܐ
However, for these intellections which are sown by this demon, let this serve you as a sign: when they fall into the heart and the spirit receives them, the heart is filled with a multitude of thoughts, for dissipation dominates it.


[p. 390] {#po.45.p390}
ܕܬܟܿܒܝܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܣ̈ܘܟܠܐ ܡܬܕܘܕ ܗܘܢܼܐ ܘܡܫܬܓܿܫܐ ܢܦܫܐ. ܘܒܠܝܠܼܝܢ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܛܟ̈ܣܐ ܘܢܡ̈ܘܣܐ܀ ܘܡܐ ܕܐܬܒܲܠܒܠܘ ܛܟܼ̈ܣܐ ܘܢܡ̈ܘܣܐ ܕܩܠܝܬܐ ܐܬܦܲܠܗܕ ܫܠܝܼܐ ܘܢܛܘܪ̈ܬܗ ܟܠܗܝܢ ܐܫܬܼܪ̈ܝ ܠܗܝܢ. ܐܬܡܠܼܝ ܠܒܐ ܕܠܘܚܼܝܐ ܘܫ̇ܚܩܐ܀ ܐܫܬܼܪ̈ܝ ܠܗ ܐܣܼܪܐ ܕܒܝܬ ܦܓܪܐ ܠܢܦܫܐ ܠܗܘܢܐ. ܘܗܘܼܘ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܨܒ̈ܝܢܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܦܓܪܐ ܪܐܶܓ ܡܕܡ ܕܢܿܟܐ ܠܢܦܫܐ. ܘܢܦܫܐ ܪܓܿܐ ܡܕܡ ܕܢܿܟܐ ܠܦܓܪܐ܀ ܘܬܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ
The more these intellections rush, (the more) the intellect is troubled and the soul agitated; all the rules and laws are disturbed; and once the rules and laws of the cell are troubled, the silence dissipates and all its observances are abolished. The heart is filled with trouble and torment, the bond between the body, the soul, and the intellect breaks, and there are two wills, because the body desires what harms the soul, and the soul desires what harms the body, and both are in opposition to one another.
ܣܩ̈ܘܒܠܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܚ̈ܕܕܐ ܀ ܘܡܐ ܕܗܢܘܢ ܥܡ ܚ̈ܕܕܐ ܡܬܟ̇ܬܫܝܢ ܥܒܪܬ ܚܕܘܬܐ ܗܿܝ ܕܡܢ ܐܡܝܢܼܘܬܐ ܕܥܡ̈ܠܐ ܕܡܝܬܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܒܦܓܪܐ ܘܒܢܦܫܐ܀ ܒܛܠ ܫܠܡܐ ܗܿܘ ܕܡܢ ܫܦܝܘܬܐ ܕܚ̈ܘܫܒܐ ܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܒܠܒܐ. ܘܡܢ ܚܕܐ ܝܕܥܬܐ ܝܚܝܕܝܬܐ ܕܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܗܝ ܕܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܡܒܘܥܐ ܕܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܘܕܚ̈ܝܐ. ܗܘܼܐ ܠܒܐ ܡܒܘܥܐ ܕܝ̈ܕܥܬܐ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܬܐ. ܗܠܝܢ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܝܢ ܚܫܘܟܐ ܕܛܘܥܝܝ ܘܡܢ ܦܫܝܼܛܘܬܐ ܕܚܘܫܒܐ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܩܢܼܐ ܨܢܝܼܥܘܬ ܚܘ̈ܫܒܐ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܐ ܥܡ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܣܓ̈ܝܐܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܥܠܝܗܼܝܢ ܐܠܨܐ ܕܢܐܡܿܪ ܀
And, when they are in struggle with one another, in him disappears that joy which he had in the body and in the soul following the constancy in the works of virtue; (and) it ceases, that peace which was in the heart, coming from the limpidity of thoughts, and, in place of the unique, unifying knowledge he had—that which is the source of light and life—the heart has become a source of multiple knowledges which are the darkness of error; and, instead of the simplicity of a unifying thought, he has acquired the deception of multiple thoughts, as well as many other things of which it is not necessary for us to speak.
ܗܢܐ ܝܕܥ̇ܐ ܕܥܕܡܐ ܠܗܫܐ ܣܿܡܢܢ ܠܐܝܢܐ ܕܠܝܬ ܠܗ ܚܙܬܐ ܕܩܢܘܡܗ ܒܥܕܢ ܨܠܘܬܐ ܣܡܢܝܗ. ܢܿܬܠ ܕܝܢ ܝܿܕܥܐ ܐܦ ܠܐܝܢܐ ܕܐܝܬ ܠܗ ܚܙܬܐ ܕܫܘܦܪܐ ܕܩܢܘܡܗ ܒܥܕܢ ܨܠܘܬܐ. ܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܩܿܐܡ ܐܢܬ ܒܥܕܢ ܨܠܘܬܐ. ܗܕܐ ܐܬܳܐ ܬܗܘܼܐ ܠܟ ܥܠ ܬܪܲܡܝܬܐ ܕܙܪܥܐ ܕܣ̈ܘܟܠܘܗܝ ܕܗܢܐ ܡܢܣܝܢܐ. ܐܘ ܚܲܙܝܐ ܕܩܘܫܬܐ. ܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܡܪܡܐ ܣ̈ܘܟܠܘܗܝ ܒܠܒܐ. ܢܦܫܐ ܡܫܬܚܠܦܐ ܡܢ ܬܐܘܖ̈ܝܐ ܕܩܝ̇ܡܐ ܒܗ̇ ܘܡܢ ܦܫܝܼܛܘܬܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܬܐ ܕܩܲܢܝܐ ܒܥܕܢ ܨܠܘܬܐ ܥܡ ܚܙܬܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܬܐ ܕܕܢܿܚܐ ܒܗ̇ ܘܩ̇ܦܣܐ ܝܼܬܗ̇ ܠܓܘ ܡܢ ܬܘ̈ܢܘܗܝ ܓ̈ܘܝܐ ܕܠܒܐ. ܥܕܡܐ ܕܡܛܫܐ ܡܕܥܐ ܫܘܦܪܗ ܒܥܕܢ ܨܠܘܬܐ. ܘܠܐ ܡܚܲܘܐ ܠܟ ܝܼܬܗ ܀
116 This sign (of knowledge) that we have established until now, we have established for him who does not have the vision of himself at the moment of prayer; but let us also give the sign (of knowledge) for him who has the vision of his own beauty at the moment of prayer. When, therefore, you rise at the moment of prayer, this will be for you the sign that this tempter is spreading the seed of his intellections, O seer of truth: when he spreads his intellections in the heart, the soul is transferred out of the contemplation in which it found itself, and out of the spiritual simplicity it possessed at the time of prayer, at the moment when the spiritual vision rose within it, and held its being within the inner dwellings of the heart, to the point that the spirit hid its own beauty at the moment of prayer, and did not show you its own being.
ܡܛܠ ܕܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܡܢ ܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܢ ܒܠܒܐ ܦܫܝܼܛܐ ܘܪܘܚܢܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܣܝ̇ܟܐ ܥ̇ܒܕܝܢ ܠܗ. ܗܠܝܢ ܕܝܢ ܕܡܢ ܗܘ ܠܝܼܛܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܢ ܡܢ ܦܫܝܼܛܘܬܐ ܠܡܪܟܒܘܬܐ ܡܲܝܬܝܢ ܠܗ. ܘܚܠܦ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܕܡܬܡܠܐ ܠܒܐ ܡܢ ܡܥܒܕܢܘܬܐ ܕܣ̈ܘܟܠܐ ܕܡܢ ܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܬܡܠܐ ܠܒܐ ܚܫܟܐ ܣܓܝܐܐ ܒܪܡ ܝܕܥܿܐ ܫܪܝܪܐ ܗܢܐ ܢܗܼܘܐ ܠܟ ܐܘ ܚܲܙܝܐ ܒܝܬ ܫܪܪ̈ܐ ܠܛܘܥܝܝ ܦܪܘܫܘܬܗ ܕܪ̈ܓܫܐ ܬܠܝܬܝܐ ܀
117 For the intellections that are in the heart by grace make it simple, spiritual, and infinite, while these (intellections) that come from this cursed one make it pass from simplicity to complexity; and, instead of the light with which the heart is filled by the action of the intellections (coming) from grace, the heart is filled with a thick darkness by those (of the demon). However, let this serve you as a true sign (of knowledge), O


[p. 392] {#po.45.p392}
ܡܛܠ ܕܒܗܢܐ ܩܪܒܐ ܗܿܢܐ ܪܓܫܼܐ ܝܬܝܪ ܩܢܐ ܚܲܝܠܐ ܠܡܦܼܪܫ ܒܝܬ ܫܪܪ̈ܐ ܠܛܘܥܝܐ. ܕܥܡ ܕܡܫܿܪܝܢ ܣ̈ܘܟܠܐ ܕܡܢ ܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܡܿܥܒܕܝܢ ܒܠܒܐ ܪܝܼܚܐ ܗܿܘ ܗܼܢܝܐܐ ܥ̇ܛܪ ܘܢ̇ܦܩ ܡܢ ܓܘܗ ܕܠܒܐ ܒܕܡܼܘܬ ܡܒܘܥܐ ܕܡ̈ܝܐ. ܘܠܙܘ̈ܥܐ ܕܦܓܪܐ ܘܕܢܦܫܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܫܼܢܬܐ ܗܟܢܐ ܡܿܛܒܥ ܠܗܘܢ ܒܗܢܝܐܘܬܗ. ܡܛܠ ܕܒܗܕܐ ܛܥ̣ܡܬܐ ܕܗܢܐ ܒܘܿܣܡܐ ܬܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܡܫܘܬܦܝܢ ܦܓܪܐ ܘܢܦܫܐ. ܣܘܟ̈ܠܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܡܢ ܗܿܘ ܠܝܼܛܐ ܡܙܕܪ̈ܥܝܢ ܥܡ ܕܡܫ̇ܪܝܢ ܡܿܥܼܒܕܝܢ ܪ̈ܝܼܚܐ ܣܼܪܝܐ ܘܢܕܝܕܐ ܓܶܣܐ ܘܢܿܦܩ ܡܢ ܠܒܐ. ܕܡܢ ܣܪܝܘܬܗ ܡܫܬܓܿܫܝܢ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܡܘ̈ܙܓܐ ܕܦܓܪܐ ܘܕܢܦܫܐ ܀
seer, between truth and error: the discernment (coming) from the third sense; for, in this struggle, it is mainly this sense that possesses the power to discern truth from error; because, at the same time as the intellections (coming) from grace begin to act in the heart, this sweet odor is released and comes out from the inside of the heart like a spring of water, and thus plunges the movements of the body and the soul into its sweetness as if into a sleep, for, to taste these delights, the soul and the body associate both together. On the other hand, the intellections sown by the cursed one, as soon as they begin to operate, a foul and filthy odor gushes and comes out of the heart; and, by its infection, all the relations of the body and the soul are troubled. Reminder of the remedies
ܐܠܐ ܐܢܬ ܗܟܝܠ ܐܘܿ ܦ̇ܠܘܚܐ ܆ ܟܡܐ ܕܗܢܐ ܩܪܒܐ ܡܩܪܒ ܥܡܟ ܠܐ ܬܫܒܘܩ ܠܚܘܫܒܟ ܕܢܼܗܪܘܓ ܒܣܘܟܠܐ ܡܕܡ. ܐܠܐ ܐܩܝܼܡܝܗܝ ܠܗܘܢܟ ܐܝܟ ܟܐܦܐ ܕܠܐ ܪ̈ܓܫܼܬܐ. ܘܐܝܟ ܚܲܪܫܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܡܿܠܠ. ܘܬܗܼܘܐ ܠܟ ܐܡܝܼܢܘܬܐ ܕܩܪܝܢܐ ܘܕܣܿܝܡ ܒܘܪ̈ܟܐ ܘܕܓܘܪ̈ܓܚܐ ܕܩܕܡ ܨܠܝܒܐ. ܘܚܫܼܐ ܘܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܕܬܘܼܬ ܢܦܫܐ. ܘܥܒܿܪ
118 As for you, O practicing one, as long as this combat takes place with you, do not let your thought meditate on any intellection whatsoever, but hold your intellect like an insensible stone and like a mute (man) who does not speak. Be assiduous in reading, in genuflections, and in prostrations before the cross; in the pain and tears of repentance of the soul; and, by the strength of the help of Our Lord, (the combat) will cease for you.
ܡܢܟ. ܒܚܝܼܠ ܥܘܕ̈ܪܢܗ ܕܡܪܢ ܀ ܡܢ ܒܬ ܕܝܢ ܬܟܬܘܼܫܗ ܕܗܢܐ ܩܪܒܐ ܕܥܡ ܗܘܢܐ ܡܬܝܿܗܒܐ ܠܗ ܦ̇ܪܘܫܘܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܐ. ܥܡ ܝܕܘ̈ܥܬܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܒܗܢܐ ܙܢܼܐ ܕܣ̈ܘܟܠܐ ܡ̇ܿܩܪܒ ܥܡܗܘܢ ܫܐܕܐ ܗܢܐ. ܥܡ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܝܢ ܕܠܐ ܩܢܝܢ ܝܕܥܬܐ ܒܛܟܼܣܐ ܗܿܘ ܕܙܡܝܖ̈ܬܐ ܫܟ̣ܝܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܐܡܼܪܝܢ ܡܢ ܠܥܠ ܡܿܩܪܒ
On the other hand, after the combat of this struggle with the intellect, the discernment of spirits is given to him. For, against people who have knowledge, it is with the help of this kind of intellections that this demon makes war. But against those who have not acquired knowledge, it is by the tactic of obscene songs, of which we spoke above, that he struggles with them.
ܥܡܗܘܢ ܀ ܗܿܘ ܓܝܪ ܕܨܿܒܐ ܕܢܐ̈ܠܦ ܐܘܡܢܘܬܐ ܕܟܠܗܘܢ ܩܪ̈ܒܐ ܕܡܿܩܪܒܝܢ ܥܡ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܝ̇ܬܒܝܢ ܒܫ̣ܠܝܬܐ ܢܛܪ ܚܘܫ̈ܒܘܗܝ ܒܙܗܼܝܪܘ. ܘܠܐ ܢܥܼܒܕ ܡܕܡ ܕܠܐ ܡܠܟܼܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܠܬܐ ܕܐܒ̈ܗܝܢ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ. ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܕܝ̇ܿܬܒ ܒܫܼܠܝܐ ܘܢ̇ܛܪ ܚܘ̈ܫܒܘܗܝ ܣܗܕܐ ܗܘ ܒܚܼܝܪܐ. ܘܟܠܝܠܐ ܕܫܼܘܒܚܐ ܥܡ ܣ̈ܗܕܐ ܡܬܬܣܼܝܡ ܠܗ ܒܗܿܘ ܝܘܡܐ ܕܡܬܓܠܐ ܡܪܢ ܥܡ ܚܲܝܠܐ ܘܫܼܘܒܚܐ ܕܡܠܐܟ̈ܘܗܝ ܩܕܝ̈ܫܐ.
Let him indeed who wants to learn the strategy of all the combats that those who establish themselves in solitude have to undergo, let him guard his thoughts with attention and do nothing without (taking) counsel, in accordance with the word of our spiritual Fathers. The monk who establishes himself in solitude and guards his thoughts is a proven martyr, and a crown of glory at the same time as the martyrs will be given to him on the day when Our Lord will manifest Himself with the power and glory of his holy angels.
ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܝܿܬܒ ܒܫܼܠܝܐ ܥܠ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܝܬܪ̈ܬܐ ܣܢܼܝܩ. ܥܠ ܢܛܘܖܪܬܐ ܕܫܬܩܼܐ ܕܦܓܪܐ ܘܥܠ ܕܚܘ̈ܫܒܐ ܕܢܦܫܐ. ܘܥܠ ܨܘܡܐ ܐܡܝܢܐ ܕܡܫܘܚܬܐ. ܘܥܠ ܡܟܝܼܟܘܬܼܐ ܘܥܠ ܚܘܒܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܐܣܒ ܒܐ̈ܦܐ. ܘܥܠܗܝܿ ܕܠܐ ܢܗܼܘܐ ܠܗ ܡܿܦܩܐ ܘܡܿܥܠܐ ܘܥܢܝܼܢܐ ܥܡ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܐ ܣܛܪ
119 The monk who establishes himself in solitude needs the following virtues: the guarding of the silence of the body and (that) of the thoughts of the soul; continual fasting with measure; humility and charity without hypocrisy; the fact of not going and coming nor frequenting many people, with the exception of one


[p. 394] {#po.45.p394}
ܡܢ ܚܕ ܐܢܫ ܕܢܓܼܠܐ ܠܗ ܚܘܫ̈ܒܘܗܝ ܘܢܣܒ ܡܢܗ ܡܠܼܟܐ ܥܠ ܦܘܠܚܢ ܡܝܬܪ̈ܬܐ ܀
(man) to whom he will reveal his thoughts and from whom he will take counsel regarding the exercise of virtues.
ܙܕܩܿ ܠܗ ܗܟܝܠ ܠܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܕܝܿܬܒ ܒܫܼܠܝܐ ܒܩܠܝܬܗ ܕܠܐ ܢܿܟܣܐ ܚܘܫ̈ܒܘܗܝ ܡܢ ܗܿܘ ܕܠܗ ܗܝܼܡܢ ܢܦܫܗ. ܘܠܐ ܢܥܒܕ ܨܒܘ ܕܠܐ ܡܠܟܼܐ. ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܕܝܿܬܒ ܒܫܼܠܝܐ ܘܠܐ ܓܿܠܐ ܚܘ̈ܫܒܗ ܩܕܡ ܐܒܘ̈ܗܝ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܕܗܢܐ ܣܪܹܝܩ ܗܘ ܥܡܠܼܗ. ܘܟܠܗ̇ ܬܐܓܘܖܬܗ ܒܐܝ̈ܕܐ ܕܒܥܠܕܒ̈ܒܘܗܝ ܣܝܼܡܐ. ܗܿܘ ܕܝܢ ܕܗܟܢܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܕܡܐ ܠܗܿܘ ܕܒܚܕܐ ܐܝܼܕܗ ܒܿܢܐ ܘܒܐܚܪܬܐ ܣ̇ܬܪܿ. ܘܠܘ ܣܿܓܝ ܡܿܓܪ ܒܗ ܒܫܠܼܝܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܠܐ ܠܘܼܐܐ ܠܗ ܚܲܝܠܐ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܕܡܟܝܟܘܬܐ ܇ ܘܙܝܢܐ ܕܨܠܘܬܐ ܕܐܒ̈ܘܗܝ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܀
It is therefore necessary that the monk who establishes himself in a cell in solitude does not hide his thoughts from the one to whom he has entrusted his soul, and does not do anything without counsel. The monk who establishes himself in solitude and does not reveal his thoughts before his spiritual fathers, his work is vain and all his commerce is placed in the hands of his enemies: certainly, he who (acts) thus is like him who builds with one hand and demolishes with the other. He will not remain long in solitude, for the holy strength of humility does not accompany him, nor the armor of the prayers of the spiritual fathers.
ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܕܓܿܠܐ ܚܘܫ̈ܒܘܗܝ ܒܫ̣ܠܝܐ ܕܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܕܡܐ ܠܩܪܒܬܢܐ ܕܡܚܲܙܩ ܒܟܠܗܘܢ ܡ̈ܐܢܝ ܩܪܒܐ ܘܠܐ ܕܚܿܠ ܡܢ ܣܕܪ̈ܐ ܕܒܥܠܕܒ̈ܒܐ ܘܢܣܒ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܙܟܘܬܐ ܘܡܚܼܲܕܐ ܠܗ̇ܘ ܕܙܲܝܢܝܗ ܘܫܕܪܗ ܠܩܪܒܐ. ܗܿܘ ܕܝܢ ܕܝܿܬܒ ܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܘܡܛܫܐ ܚܘܫ̈ܒܘܗܝ ܘܠܐ ܢܣܒ ܡܠܟܼܐ ܥܠ ܦܘܠܚܢ ܡܝܬܪ̈ܬܐ. ܕܡܿܐ ܠܗ̇ܘ ܕܗ̇ܘ ܕܩܿܐܡ ܒܣܕܪ̈ܐ ܕܒܥ̈ܠܕܒܒܐ ܕܠܐ ܙܝܢܳܐ ܘܕܠܐ ܡ̈ܐܢܝ ܩܪܒܐ. ܕܥܕ ܨܒܿܐ ܠܡܩܪܒܘ ܒܿܠܥ ܓܐܪ̈ܐ ܡܢ ܒܥ̈ܠܕܒܒܐ ܘܗܘ̇ܐ ܓܘܚܟܐ ܘܣܘܪ̈ܕܐ ܠܟܠܗܘܢ ܚܙܝ̈ܘܗܝ ܀
As for the monk who reveals his thoughts (to his fathers) in the solitude of the cell, he is like a combatant equipped with all the weapons of war; he does not fear the line of enemies; he snatches victory from them and he rejoices the one who equipped him and sent him to combat. But he who establishes himself in a cell, hides his thoughts, and does not take counsel for the exercise of virtues is like him who stands facing the enemy front without equipment and without combat weapons, so that, while he wants to fight, he is riddled with arrows by the enemies and becomes a subject of mockery and terror for all those who see him.

### Resorting to a guide and obeying him
ܐܠܐ ܐܢܬ ܗܟܝܠ ܐܘ̇ ܐܚܘܢ ܡܐ ܕܝ̇ܬܒ ܐܢܬ ܒܫܼܠܝܐ ܒܩܠܝܬܐ. ܟܠ ܚܘܫܒܐ ܕܗ̇ܘܐ ܠܟ ܐܸܢ ܕܝܡܝܢܐ ܘܐܸܢ ܕܣܡܼܠܐ ܩܪܒܝܗܝ ܠܩܕܡ ܡܫܒܠܝܟ. ܘܗܘ ܡܕܡ ܕܐܡܿܪ ܠܟ ܥܒܕ ܒܚܦܼܝܛܘ. ܟܕ ܠܐ ܣܿܛܐ ܐܢܬ ܡܢܗ ܠܐ ܠܝܡܝܢܐ ܘܠܐ ܠܣܡܠܐ. ܘܚܼܙܝ ܕܠܡܐ ܡܐ ܕܢܿܣܒ ܐܢܬ ܡܼܠܟܐ ܡܢ ܡܫܒܠܢܟ ܠܐ ܬܫܼܠܡ ܠܡܠܼܟܐ ܕܢܣܒ ܐܢܬ ܘܗܿܘܝܐ ܚܪܬܟ ܐܝܟ ܗܺܝ ܕܥܿܟܪ ܘܕܓܝܚܙܝ ܘܕܣܟܪܝܘܛܐ ܇ ܕܠܐ ܐܫܬܡܼܥܘ ܠܡܠܼܟܐ ܕܪ̈ܒܢܝܗܘܢ. ܘܛܲܫܹܝܘ ܠܙܪܥܐ ܒܝܼܫܐ ܕܙܪ̈ܥܼ ܒܗܘܢ ܣܛܢܐ ܒܠܒ̈ܘܬܗܘܢ. ܘܦܘܪ̈ܥܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܫܬܡܥܢܘܬܐ ܕܙܿܕܩ ܗܘܐ
120 But you then, O our brother, when you establish yourself in solitude in a cell, every thought that you have, whether from the right or from the left, present it before your guide and do with diligence what he tells you, without deviating from it either to the right or to the left. Watch that, when you take counsel from your guide, you do not betray the counsel that you receive, and that your end is not like that of Achar, of Gehazi, and of the Iscariot who did not obey the counsel of their masters, hid the bad seed that Satan had sown in them in their hearts, and received in themselves, for their disobedience, the retribution that befits their error.
ܠܛܥܝܬܗܘܢ. ܒܩܢܘܡܗܘܢ ܩܲܒܠܘܗܝ ܀ ܐܠܐ ܐܢܬ ܐܘ̇ ܐܚܐ ܕܝ̇ܬܒ ܒܫܼܠܝܐ ܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܐܫܬܡܼܥ ܠܪ̈ܒܢܝܟ ܡܛܠ ܐܠܗܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܫܬܡܼܥܘ ܫ̈ܠܝܚܐ ܠܪܒܗܘܢ ܫܪܝܪܐ ܘܩܲܘܝܘ ܒܐܘܪ̈ܫܠܡ ܐܟ ܕܦܲܩܕ ܐܢܘܢ ܇ ܕܐܢܬܘܢ ܩܘܘ ܒܓܘ ܐܘܪ̈ܫܠܡ ܇ ܥܕܡܐ ܕܬܠܒܼܫܘܢ ܚܲܝܠܐ ܡܢ ܪ̈ܘܡܐ. ܘܩ̇ܘܝܘ ܐܝܟ ܦܘܩܕܢܗ ܒܐܘܪܫܠܡ ܥܕܡܐ ܕܩܲܒܠܘ ܚܲܝܠܐ ܡܢ ܪܘܡܐ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܠܫ̈ܢܐ ܕܢܘܪܐ
121 But you, O brother who establish yourself in solitude in a cell, obey your masters because of God, as the apostles obeyed their true master and remained in Jerusalem as He had ordered them: "You, remain in Jerusalem until you are clothed with the strength from on high." And they remained according to his command in Jerusalem until they had received


[p. 396] {#po.45.p396}
ܘܗܘܘ ܟܪ̈ܘܙܐ ܕܡܿܠܟܘܬ ܫܡܝܐ ܒܐܪ̈ܒܥ ܦܢܝ̈ܬܗ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܐܝܟ ܦܘܩܕܢܗ ܕܪ̈ܒܗܘܢ ܕܠܘܬܗܘܢ. ܕܙܠܼܘ ܬܠܡܼܕܘ ܠܟܠܗܘܢ ܥܡ̈ܡܐ ܘܐܥܡܕܘ ܐܢܘܢ ܒܫܡ ܐܒܐ ܘܒܪܐ ܘܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܩܘܕܫܐ. ܘܗܐ ܐܢܐ ܥܡܟܘܢ ܐܢܼܐ ܥܕܡܐ ܠܫܘܠܡܗ ܕܥܠܡܐ. ܗܢܐ ܫܘܕܝܐ ܕܥܼܒܕ ܡܪܢ ܠܘܬ ܬܠܡܝ̈ܕܘܗܝ ܩܕܝ̈ܫܐ ܠܘ ܠܘܬܗܘܢ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܥܼܒܕܗ ܐܠܐ ܐܦ ܠܘܬ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܪ̈ܕܝ̈ܢ ܒܥܼܩ̈ܒܬܗܘܢ ܇ ܘܡܫܬܡܥܝܢ ܠܡܠܼܟܐ ܕܪ̈ܒܢܝܗܘܢ ܐܟܘܬܗܘܢ ܆ ܕܗ̇ܘܐ ܥܡܗܘܢ ܡܪܢ ܘܝ̇ܗܒ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܩܘܕܫܐ ܒܠܒ̈ܘܬܗܘܢ ܗܿܝ ܕܡܿܠܦܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܠܥܼ̈ܙܐ ܚ̈ܕܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܡܿܠܠܝܢ ܘܪ̈ܺܐܙܐ ܕܡܬܓܠܝܢ ܒܗܿܘ ܠܫܢܐ ܕܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܡܩܲܒܠܝܢ ܡܢ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܩܘܕܫܐ ܇ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܝܿܬܒܝܢ ܒܫܠܝܐ ܒܩ̈ܠܝܬܗܘܢ ܘܝܿܨܦܝܢ ܕܦܘܠܚܢ ܡܝܬܪ̈ܬܐ ܒܝܕܥܬܐ ܀
the power from on high in the form of tongues of fire. And they became the heralds of the kingdom of heaven to the four corners of the world, according to the command of their master concerning them: Go, make disciples of all nations and baptize them in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit... And behold, I am with you... until the consummation of the world. This promise that Our Lord made to his holy disciples, He did not make only to them, but also to all those who walk in their footsteps and who, like them, obey the counsel of their masters, because Our Lord is with them and bestows the Holy Spirit upon their hearts, He who teaches them new, ineffable languages, and mysteries which are revealed by this tongue of fire received from the Holy Spirit by those who establish themselves in solitude in their cells and apply themselves with knowledge to the exercise of the virtues.
ܗܘܿ ܕܝܢ ܕܗܿܘܐ ܥܡܗ ܩܪܒܐ ܘܡܫܬܡܼܥ ܠܣܘ̈ܟܠܘܗܝ ܕܒܝܫܐ ܇ ܘܠܐ ܓܿܠܐ ܘܡܦܪܣܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܠܚܘܫ̈ܒܘܗܝ ܩܕܡ ܐܒ̈ܗܘܗܝ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܡܦܣܝܢ ܒܐܘܡܢܘܬܐ ܕܩܪ̈ܒܐ ܡܬܝܕܥ̈ܢܐ ܆ ܒܫܘܠܡܐ ܕܐܓܘܢܗ ܠܫܐܕܐ ܕܪ̈ܡܘܬܐ ܡܫܬܠܸܡ. ܘܡܢ ܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܕܫܪܪ̈ܐ ܫ̇ܕܐ ܠܗ ܘܪܝܫܐ ܥܿܒܕ ܠܗ ܠܗܪ̈ܣܝܣ ܛܡ̈ܐܬܐ. ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܗܼܘ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܗܝܢ ܪܝܫܐ
122 But he who undergoes a struggle, who obeys the intellections (suggested) by the Evil One, who does not reveal and does not lay bare his thoughts before his fathers who know the tactics of "spiritual" struggles, (that person) is delivered, at the end of his struggle, to the demon of pride; and (this one) makes him fall from the true faith, to make of him the instigator of pernicious heresies of which he becomes the leader and the corrupt instructor.
ܘܡܿܠܦܢܐ ܕܙܐܦܐ܀܀ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܕܝܢ ܚܟܝܼܡܐ: ܐܝܢܐ ܕܗ̇ܘܐ ܥܡܗ ܩܪܒܐ ܘܓܿܠܐ ܠܗ ܩܕܡ ܐܒܗ̈ܘܗܝܿ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܡܢ ܒܬܖ̈ ܬܟܬܘܫܗ ܕܥܡ ܗܢܐ ܩܪܒܐ ܇ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܘܗ̈ܒܬܐ ܡܬܝ̈ܗܒܢ ܠܗ ܡܢ ܛܝܒܘܬܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ. ܗ̇ܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܡܘܗܒܬܐ ܕܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܕܥܠ ܪܺ̈ܐܙܐ ܕܡܣܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܒܟ̈ܬܒܐ ܩܕܝ̈ܫܐ. ܕܐܝܟ ܫܼܡܫܐ ܗܟܢܐ ܕܢܝܼܚܝܢ ܩܕܡܘܗܝ ܣܘ̈ܟܠܝܗܘܢ. ܚܕܘܬܐ ܕܠܝܬ ܠܗ̇ ܣܟܐ ܆ ܘܠܐ ܡܨܐ ܡܕܥܐ ܡܣܲܝܒܪ ܠܗܿ. ܟܢܝܫܼܘܬܐ ܕܚܘ̈ܫܒܐ. ܕܣܛܪ ܡܢ ܥܘܗܕܢܝܐ ܕܚܘܒܐ ܕܪ̈ܚܡܬܗ ܕܦܪܘܩܢ ܠܐ ܗ̇ܘܐ ܒܠܒܗ ܕܒܪܢܫܐ. ܦܪܘܫܘܬܐ ܩܕܝܫܬܐ ܇ ܐܝ̇ܕܐ ܕܦܿܪܫܐ ܒܝܬ ܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܡܢ ܟܝܢܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܡܢ ܡܠܐܟܐ ܕܒܛܝܠܘܬܐ ܠܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܡܢ ܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܫ̇ܘܚܝܢ ܠܗ̇ܿܢܘܢ ܕܡܢ ܕܠܩܘܒܠܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܢ. ܥܡ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܐܿܠܨܐ
On the other hand, the wise monk who undergoes a struggle and reveals it to his spiritual fathers, after the struggle that has taken place in this combat, here are the gifts that are granted to him by the grace of God, namely the gift of intellections concerning the mysteries that are hidden in the holy Books, to the point that their intellections, like a sun, are made manifest before him in this way: a boundless joy that the spirit cannot bear; the recollection of thoughts which does not exist in the heart of man without the memory of the merciful charity of our Savior; the holy discernment which distinguishes between intellections—(on the one hand) those which are granted by nature, those which are by the Angel of Providence, and those which germinate by grace; (on the other hand) those which come from the adversary—as well as many others (discernments) that it is not necessary to mention. Struggles with other demons
ܕܢܐܡܪ ܐܢܝ̈ܢ ܀ ܗܿܘܐ ܕܝܢ ܩܪܒܐ ܐܚܪܢܐ ܥܡ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܝܿܬܒܝܢ ܒܫܼܠܝܐ ܒܩܠܝܬܼܐ ܗܟܢܐ. ܗܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܫܐܕܐ ܡܨܛܕܝܢܐ ܡܬܩܪܐ ܡܢ ܚ̈ܟܝܡܐ ܕܪܘܚ. ܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܡܫܪܐ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܢܩܲܪܒ ܕܒܝܚܐ ܕܨ̈ܠܘܬܗ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܐܘܿ ܕܢܿܣܒ ܚܠܝܘܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܣܘܟܠܐ ܕܩܪܝܢܐ ܐܬܐ ܗܢܐ ܡܢܣܝܢܐ ܘܩ̇ܐܡ ܠܒܪ ܡܢ ܩܠܝܬܐ. ܘܐܝܟ ܪܟܫܐ
123 There exists yet another struggle for those who establish themselves in solitude in a cell, here it is: it is the demon that the spiritual sages call the mocker. When the monk begins to make his arrangements as if to offer the sacrifice of his prayers to God, or to gather the sweetness of the intellection of reading, this tempter comes and stands outside the


[p. 398] {#po.45.p398}
ܣܓܝܐܐ ܡܒܲܥܛ ܘܡܲܫܡܥ ܩܠܗ ܚܕܪ̈ܝ ܩܠܝܬܗ ܕܝܚܝܕܝܐ. ܐܦ ܥܠ ܬܪ̈ܥܐ ܢܿܩܫ. ܐܝܟ ܕܢܿܣܒܪ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܕܐܢ̈ܫܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܐ ܕܐܬܼܘ ܠܚܙܬܗ. ܕܡܐ ܕܡܫܼܬܡܥ ܠܗ ܘܢ̇ܦܩ ܠܡܼܚܙܐ ܡܒܲܛܠ ܠܗ ܡܢ ܥܢܝܢܐ ܫܦܼܝܪܐ ܕܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܀ ܐܢܕܝܢ ܡܚܲܡܣܢ ܐܚܐ ܒܦܘܠܚܢܗ ܘܠܐ ܢܿܦܩ ܠܒܪ ܡܫܚܠܦ ܠܗ̇ ܠܐܘܡܢܘܬܗܼ ܠܘܬ ܐܚܪܢܐ. ܗ̇ܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܡܲܪܥܠ ܠܗܝܢ ܠܐܣ̈ܐ ܕܩܠܝܬܐ ܇ ܐܦ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܩܿܐܡ ܥܠܝܗ̇ ܐܚܐ ܘܨܲܪ̈ܐ ܘܡܙܝܼܥ ܠܗ ܠܬܛܠܝܠܗ ܕܒܝܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܗܿܘ ܕܙܘܥܐ ܪܖ̈ܒܐ ܗܼܘܐ. ܘܡܿܠܟ ܠܗ ܠܐܚܐ. ܕܐܢ ܠܐ ܥܿܪܩ ܐܢܬ ܘܢ̇ܦܩ ܐܢܬ ܠܒܪ ܒܪ ܫܥܬܗ ܩܠܝܬܟ ܩܒܪܟ
cell: he prances like a multitude of horses that paw the ground and make their neighing heard around the monk's cell. He even knocks at the door, as if to make the monk believe that many people have come to see him, so that if he obeys him and goes out to see, (the demon) deprives him of the important occupation to which he was applying himself. 124 But if the brother perseveres in his exercise and does not go outside, (the demon) changes tactics for another, which is this: he shakes the walls of the cell, as well as the ground on which the brother is; and he splits and shakes the roof of the house as if there were a great earthquake. And he gives this advice to the brother: "If you do not flee and go outside, this very instant your cell will become your tomb."
ܗܘܝܐ. ܐܢ ܕܝܢ ܡܚܲܡܣܢ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܘܠܐ ܢܿܦܩ ܠܒܪ ܡܢ ܒܝܬ ܐܓܘܢܗ ܒܬܪ ܗܢܐ ܩܪܒܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܐ ܠܘܬܗ ܕܐܚܐ ܣܥܘܖ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܛܝܒܘܬܐ. ܗܿܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܕܝܿܩܕ ܠܒܗ :ܐܝܟ ܕܒܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܒܪܚܡܬ ܫܠܝܐ ܕܩܠܝܬܐ. ܘܐܡܝܢܘܬܐ ܕܣܿܝܡ ܒܘܪ̈ܟܐ ܘܪ̈ܚܼܡܬܐ ܕܬܢܼܝܐ ܕܡܙܡܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܦܣܩܐ. ܘܩܪܝܢܝܐ ܐܡܝܢܐ. ܗ̇ܿܘ ܕܦܪܚܐ ܚܠܝܘܬܐ. ܕܣܘ̈ܟܠܘܗܝ ܒܚܟܗ ܕܡܕܥܐ ܐܝܟ ܟܟܪܝܬܐ ܕܕܒܫܐ ܕܡܚܠܝܐ ܠܚܟܼܗ ܕܦܓܪܐ.
If the monk persists and does not go out of the dwelling of his arena, then, after this struggle, there will take place in the brother the visitation of grace, that is to say, his heart will burn like fire with the love of the solitude of the cell, of assiduity in genuflections, and of the love of the uninterrupted recitation of psalms and of continuous reading, which will spread the sweetness of its intellections to the palate of his spirit, like the honeycomb which gives a sweet flavor to the palate of the body.
ܗܢܐ ܩܪܒܐ ܚܒܪܘܬܗ ܥܡ ܫܐܕܐ ܕܩܘܛܥܐ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܇ ܐܦ ܦܗܝܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܚܘ̈ܫܒܐ ܘܩ̈ܠܐ ܦܚ̈ܝܼܚܐ ܕܡܫܬܡܥܝܢ ܒܐܐܪ ܐܡܪܝܢ ܕܗܘ ܡܲܥܒܕ ܠܗܘܢ. ܘܗܘܝܘ ܕܩ̇ܪܐ ܠܐ̈ܚܐ ܒܠܝ̈ܠܘܬܐ ܘܡܩܼܝܡ ܠܗܘܢ ܠܡܙܡܪ ܘ ܐܝܟ ܕܟܬܝܼܒ ܒܐܒ̈ܗܬܐ ܀
125 This struggle puts him in relation with the demon of acedia; as for the wandering of thoughts and the dissolute voices that are heard in the air, it is said that it is he who produces them and who calls the brothers during the night and makes them rise to chant, as it is written among the Fathers.
ܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܗܢܐ ܩܪܶܒܐ ܗܘܐ ܥܡܗ ܕܐܚܐ ܒܡܘܬܒܐ ܕܫܠܝܐ ܕܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܇ 'ܫܐܕܐ ܕܕܚܼܠܬܐ'. ܕܥܕܡܐ ܠܪܚܫܐ ܫܝܼܛܐ ܐܢ ܪܲܚ݇ܫ ܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܐܘܿ ܐܢ ܕܒܒܼܐ ܙܐܿܡ ܪܥܠ ܠܒܗ ܕܝܚܝܕܝܐ. ܐܝܟ ܐܢܫ ܕܦܠܸܛ ܡܢ ܐܝܕܝ ܠܣ̈ܛܝܐ. ܗܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܫܐܕܢܐ ܠܝܼܛܐ ܡܚܲܘܐ ܢܦܫܗ ܙܒ̈ܢܝܢ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܝܢ ܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܒܕܡ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܚ̈ܝܼܠܬܐ. ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܡܐ ܕܚ̈ܙܐ ܠܗܝܢ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܇ ܡܢ ܕܚܝܠܘܬܐ ܕܚܙܬܗܝܢ ܀ ܟܠܗ ܓܘܫܡܗ ܕܒܪܢܫܐ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܡܒܘܥܐ ܕܡ̈ܝܐ ܗ̇ܘܐ. ܘܥܕܡܐ ܠܗܕܐ ܡܲܡܠܟܐ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܕܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܇ ܥܕܡܐ ܕܡܬܼܬܒܪ ܠܫܢܗ ܒܦܘܡܗ ܘܠܐ ܡܨܐ ܕܢܡܿܠܠ. ܘܐܠܘ ܠܐ ܪ̈ܚܡܘܗܝ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܕܣܿܡܟܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܗ ܠܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܒܗܢܐ ܩܪܒܐ: ܟܒܪ ܐܦ ܢܦܫܗ ܡܫܢܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܡܢ ܦܓܪܗ.
After this struggle, there is with the brother sojourning in solitude in a cell the demon of fear, to the point that, at the vile reptile that comes crawling into the cell, or at the slightest gnat that buzzes, the monk's heart trembles, like someone who has escaped from the hands of brigands. Then this cursed demon manifests himself several times in the cell under terrifying forms, at the sight of which (in) the monk, through the fear of their vision, the entire body of the man becomes like a spring of water. (Fear) reigns to such a point over the monk that his tongue is crushed in his mouth and he cannot speak. And if the mercy of God did not sustain the monk in this struggle, perhaps his soul would even leave his body!


[p. 400] {#po.45.p400}

### Remedies: psalms and signs of the cross
ܐܠܐ ܐܢܬ ܐܘ̇ ܐܚܐ ܕܐܝܬ ܥܡܗ ܗܢܐ ܩܪܒܐ: ܡܐ ܕܚܿܙܐ ܐܢܬ ܠܗܝܢ ܠܕܡ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܚܿܝܼܠܬܐ ܆ ܩܘܫ ܒܩܲܝܬܪܗ ܕܒܪ ܐܝܫܝ ܟܕ ܐܡܿܪ ܐܢܬ ܗܟܢܐ: ܢܩܘܡ ܠܡ ܐܠܗܐ ܘܢܬܒܿܕܪܘܢ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܒܥܠܕܒ̈ܒܘܗܝ. ܘܢܲܥܪܩܘܢ ܣ̈ܢܐܝܟ ܡܢ ܩܕܡܝܟ ܘܐܝܟ ܫܥܘܬܐ ܕܡܬܦ̇ܿܫܪܐ ܡܢ ܩܕܡ ܚܡܝܡܘܬܗܿ ܕܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܇ ܢܬܛܲܠܩܘܢ ܒܥ̈ܠܕܒܒܝܟ ܡܢ ܕܡ ܘܠܐ ܬܬܼܠ ܡܪܝܐ ܪ̈ܓܬܗ ܠܥܿܘܠܐ. ܘܬܪ̈ܥܝܬܗ ܒܝܼܫܬܐ ܠܐ ܬܣܼܩ ܠܪܝܫܗ. ܘܐܠܗܐ ܦܨܢܝ ܡܪܝܐ ܠܥܘܕܪ̈ܢܝ ܟܬܪ. ܢܒܼܗܬܘܢ ܘܢܚ̇ܦܪܘܢ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܒܿܥܝܢ ܠܡܿܘܒܕܘܬܗ̇ ܠܢܦܫܝ. ܘܦܨܢܝ ܡܪܝܐ ܡܢ ܐܝܼܕܗ ܕܪ̈ܫܝܥܐ. ܘܐܠܦ ܡܪܝܐ ܐܝܕܝ ܘܐ ܠܩܪܒܐ. ܪܘܚܲܕܢܝ ܒܟܘܪܩܢܟ ܘܐܒܗܬ ܠܣܺܐܢܝ ܒܥܘܢܪܟ. ܘܨܠܝ ܡܪܝܐ ܐܕܢܟ ܘܥܢܝܢܝ. ܘܢܗܦܘܟ ܥܘܠܗ ܕܣܢܐܐ ܒܪܝܫܗ. ܘܕܚܼܠܬܐ ܘܙܘܥܼܬܐ ܬܦܼܠ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܕܒܥܠܕܪܐ ܕܡܲܩܪܒ ܥܡܝ.
126 But you, O brother, against whom this struggle takes place, when you see these terrifying forms, play the harp of the son of Jesse by saying: Let God arise and let all his enemies be scattered; let those who hate You flee before You; and: As wax melts in the presence of the heat of fire, let your enemies vanish before You; and: Lord, do not grant his desire to the impious and let his iniquitous project not succeed; and: God, deliver me; Lord, be my help; shame and dishonor to those who seek to destroy my soul; and: Lord, deliver me from the hand of the impious; and: Teach, Lord, my hands to fight; and: Make me rejoice in your salvation; and: confound those who hate me by your help; and: Lend your ear, Lord, and answer me, let the iniquity of him who has hatred return upon his head and let fear and terror fall upon the enemy who fights me.
ܘܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܕܡܡܲܚܐ ܐܢܬ ܠܗܐ ܒܗܠܝܢ ܓܐܪ̈ܐ ܘܠܘܠܝ̈ܬܐ ܕܦܬܓ̈ܡܘܗܝ ܕܒܪ ܐܝܫܝ ܆ ܚܬܘܡ ܠܪ̈ܓܫܝܟ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܒܐܬܐ ܕܨܠܝܒܐ ܘܒܪ ܫܥܬܗ ܡܬܛܠܩ ܡܢ ܩܕܡܝܟ.
And, after you have struck him with these arrows and spears of the verses [of the son of Jesse], mark all your senses with the sign of the cross, and, on the spot, (the demon) will disappear from before you.
ܗܢܘ ܕܝܘܐ ܕܐܡܼܪ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܩܶܕܝܫܐ ܐܘܓܪܝܣ ܕܡܒܪ̈ܩܢ ܥܝܢ̈ܘܗܝ ܐܝܟ ܢܘܪܐ ܇ ܘܡܚܲܘܐ ܕܡ̈ܘܬܐ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܚ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܦ̇ܪܚܝܢ ܒܐܐܪܿ. ܐܡܿܪ ܗܘܐ ܠܝ ܕܝܢ ܐܢܫ ܡܢ ܐܚ̈ܐ. ܕܒܚܕ ܡܢ ܝܘ̈ܡܝܢ ܟܕ ܪܡܐ ܗܘܝܬ ܒܩܪܒܐ ܥܡܗ ܕܕܝܘܐ ܗܢܐ ܠܝܼܛܐ: ܢܦܩܬ̇ ܠܒܪ ܡܢ ܩܠܝܬܝ ܒܠܠܝܐ ܕܡܿܛܝܠ ܗܘܝܬ. ܘܟܕ ܥܿܛܦܬ ܡܢ ܩܕܡ ܕܢܿܗܖ ܨܦܪܐ 'ܦܬܚܿܬ ܬܪ̈ܥܐ ܒܲܪܝܐ ܕܩܠܝܬ, ܘܟܕ ܥܿܠܬ ܚܿܙܐ ܐܢܼܐ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܬܢܝܼܢܐ ܟܕ ܡܬܝܼܚ ܒܟܠܗ̇ܿ ܩܠܝܬܐ. ܘܢܦܩ̈ܢ ܗܘܝ ܡܢ ܦܘܡܗ ܒܕܡܼܘܬ ܓܘ̈ܙܠܬܐ ܕܢܘܪܐ. ܘܥܝ̈ܢܘܗܝ ܡܒܪ̈ܩܢ ܗܘ̈ܝ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܠܡܦ̈ܝܼܕܐ ܕܢܘܪ̈ܐ. ܐܢܳܐ ܕܝܢ ܟܕ ܚܿܙܝܬܗ ܐܬܡܼܣܝ ܠܒܝ ܒܓܘܝ ܘܠܐ ܡܨܐ ܗܘܝܬ ܠܡܼܥܠ ܠܓܘܗ̇ ܕܩܠܝܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܠܡܦܼܩ ܠܒܪ ܡܢ ܕܚܼܠܬܐ ܕܗܿܝ ܚܙܬܐ ܡܫܼܲܟܪܬܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܗܟܢܐ ܥܫܢܬ ܕܚܠܬܐ ܥܠܝ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܦܠܐ ܐܝܕܝ ܡܨܐ ܗܘܝܬ ܠܡܿܪܚܫܘ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܥܿܒܕ ܐܬܐ ܕܨܠܝܒܐ. ܗܼܘ ܕܝܢ ܡܬܗܿܦܟ ܗܘܐ ܘܐܬܐ ܠܘܩܒܠܝ ܐܝܟ ܕܢܒܼܠܥܢܝ.
127 This is the demon of whom Saint Evagrius speaks: "His eyes flash like fire, and he appears in forms similar to serpents flying in the air." Indeed, one of the brothers said to me: "One day, while I was grappling in the struggle with this cursed demon, I went out of my cell during the night to take a walk. As I was returning before the dawn shone, I opened the outer door of my cell, and when I had entered, I saw something like a dragon stretched out in the entire cell; from its mouth came something like flames of fire, and its eyes sparkled like lamps of fire. Upon seeing it, my heart split within me, and I could neither enter the cell nor go outside, because of the fear that this hideous vision (gave me), for fear had so much power over me that I could no longer even move my hand to make the sign of the cross. As for him, he turned and came straight at me as if to devour me, but


[p. 402] {#po.45.p402}
ܐܢܳܐ ܕܝܢ ܟܕ ܪܡܼܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܗ̇ܝ ܕܚܠܼܬܐ ܥܠܝ ܘܠܐ ܝܿܕܥ ܗܘܝܬ ܡܿܢܐ ܐܥܒܕ. ܐܬܦܼܣܩ ܠܗ ܣܒܪܐ ܕܚ̈ܝܝ ܡܢ ܕܚܠܬܐ.
I, while fear was falling upon me and I did not know what to do, the hope of living was torn from me as a result of the fear.
ܐܠܗܐ ܕܝܢ ܛܒܐ ܘܡܪܚܡܢܐ ܟܕ ܚܙܢܝ ܕܒܐܝܢܐ ܐܓܘܢܐ ܕܕܚܠܼܬܐ ܪܡܐ ܗܘܝܬ ܡܢ ܫܠܝ ܐܪܡܝ ܒܬܪܥܝܬܝ ܥܘܗܕܢܐ ܕܙܡܝܪ̈ܬܗ ܕܛܘܒܢܐ ܕܘܝܕ ܘܥܡܗ ܕܫ̇ܪܝܬ ܒܡܙܡܘܪ̈ܐ, ܐܦ ܝܡܝܢܝ ܐܬܡܚܲܝܠܬ, ܘܥܒ̇ܕܬ ܠܩܘܒܠܗܼ ܐܬܿܐ ܕܨܠܝܒܐ ܘܟܕ ܚܙܐ ܡܪܘܕܐ ܐܬܐ ܕܨܠܝܒܐ: ܐܬܛܲܠܩ ܐ ܡܢ ܩܕܡܝ ܘܗܘܼܐ ܐܝܟ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ. ܟܕ ܕܝܢ ܚ̇ܙܝܬ ܐܢܐ ܡܕܡ ܕܗܘܼܐ ܆ ܢܿܦܠܬ ܥܠ ܐܦ̈ܝ ܝܘܡܐ ܟܠܗܼ ܟܕ ܡܿܣܩ ܐܢܿܐ ܩܘܒܠܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܥܠ ܡܕܡ ܕܣܼܥܪ ܠܘܬܝ ܆ ܒܪܡ ܕܝܢ ܪ̈ܝܼܚܐ ܕܣܪܝܘܬܗ ܐܓܪ ܒܩܠܝܬܝ ܬܠ̈ܬܐ ܝܘ̈ܡܝܢ. ܘܬܘܒ ܡܢ ܗ̇ܘ ܝܘܡܐ ܠܐ ܥܠܬ ܕܚܸܠܬܐ ܠܠܒܝ. ܐܠܐ ܗܟܘܬ ܐܬܓܲܢܒܪ ܪ̈ܥܝܢܝ ܒܚܼܲܝܠܬܢܘܬܐ ܘܒܠܒܝܒܘܬܐ: ܕܐܢ ܗܼܘܬܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܟܠܗܿ ܚ̈ܘܬܐ ܘܥܩܪ̈ܒܐ ܘܪ̈ܚܫܐ ܫܕܝܝ ܡܪܬܐ ܠܐ ܕܚܿܠ ܗܘܝܬ
But God, good and merciful, seeing into what a terrifying struggle I was thrown, suddenly made the memory of the psalms of the blessed David return to my thought; and at the very moment when I began to psalmody, my right hand was strengthened, and facing the (dragon) I made the sign of the cross. And when the rebel saw the sign of the cross, he disappeared before me and was as if annihilated. When I saw what had happened, fallen face to the ground, I remained the entire day offering thanksgivings to God for what He had done for me; but the odor of his stench remained in my cell for three days. From that day on, fear no longer entered my heart, but my spirit was so strengthened in energy and courage that, even if the entire earth were of serpents, scorpions, and venomous reptiles, I would not be afraid of them." Such are the words that this foreign brother said before me.
ܡܢܗܘܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܐܡܼܪ ܩܕܡܝ ܐܚܐ ܗܿܘ ܐܟܣܢܝܐ. ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܝܢ ܕܡܲܩܪܒܝܢ ܥܡܗ ܕܫܐܕܐ ܗܢܐ ܠܝܼܛܐ ܡܣܝܒܪܘܬܐ ܣܓܝܐܬܐ ܙܿܕܩ ܠܗܘܢ ܠܡܼܩܢܐ. ܐܝܟ ܕܠܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܚܠܼܬܐ ܢܫܒܼܩܘܢ ܩ̈ܠܝܬܗܘܢ ܘܢܦܼܩܘܢ ܡܢܗܝܢ ܂ ܐܠܐ ܚܡܣܢܘܢ ܘܢܬܒܼܘܢ ܠܓܘ ܡܢ ܬܪ̈ܥܐ ܟܕ ܫܳܗܪܺܝܢ ܘܢ̇ܿܩܫܝܢ ܥܠܐ ܬܪܥܗ ܕܡܪܢ ܒܠܠܝܐ ܘܒܐܝܡܡܐ ܘܡܪܢ ܡܲܥܒܪ ܠܗ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܩܠܝܠܐܝܬ. ܡܛܠ ܕܠܘ ܣܿܓܝ ܡܿܓܪ ܩܪܒܐ ܗܢܐ. ܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܗܢܐ ܩܪܒܐ ܡܬܚܲܝܠܐ ܡܢܿܬܐ ܕܚܡܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܐ. ܘܐܝܟ ܢܘܪ̈ܿܐ ܕܡܛܠܩܐ ܠܚܒܬܐ ܗܟܢܐ ܡܛܠܩܐ ܡܢ ܠܒܐ ܟܠ ܕܚܼܠܬܐ ܕܗܿܘܝܐ ܐܢ ܡܢ ܫ̈ܺܐܕܐ ܘܐܢ ܡܢ ܐܘ̈ܠܨܢܘܗܝ ܕܦܓܪܐ. ܘܗܟܘܬ ܪܘܐ ܠܒܐ ܒܚܘܒܗ ܕܡܪܢ ܘܡܬܡܠܐ ܚܕܘܬܐ ܕܐܡܝܢܐܝܬ ܡܠܬܗ ܕܦܘܠܘܣ ܩܿܥܐ ܘܐܡܿܪ. ܕܠܐ ܢܘܪ̈ܐ. ܘܠܐ ܣܝܦܐ. ܘܠܐ ܡ̈ܠܐܟܐ. ܘܠܐ ܒܢܝ̈ܢܫܐ. ܘܠܐ ܕܩܝ̈ܡܢ. ܘܠܐ ܕܥ̈ܬܝܕܢ
128 Those, therefore, who struggle against this cursed demon must acquire much endurance, so as not to abandon their cells under the effect of fear and not to go out of them. But let them be patient and sit inside, far from the door, watching and knocking at the door of Our Lord night and day; and Our Lord will make this struggle pass far from them quickly, since it does not last long. After this struggle, the irascible part of the soul is strengthened, and, just as fire destroys straw, so it makes all the fear that was in the heart disappear, whether it be because of demons or because of bodily trials. And the heart is so intoxicated with the love of Our Lord and filled with joy, that it continually proclaims and repeats the word of Paul: Neither fire nor sword, nor angels nor men, nor present nor future, nor even any other creature, if it existed, could separate me from the love of Our Lord Jesus Christ.
ܘܐܦܠܐ ܒܪܝܼܬܐ ܐܚܪܬܐ ܐܸܢ ܐܝܬ ܗ̇ܘܐ. ܡܲܨܝܐ ܗܘܬ ܕܬܦܼܪܫܢܝ ܡܢ ܚܘܒܗ ܕܡܪܢ ܝܫܘܥ ܡܫܝܚܐ. ܐܬܟܬܫܿܘ ܩܠܝܠ ܐܚ̈ܝ. ܘܩܘܡܘ ܠܘܩܒܠ ܢܣ̈ܝܘܢܐ ܕܥܿܪܨܝܢ ܥܠܝܟܘܢ ܒܡܘܬܼܒܐ ܫܲܠܝܐ ܕܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܕܬܫܼܬܘܘܢ ܠܗܕܐ ܦܪܗܝܣܝܐ ܇ ܕܬܩܥܘܢ ܒܚܕܘܬܐ ܕܠܒܟܘܢ ܕܚܢܢ ܙܩܝ̣ܦܝܢܢ ܠܥܠܡܐ ܕܢܣ̈ܝܘܢܐ ܇ ܘܢܣܝ̈ܘܢܘܗܝ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܙܩܝܼܦܝܢ ܠܢ ܡܛܠ ܪ̈ܚܡ̣ܬܗ ܕܡܪܢ ܝܫܘܥ ܡܫܝܚܐ. ܒܠܥܕ ܓܝܪ ܡܢ ܡܣܝܒܪܢܘܬܐ ܕܐܘܠܨܢܐ ܠܐ ܡܫܬܘܐ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ
129 Struggle a little, my brothers, and hold fast against the temptations that oppress you in the sojourn of solitude in the cell, in order to merit this confident freedom to proclaim in the joy of your heart that we are crucified by the world of temptations and that the temptations of the world are crucified by us because of the love of Our Lord Jesus Christ.


[p. 404] {#po.45.p404}
ܠܡܡܠܠܐ ܪܐܙܢܝܐ ܕܗ̇ܿܘܐ ܒܥܕܢܐ ܕܨܠܘܬܐ ܇ ܗ̇ܘ ܕܥ̇ܒܕ ܥܢܝܢܐ ܥܡ ܐܠܗܐ ܒܦܪܗܝܣܝܐ ܕܚܘܒܐ ܕܠܘܬܗ. ܥܗܕܘ ܐܘ ܐܚܝ ܠܡܠܬܗ ܕܡܚܝܢܢ ܡܪܢ ܝܫܘܥ ܕܐܡܪ. ܕܐܢܬܬܐ ܡܐ ܕܝܠܕܐ ܇ ܟܪܝܐ ܠܗ̇ ܕܡܛܐ ܝܘܡܐ ܕܡܘܠܕܗ̇. ܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܝܠܕܬ ܒܪܐ ܠܐ ܥܗܕܐ ܐܘܠܨܢܗ̇. ܡܛܠ ܚܕܘܬܐ ܕܐܬܝܠܕ ܒܪܢܫܐ ܒܥܠܡܐ. ܗܟܢܐ ܐܦ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܡܣܝܒܪܝܢ ܐܘܠܨܢ̈ܐ ܇ ܡܢ ܚܕܘܬܐ ܕܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܗ̇ܘ ܕܝܕܥܬܐ ܇ ܕܡܬܝܠܕ ܠܗܘܢ ܒܓܘ ܠܒܘ̈ܬܗܘܢ. ܡܢ ܡܣܝܒܪܢܘܬܐ ܕܐܘܠܨ̈ܢܐ ܛܥܝܢ ܠܟܠܗܝܢ ܥܩ̈ܬܐ ܕܥܒܪ ܥܠܝܗܘܢ ܒܙܒܢܐ ܕܬܟܬܘܫܐ ܕܥܡ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܒܝ̈ܫܬܐ. ܐܡܪܝܢ ܓܝܪ ܐܒܗ̈ܝܢ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܕܩܠܝܬܐ ܕܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܐܬܘܢܐ ܗܝ ܕܒܒܝܠ. ܐܝܟܐ ܕܬܠ̈ܬܐ ܛܠܝܐ ܚܙܘ ܠܒܪܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܀
For without the endurance of trials, the monk cannot merit the mystical dialogue that takes place at the moment of prayer, that which establishes an exchange with God thanks to the familiarity of the love that one has for Him. Remember, my brothers, the word of our life-giver, Our Lord Jesus, who said: The woman about to give birth is saddened because the day of her delivery has come; but when she has brought a son into the world, she no longer remembers her trial, in the joy that a man is born into the world. So it is also for all those who endure trials, because of the joy of this fruit of knowledge that is born to them in the midst of their hearts. Thanks to the endurance of trials, they forget all the anguishes that have passed over them, at the time of the struggle with the evil spirits. Our spiritual Fathers, indeed, say that the cell of the monk is that furnace of Babylon where the three children saw the Son of God.
ܚܙܘ ܐܚ̈ܝ. ܐܠܘ ܠܐ ܣܝܒܪܘ ܥܘܙܗ̇ ܕܫܠܗܒܝܬܐ ܇ ܘܦܣܩܘ ܥܠ ܢܦܫܗܘܢ ܡܘܬܐ ܕܒܝܬ ܚܢܢܝܐ ܠܐ ܡܫܬܘܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܗ̇ܝ ܚܙܬܐ ܫܒܝܚܬܐ ܇ ܘܠܗ̇ܝ ܥܢܢܐ ܕܛܐܠܐ ܕܕܥܟܬ ܠܫܠܗܒܝܬܐ ܕܢܘܪ̈ܐ. ܦܣܩܘ ܕܝܢ ܗܢܘܢ ܛܘܒ̈ܢܐ ܒܢܦܫ̈ܬܗܘܢ ܕܛܒ ܗܘ ܠܢ ܡܘܬܐ ܕܚ̈ܝܐ ܕܙܒܢܐ ܙܥܘܪܐ ܇ ܡܢ ܗ̇ܝ ܕܟ̇ܦܪܝܢܢ ܒܐܠܗܐ. ܐܝܘ ܠܚܘܒܐ ܚܡܝܡܐ ܕܐܬܠܝ̈ܛܐ ܢܨܚ̈ܝܐ ܆ ܕܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܥܟܗ̇ ܠܫܠܗܒܝܬܐ ܕܢܘܪܐ. ܗܢܘ ܬܗܪܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܡܠܠ ܕܢܘܪܐ ܠܢܘܪܐ ܕܥܟܬ. ܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܚܘܒܐ ܕܒܝܬ ܚܢܢܝܐ ܠܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܒܒܠܝܬܐ ܕܢܒܘܟܕܢܨܪ ܕܥܟܬ. ܗܟܢܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ. ܐܦ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܐܝܬ ܒܠܒܗܘܢ ܗ̇ܝ ܢܘܪܐ ܐܠܗܝܬܐ. ܕܡܐ ܕܥ̇ܐܠܝܢ ܠܐܬܘܢܐ ܕܩܠܝܬܐ ܇ ܕܒܝܕ ܡܣܝܒܪܢܘܬܐ ܕܐܘ̈ܠܨܢܐ ܚܡܝܡܘܬܐ ܕܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܚܘܒܗܘܢ ܡܕܥܟܐ
130 See, my brothers; if Ananias and his companions had not endured the ardor of the flame and chosen death for themselves, they would not have merited this glorious vision and this cloud of dew that extinguished the flame of fire. For these blessed ones decided within themselves: Better for us is death (after) a life of short duration, rather than to deny God. Long live the burning charity of the victorious athletes: how it extinguished the flame of fire! Here is the ineffable wonder: Fire extinguishes fire! The fire of love of Ananias and his companions extinguished the fire of the Babylon of Nebuchadnezzar! So it is also with all those who have this divine fire in their heart: when they enter the furnace of the cell, through patience in trials, the heat of the fire of their love extinguishes the heat of the natural passions.
ܠܗ̇ ܠܚܡܝܡܘܬܐ ܕܚ̈ܫܐ ܟܝ̈ܢܝܐ ܀ ܐܘ ܟܡܐ ܝܐܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܠܦ̈ܠܚܐ ܙܪ̈ܝܙܐ ܫܕܪܟ ܡܝܫܟ ܘܥܒܕܢܓܘ ܟܕ ܥܛܝܦܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܗ̇ܝ ܢܘܪܐ ܐܠܗܝܬܐ ܇ ܕܡܓܢܐ ܗܘܬ ܥܠܝܗܘܢ ܗ̇ܝ ܥܢܢܐ ܘܡܗܠܟܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܒܓܘ ܐܬܘܢܐ ܐܟ ܕܒܐܦ̈ܕܢܐ ܕܡܠܟܘܬܐ. ܘܡܣܩܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܩܘܒܠܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܕܐܒ̈ܗܝܗܘܢ ܕܗܘ ܪܡܝ ܛܐܠܐ ܕܒܣܝܡܘܬܗ ܥܠܝܗܘܢ ܘܫܘܙܒ ܐܢܘܢ ܡܢ ܢܘܪܐ ܥܙܝܙܬܐ ܕܚܣܡܐ ܕܒܒ̈ܠܝܐ. ܡܣܝܒܪܢܘܬܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܒܝܬ ܚܢܢܝܐ ܫܚܠܦܬ ܠܦܓܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܡܢ ܚܙܬܐ ܕܟܝܢܐ ܕܒܝܬ ܐܕܡ ܠܚܙܬܐ ܕܡܠܐܟ̈ܝ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܀
131 Oh! How beautiful it was for the valiant ascetics that were Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego, clothed in this divine fire, that this cloud should spread over them, that they should walk inside the furnace as in the palaces of the kingdom; and that they should send up thanksgivings to the God of their Fathers, because He had poured upon them the dew of his delights and had saved them from the burning fire of the jealousy of the Babylonians. Indeed, the patience of Ananias and his companions had caused their body to pass from the natural vision of the race of Adam to the vision of the angels of light.
ܗܟܢܐ ܐܦ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܡܣܝܒܪܝܢ ܐܘ̈ܠܨܢܐ ܒܐܬܘܢܐ ܕܩܠܝܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܩܛܥܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܡܢ ܐܘ̈ܠܨܢܐ ܕܣܡܟܝܢ ܥܠܝܗܘܢ ܡܫܬܚܠܦܝܢ ܦܓܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܡܢ ܦܓܪܢܘܬܐ ܠܪܘܚܢܘܬܐ ܘܢܗܪ̈ܝܢ ܦܪ̈ܨܘܦܝܗܘܢ ܒܢܘܗܪܐ
Likewise also, for all those who endure the tribulations of the furnace of the cell, and who, following the tribulations that fall upon them, do not let themselves go to acedia, their bodies will be transformed from the somatic stage to the pneumatic stage; and their faces will be illuminated with the holy light that rises in their hearts, like that which appeared to


[p. 406] {#po.45.p406}
ܩܕܝܫܐ ܕܕܢܚ ܒܠܒ̈ܘܬܗܘܢ. ܐܝܟ ܗ̇ܘ ܕܕܢܚ ܥܠ ܕܒܝܬ ܚܢܢܝܐ ܛܘ̈ܒܢܐ ܕܡܢ ܚܙܬܐ ܕܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܫܒܝܚܐ ܕܐܙܠܓ ܥܠܝܗܘܢ ܐܬܚܦܝ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܗ̇ ܕܢܘܪܐ ܒܒܠܝܬܐ ܇ ܡܢ ܩܕܡ ܥܝܢ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܘܠܐ ܚܙܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܗ̇ ܀
Ananias and his blessed companions, so that, by the vision of the light of glory that shone upon them, the light of the Babylonian fire was hidden from before their eyes, and they did not see it.

### Summary of the somatic stage
ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܗܟܝܠ ܕܥܡ̈ܠܐ ܘܡܘܬܒܐ ܕܒܩܠܝܬܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ. ܨܘܡܐ. ܢܙܝܪܘܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܟܠ ܡܐܟ̈ܠܢ. ܡܣܪܩܘܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܢܟ̈ܣܐ ܘܩܢܝ̈ܢܐ. ܫܗܪ̈ܐ ܕܦܓܪܐ. ܨܗܝܐ ܕܡ̈ܝܐ. ܗܠܝܢ ܥܡ̈ܠܐ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܕܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܦܓܪܢܘܬܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ. ܡܛܠ ܕܗ̇ܘܝܘ ܪܬܚܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܕܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܝܬܒܝܢ ܒܫܠܝܐ. ܒܗܢܐ ܪ̈ܬܚܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܕܗܕܐ ܡܫܘܚܬܐ. ܠܝܬ ܠܗܝܢ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܡܝܬܪ̈ܬܐ ܠܐ ܟܝܠܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܩܠܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܪ̈ܬܚܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܘܠܥܠ ܡܢ ܟܝܢܐ ܨܒܐ ܕܢܬܕܒܪ ܒܚ̈ܐܦܘܗܝ ܠܐ ܡܣܬܝܒܪ̈ܢܐ. ܒܗ̇ܝ ܕܡܢ ܢܘܪܪܐ ܕܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܟܝܢܝ̈ܐ ܗ̇ܘܐ ܗܢܐ ܪ̈ܬܚܐ. ܘܢܟܝܢܐ ܣܓܝܐܐ ܥܒܕ
132 Therefore, the beginning of the labors and of the sojourn in the cell are the following: fasting, abstinence from all food, the stripping away of goods and possessions, bodily vigils, thirst for water: all these labors belong to the somatic stage because it is the first fervor of those who establish themselves in solitude. In this first fervor of this stage, there is for these virtues neither gauge, nor measure, nor weight, because it is the first fervor, and, by its unbearable violences, it wants to conduct itself above nature, for this fervor comes from the fire of natural seeds, and it greatly harms the body, because, following a great leanness, all the veins of the body dry up and are altered.
ܒܦܓܪܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܡܢ ܢܚܘܒܘܬܐ ܣܓܝܬܐ ܡܬܝܒܫܝܢ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܫܪ̈ܝܢܘܗܝ ܕܦܓܪܐ ܘܩܢܝܢ ܢܟܝܢܐ ܀ ܐܦܠܐ ܓܝܪ ܫ̈ܐܕܐ ܡܩܪܒܝܢ ܥܡܗ ܕܗܝܐ ܪܬܚܐ ܒܫܘ̈ܪܝܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܝܕܥܝܢ ܕܠܝܬ ܠܗܘܢ ܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܠܘܬܗ ܕܐܚܐ ܇ ܟܡܐ ܕܗܢܐ ܪܬܚܐ ܕܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܟܝ̈ܢܝܐ ܫܓܪ ܠܗ̇ ܠܢܦܫܐ ܒܚܡܝܡܘܬܗ. ܒܠܚܘܕ ܕܝܘܐ ܕܫܘܒܚܐ ܣܪܝܩܐ ܗܡܣ ܒܗ. ܐܢܕܝܢ ܡܬܛܦܝܣ ܠܫܘܕܠܘܗܝ ܕܕܝܘܐ ܗܝܐ. ܗ̇ܝܕܝܢ ܡܫܬܗܐ ܠܗ ܗܢܐ ܪܬܚܐ ܕܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܟܝ̈ܢܝܐ ܡܢܗ̇ ܕܢܦܫܐ ܘܡܡܠܟܐ ܥܠܝܗ̇ ܡܐܝܢܘܬܐ ܘܒܘܠܒܠܐ ܕܚܘ̈ܫܒܐ. ܘܡܢ ܒܬܪ̈ ܗܕܐ ܡܫܬܠܛ ܒܗ ܒܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܕܝܘܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ. ܗ̇ܘ ܕܡܡܟ ܠܗ ܥܕܡܐ
133 The demons themselves do not fight, at the beginning, against this fervor, for they know that they have no hold on the brother as long as this fervor of these natural seeds burns the soul with its heat; only the demon of vainglory attacks him. If (the monk) obeys the seductions of this demon, then this fervor of the natural seeds cools in his soul, and boredom and the confusion of thoughts reign over it. Then the demon of fornication takes hold of the monk, which lowers him to the lower Sheol.
ܠܫܝܘܠ ܬܚܬܝܬܐ ܀ ܐܢܕܝܢ ܩܝܡܐ ܢܦܫܐ ܚܝܠܬܢܝܐܝܬ ܒܟܢܦܐ ܕܩܪܒܐ ܠܘܩܒܠ ܦܘܪ̈ܣܐ ܘܫܘ̈ܕܠܐ ܕܕܝܘܐ ܕܫܘܒܚܐ ܣܪܝܩܐ ܠܐ ܕܥܟ ܡܢܗ̇ ܗ̇ܘ ܪܬܚܐ ܕܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܟܝ̈ܢܝܐ. ܘܟܡܐ ܕܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܗ̇ܘ ܪ̈ܬܚܐ ܢܛܝܪܐ ܠܘܬܗ̇ ܕܢܦܫܐ ܠܐ ܡܨܝܢ ܚܕ ܡܢ ܚ̈ܫܐ ܡܫܥܒܕܝܢ ܠܗ̇ ܠܨܒܝܢܗܘܢ. ܡܛܠ ܕܒܗ ܒܗܢܐ ܪܬܚܐ ܡܬܡܛܝܐ ܠܕܟܝܘܬܐ ܇ ܗ̇ܝ ܕܡܢܗ̇ ܘܠܥܠ ܐܬܪܐ ܕܫܦܝܘܬܐ ܀
But if the soul resists energetically in the midst of the struggle against the stratagems and seductions of the demon of vainglory, the fervor of the natural seeds does not extinguish in it, and, as long as the fire of this fervor is preserved near the soul, none of the passions can subject it to their will, for it is by this fervor that (the exercitant) arrives at (the state of) purity, from and above which is the place of limpidity.
ܟܠܗ̇ ܓܝܪ ܝܕܥܬܐ ܕܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܦܓܪܢܘܬܐ ܡܬܓܫܡܬܐ ܗܝ ܡܛܠ ܕܠܥܠ ܡܢ ܪܘܟܒܐ ܠܝܬ ܠܗ̇ ܡܕܡ. ܘܟܠ ܐܡܬܝ ܕܒܥܝܐ ܕܬܚܘܪ ܒܐܠܗܐ. ܒܕܡ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܟܒܐ ܨܝܪܐ ܠܗ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܘܚܐܪܐ ܒܗ. ܘܐܢ ܓܠܝܢܐ ܐܘ ܚܙܘܐ ܡܕܡ ܗ̇ܘܐ ܠܘܬ ܗ̇ܘ ܡܢܘ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ.
134 For all the knowledge of the somatic stage is of the order of corporeality, because it has nothing outside of what is composed; and each time it seeks to look at God, it represents God and looks at Him under composed forms. And if the one who is at this stage has a revelation or a vision


[p. 408] {#po.45.p408}
ܒܗܕܐ ܡܫܘܚܬܐ: ܡܬܪ̈ܓܫܢܐܝܬ ܗܘ̇ܐ ܠܘܬܗ ܘܠܐ ܗܘܐ ܡܬܝܕܥܢܐܝܬ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܓܠܝ̈ܢܐ ܕܗܼܘܘ ܠܘܬ ܟܐ̈ܢܐ ܩܕܡ̈ܝܐ: ܢܘܚ ܐܡ̇ܿܪ ܐܢܼܐ ܘܐܒܪܗܡ ܘܐܝܣܚܩ ܘܝܥܩܘܒ ܇ ܘܫܪܟܐ ܕܟ̈ܐܢܝܐ ܕܗܼܘܘ ܥܕܡܐ ܠܢܡܘܣܐ. ܝܕܥܬܐ ܗܟܝܠ ܕܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܩܿܝܡܝܢ ܒܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܦܓܪܢܘܬܐ: ܝܕܥܬܐ ܟܝܢܝܬܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܐܝܬܝܗܿ. ܡܛܠ ܕܟܠܡܕܡ ܕܡܬܚܿܫܒ ܦܓܪܢܐ ܥܠ ܐܠܗܐ ܒܕܡܼܘܬܐ ܕܦܓܪܐ ܕܠܒܝܼܫ ܐܢܳܐ ܥܠܘܗܝ. ܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܦܓܪܢܘܬܐ: ܥܕܡܐ ܠܡܿܥܒܪܬܐ ܬܢܝܢܝܬܐ ܕܒܝܘܪ̈ܕܢܢ
whatsoever, it concerns for him the senses and not knowledge in spirit, like the revelations that were made to the first Just, I mean Noah, Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob and the other Just who existed before the Law. Therefore, the knowledge of those who are at the somatic stage is the second natural knowledge, because everything that (the man of the somatic degree) conceives in thought about God, he conceives under the bodily image with which he is clothed. Indeed, the somatic stage exists until the second crossing of the Jordan. And that is enough.
ܐܝܬܝܗܿ ܘܟܕܘ. ܐܟܙܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܒ̈ܢܝ ܐܝܣܪܐܝܠ ܡܐ ܕܥܼܒܪܘ ܝܘܪܕܢܢ: ܓܙܘܪ̈ܬܐ ܬܢܝܢܝܬܐ ܓܙܪ ܐܢܘܢ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܝܫܘܥܒܪܢܘܢ: ܘܗܿܝܕܝܢ ܥܠܼܘ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܡܘܠܟܢܐ: ܘܐܬܟ̇ܬܫܘ ܥܡ ܫ̈ܒܥܐ ܥܡ̈ܡܝܢ ܕܥܿܡܪܝܢ ܒܗ̇ܿ. ܗܟܢܼܐ ܐܦ ܚܢܢ ܐܢ ܠܐ ܓܿܙܪ̈ܝܢܢ ܠܒܢ ܡܢ ܒܝ̈ܫܬܐ ܓܙܘܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܕܐܝܬܝܗܿ ܕܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܢܘܬܐ: ܠܐ ܡܨܝܢܢ ܡܬܟܬܫ̇ܝܢܢ ܥܡ ܫܒܥܐ ܚܫ̈ܐ ܕܡܲܩܪܒܝܢ ܥܡܢ ܒܼܗܕܐ ܡܫܘܚܬܐ. ܕܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܗܿܠܝܢ. ܦܗܝܐ ܕܚ̈ܘܫܒܐ. ܚܣܡܐ. ܡܣܒܪܢܘܬܐ. ܠܐ ܝܕܥܬܐ. ܥܩܬܐ. ܠܐ ܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ. ܫܘܒܚܐ ܣܪܝܩܐ ܥܡ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܐ ܕܡܩܪ̈ܒܝܢ ܥܡ ܗܕܐ ܡܫܘܚܬܐ. ܐܟܙܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܗܿܝ ܩܕܡܝܬܐ ܟܠܗ ܦܘܠܚܢܗܿ ܦܓܪܢܐ ܗܘܿ: ܘܝܕܥܬܐ ܕܡܩܲܒܠ ܒܗܿ ܗܘܢܐ ܦܓܪܢܝܬܐ ܗܝ ܇ ܗܟܢܐ ܐܦ ܗܕܐ ܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ: ܟܠܗ̇ ܦܘܠܚܢܗ̇ ܢܦܫܢܐ ܗܼܘ. ܘܝܕܥܬܗ̇ ܟܠܗ̇ ܠܥܠ ܡܢ ܪ̈ܓܫܝ ܦܓܪܐ ܡܬܩܲܒܠܐ. ܐܦ ܩܪ̈ܒܝܗܿ ܟܠܗܘܢ
135 For, just as when the children of Israel had passed the Jordan, the blessed Joshua, son of Nun, gave them a second circumcision and they then entered the promised land and fought against the seven nations that inhabited it, likewise we too, if we do not circumcise our heart from (its) vices by a second circumcision which is the psychic stage, we will not be able to fight against the seven passions that make war on us at this stage and which are: the dissipation of thoughts, jealousy, presumption, ignorance, anguish, lack of faith, vainglory, as well as many other (passions) that make war on this stage. For, just as, in the first stage, all his ascetic exercise is bodily, and the knowledge that the intellect receives there is bodily, likewise also, in this second stage, all his ascetic exercise is psychic and all his knowledge is received beyond the senses of the body: all his struggles also are psychic. IV. Psychic stage. Second summit.
ܢܦܫ̈ܢܐ ܐܢܘܢ. ܢܐܡܪ ܕܝܢ ܗܫܐ ܐܘ ܚܒܝܒ ܐܝܟ ܫܘܘܕܝܐ ܕܠܘܬܟ. ܕܐܝܟܢܐ ܡܫ̇ܪܝܐ ܢܦܫܐ ܠܡܼܥܠ ܠܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܢܘܬܐ. ܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܫܶܠܡ ܐܩܡܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ: ܘܫ̇ܪܝ ܡܪܢ ܦܬܚܿ ܩܕܡ ܢܦܫܐ ܐܩܡܐ ܕܬܪܝܢ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ
136 Let us say then now, O beloved, according to my promise to you, how the soul begins to enter the psychic stage, after having reached the first summit, and that Our Lord has begun to open before the soul the second summit which belongs to the stage of the psychic degree.
ܕܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܢܘܬܐ. ܩܕܡܐܝܬ ܣܘܟܠܐ ܕܣܼܦܪ̈ܝ ܪܘܚܐ ܡܬܝܗܼܒ ܠܗ ܠܗܘܢܐ. ܘܐܝܟ ܫܡܼܫܐ ܕܡܲܢܗܖ ܠܟܠܗܘܢ ܟܝ̈ܢܐ ܕܒܪܝܬܐ ܩܕܡ ܦܓܪܐ ܇ ܗܟܢܐ
At the beginning, the intellection of the books of the Spirit is given to the intellect. And just as the sun illuminates all the natures of creation in front of the body,


[p. 410] {#po.45.p410}
ܡܬܢܲܗܪ̈ܝܢ ܩܕܡ ܗܘܢܐ: ܟܠܗܘܢ ܦܬܓ̈ܡܐ ܕܒܪܘܚܐ ܐܬܟܬܒܼܘ. ܘܡܐ ܕܐܕܪ̈ܟ ܣܘܟܠܗ ܕܦܬܓܡܐ: ܢܓ̇ܕ ܠܗ ܒܬܗܪ̈ܐ ܕܥܠܘܗܝ ܥܕܢܐ ܘܬܪ̈ܝܢ. ܐܘ̇ ܝܘܡܐ ܟܠܗ. ܘܗܿܝܕܝܢ ܫܿܒܩ ܠܗ ܠܡܥܼܒܪ ܠܐܚܪܢܐ. ܘܡܬܝ̇ܗܒܐ ܠܗܼ ܟܢܝܼܫܘܬ ܚܘܫ̈ܒܐ: ܕܣܼܛܪ ܡܢ ܚܕ ܚܘܫܒܐ ܕܚܳܐܪ ܒܡܕܒܪܢܘܬܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܠܝܬ ܠܗ ܐܚܪܢܝܐ.
so are illuminated in front of the intellect all the verses that have been written by the Spirit. And when he has understood the intellection of a verse, this leads (the intellect) into a wonderment concerning it for an hour or two, or a whole day. Then, (the monk) abandons it to pass to another (verse), and he is given the recollection of thoughts, so that apart from the single thought that fixes its gaze on the divine Economy, there is no other.
ܘܡܢ ܗܕܐ ܟܢܝܼܫܘܬ ܚܘܫ̈ܒܐ ܘܡܢ ܬܗܪ̈ܐ ܕܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܗܘ̇ܐ ܠܗ ܬܪ̈ܥܘܬܐ ܕܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܒܠܠܝܐ ܘܒܐܝܡܡܐ. ܠܘ ܓܝܪ ܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܕܥܨܝܢܐ ܐܘ̇ ܫܓܝܫܬܐ ܐܝܬܝ̈ܗܝܢ. ܐܝܟ ܗܵܢܺܝܢ ܕܗܿܘ ܪ̈ܬܚܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܕܐܡܼܪܢܢ ܥܠܝܗܝܢ ܡܢ ܠܥܠ. ܐܠܐ ܗܿܠܝܢ ܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܡܫ̈ܝܢܬܐ ܐܝܬܝ̈ܗܝܢ: ܘܠܘ ܕܨܒܝܢܗ ܕܒܪܢܫܐ ܐܝܬܝ̈ܗܝܢ. ܐܠܐ ܡܢ ܬܗܖ̈ܐ ܕܣܘ̈ܟܠܐ ܐܬܝܢ ܆ ܘܡܚ̈ܠܝܢ ܠܚܟܼܗ ܕܡܕܥܐ. ܗܠܝܢ ܐܝܬܝ̈ܗܝܢ ܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܕܩܿܪܝܢ ܠܗܝܢ ܐܒ̈ܗܝܢ ܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܡܘܠܟܢܐ ܇ ܕܡܐ ܕܥܠܼܘ ܐܢܫ ܠܬܡܢ ܠܐ ܕܚܿܠ ܡܢ ܩܪ̈ܒܐ ܕܡܲܩܪܒܝܢ ܥܡܗ. ܡܛܠ ܕܗܿܢܘ ܪ̈ܬܚܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܕܐܡܼܪ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܐܡܘܢܝܣ: ܗܿܘ ܕܠܘ ܠܣ̈ܓܝܐܐ ܡܢ ܝܚ̈ܝܕܝܐ ܡܬܝܼܗܒ. ܐܠܐ ܠܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܐܬܕܟܼܝܘ ܡܢ ܚܫ̈ܐ ܕܦܓܪܐ ܘܕܢܦܫܐ. ܘܥܡ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܬܝܼܗܒ ܠܗ ܪܬܚܐ ܕܣܿܝܡ ܒܘܪ̈ܟܐ. ܕܠܠܝܐ ܘܕܝܡܡܐ ܩܕܡ ܨܠܝܒܐ ܒܪܝܟ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ: ܒܬܟܫܦܬܐ ܕܩܕܡ ܐܠܗܐ.
And, from this recollection of thoughts, as well as from the wonderment of intellections, there is made for him an appeasement (accompanied) by tears night and day. And these are no longer tears of constraint or of trouble, like those of the first fervor of which we spoke above, but they are tears of peace, and they come not from the will of man, but from the wonderment (caused) by the intellections, and they give a sweet flavor to the palate of the spirit. These are the tears called by our Fathers "promised land." For once one has entered into it, one no longer fears the struggles that are engaged against oneself, for this is the second fervor of which Saint Ammonios speaks, which is not granted to many monks, but to those who have been purified of the passions of the body and of the soul; and, with these—blessed be the monk—is given the fervor of (making) genuflections, night and day, before the cross in a supplication before God. What contemplations?
ܘܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܬܠܬ ܕܐܡܿܪܬܼܿ ܡܬܡܲܪܩܐ ܥܝܢܐ ܢܗܼܝܪܬܐ ܕܗܘܢܐ ܡܢ ܟܠܗܿ ܬܛܠܘܫܬܐ ܕܒܣܪܐ ܘܕܪ̈ܘܚܐ. ܘܚܿܐܪ ܒܩܢܘܡܗ ܘܚܿܙܐ ܗܼܘ ܠܝܬܝܗ. ܒܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܫܒ̣ܝܚܐ ܕܗ̇ܘ ܕܥܿܡܪ ܒܗ. ܘܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܚܙܬܐ ܕܩܢܘܡܗ ܕܢܚܳܐ ܒܗ ܒܗܘܢܐ ܬܐܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܓܘ̈ܫܡܐ ܘܕܠܐ ܓܫܘܡ. ܘܒܗܿܝ ܩܕܡܝܬܐ ܡܬܝܼܠܕ ܒܗܿ ܣܘܟ̇ܠܐ ܕܥܠ ܬܐܘܪܝܐ ܕܕܝܢܐ ܘܕܒܛܝܼܠܘܬܐ. ܘܡܐ ܕܐܫܬܘܼܝ ܗܘܢܐ ܠܣܘܟܠܐ ܕܗܿܠܝܢ ܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܬܐܘܪ̈ܝܬܐ. ܗܿܝܕܝܢ ܪ̈ܘ̇ܐ ܒܪ ܐܢܫܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܚܡܪܐ ܒܪܚܡܬܐ ܕܒܢܝ̈ܢܫܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܒܚܙܬܐ ܕܬܐܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܗ̇ܝ ܩܕܡܝܬܐ ܡܬܚܙܝܢ ܠܗ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܒܢܝ̈ܢܫܐ. ܐܢ ܕܚ̈ܛܝܐ ܘܐܢ ܕܙܕܝ̈ܩܐ. ܘܟܠܡܕܡ ܕܣܿܥܪܝܢ ܚܕ ܚܕ
137 And by these three (gifts) that I have cited, the illuminated eye of the intellect is washed of every carnal and spiritual stain. It contemplates itself and sees its being in the glorious light of Him who dwells in it. And after the sight of itself appears in the intellect the contemplation of the corporeal and the incorporeal. And in this first (contemplation) is born an intellection concerning the contemplation of the Judgment and of Providence. And when the intellect has been judged worthy of the intellection of these two contemplations, then man is intoxicated, as with wine, with love for men. For it is in the vision of this first contemplation that all the actions of men appear to him, whether sinners or just, as well as everything that each of them accomplishes.
ܡܢܗܘܢ. ܘܐܠܘ ܬܐܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܗܕܐ ܕܕܝܢܐ ܘܕܒܛܝܼܠܘܬܐ ܠܐ ܕܢܿܚܐ ܗܘܬ
But if this contemplation of Judgment and Providence did not appear


[p. 412] {#po.45.p412}
ܒܡܕܥܗ ܕܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܘܒܝܕ ܣܘܟܿܠܐ ܕܝܠܗܝܢ ܡܬܢܬܼܦ ܗܘܐ ܠܪܚܡܬܐ ܕܒܢܝ̈ܢܫܐ. ܡܢ ܡܚ̈ܝܠܬܐ ܕܚ̇ܙܐ ܗܘܐ ܕܡܣܬܥܪ̈ܢ ܒܗܕܐ ܒܪܝܬܐ ܒܚܕ ܥܕܢܐ ܡܿ̇ܘܒܕ ܗܘܐ ܠܩܢܘܡܗ. ܒܗ̇ܝ ܕܡܬܬܼܩܠ ܗܘܐ ܒܥܒ̈ܕܝܗܘܢ. ܘܡܬܡܼܠܹܐ ܗܘܐ ܛܢܼܢܐ ܠܘܩܒܠܗܘܢ ܘܚܡܬܼܐ ܘܪ̈ܘܓܙܐ ܘܒܝܕ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܿܘܒܕ ܗܘܐ ܗܼܘ ܠܗ. ܐܠܐ ܕܐܢ ܡܪܢ ܒܡܪܚܡܢܘܬܗ ܗܟܢܐ ܦܪܢܣ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ. ܕܥܡ ܚܙܬܐ ܒܗܕܐ ܬܐܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܝܗܿܒ ܐܦ ܣܘܟܠܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ. ܕܡܐ ܕܡܫܪܐ ܗܘܢܐ ܘܚܿܐܪ ܒܡܚ̈ܝܠܬܐ ܕܒܢܝ̈ܢܫܐ ܘܨܿܒܐ ܕܢܬܬܙܝܥ ܒܛܢܼܢܐ ܠܘܩܒܠܗܘܢ. ܢܦ̇ܠܐ ܒܠܒܗ ܗܿܝ ܢܘܪܐ ܕܪ̈ܚܡܼܬܐ ܕܒܢܝ̈ܢܫܐ. ܘܐܝܟ ܕܒܥܘܡܩܐ ܡܛܲܒܥܐ ܠܗ ܠܗܘܢܐ ܚܙܬܐ ܘܣܘܟ̇ܠܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܬܐܘܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܕܕܝܢܐ ܘܕܒܛܝܠܘܬܐ.
in the mind of the monk, and if by their intellection he were not drawn toward love for men, because of the weaknesses he would see being accomplished in this creature, in a single hour he would destroy himself, because he would be scandalized by their acts. || He would (also) be filled with jealousy against them, as well as with fury and anger; and for all these (causes) he would destroy himself, unless Our Lord in His mercy had disposed these actions themselves in such a way that, in this contemplation with vision, He also gives him the intellection of other things, so that, when the intellect begins to look at the weaknesses of men and wants to excite itself with zeal against them, this fire of charity for men falls into his heart, and the sight and intellection of these contemplations of Judgment and Providence plunge the intellect as if into an abyss.
